P4 (branch `phase4-codim2`, commits 62f50b9 to 9d51e09, Rust only: no MATLAB API change, no C ABI change, no new dependency).

- 62f50b9 adds the `cusp.ode`, `bt.ode` and `bautin.ode` test models. 8694b63 adds `Point.codim2` and the `Problem` hooks `codim2_labels`, `codim2_tests` and `codim2_label`, and `locate.rs` searches one index space for the old and the codim-2 tests.
- 825d59d: `continue_lp_curve` with CP detection and the cusp coefficient c. bfdb0c0: BT and ZH detection on the LP curve and the BT coefficients (a, b). 9d51e09: `continue_hopf_curve` with GH, BT, ZH and HH detection and l2.
- Acceptance (SPEC section 9): Test 7 (cusp, CP at the origin), Test 8 (BT, LP and H curves meet at the origin, s = +1 and s = -1) and Test 9 (Bautin, GH at the origin, l2 = 4s) pass in `crates/pc-engine/tests/codim2.rs`, 10 tests. Two extra checks pass: a warped Bautin model (`bautin_warped.ode`) gives the same l2 to 1e-6, and the Brusselator Hopf curve b = 1 + a^2 matches to 1e-9.
- Checks at 9d51e09: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (no failures), `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok) and `cargo build --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-ffi` all pass. `buildtool` did not run, since no MATLAB-side file or `pc-ffi` code changed.
- SPEC section 6 now has the curve equations, the Hopf bordered function and its gradient, the codim-2 tests and the normal form formulas.
- Review fixes after the branch read-through: `locate_one` rejects a codim-2 sign change through a pole, a codim-2 zero that its problem drops gives no warning, the warning names the codim-2 test, ZH and HH are NaN for kappa <= 0, and LP and H curve runs detect specials on their first step. Each has a unit test.

P8a (package v0.3, win64 only, branch `phase8a-package`, commits 18b3f77 to bbc5fb0, plus the status commits; merged into `main` on 2026-10-30). `MinimumMatlabRelease` is R2026b, the version is 0.3.0, and the toolbox UUID is fixed in `buildfile.m`.

- `Evaluator::Aot` in `pc-engine` loads the library with `libloading`, which is now a normal dependency. `model_compile` takes the keys `emit_c`, `evaluator` ("jit", "fallback", "aot" or "none") and `aot_lib`. `"evaluator":"none"` (2026-11-10) writes the C file and builds no evaluator, so `internal.aotBuild` no longer compiles a JIT that an AOT success then drops. The C ABI is unchanged: 17 members, 128 bytes. `internal.aotBuild` runs `mex` with floating-point contraction off. On the MATLAB side, `phasecont.config("Codegen")`, `Model(file, Evaluator="aot")` and `Model.AotInfo` select and report AOT.
- `pc-cli licenses` writes `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` with one section per crate in the closure. The real run on 2026-11-10 (CI command, three targets, `pc-ffi`) gave 135 sections, 24 of them from `licenses/overrides`. The command fails when a crate has no license file and no override.
- `toolbox/examples` has a FitzHugh-Nagumo example and `ExampleTest`. It finds two Hopf points, at I = 1.4187187 and I = 0.33128134, with omega = 0.27550681. An independent `fzero` on the Jacobian trace confirms both.
- `scripts/packageToolbox.m`, `PackageTest` (31 tests) and the `package` buildtool task build `build/PhaseCont-0.3.0.mltbx`, about 4.87 MB. The script checks the stage path before it deletes anything, and it refuses a repo path that contains `&` or `;`.
- `scripts/cleanInstallCheck.m`, `scripts/buildfileValue.m`, `BuildfileValueTest` and the `package-win` CI job. The check ran in the real Windows MATLAB R2026b Add-Ons folder. It installed 0.3.0, found `phasecont.Model` in the add-on folder, ran the example and uninstalled the toolbox. The run used the same Windows MATLAB install that built the package, not a second machine, and it does not prove that a brand new MATLAB session finds the toolbox on its path. Smart App Control did not block the freshly built `pckern.mexw64`.
- Checks at the tip (bbc5fb0): `cargo test` gave 894 passed and 0 failed. `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool cargo mex test package"` gave 150 tests, 149 passed, 0 failed and 1 incomplete (`BuildTest/wslRootElsewhereUsesToolboxBin`, skipped by an assumption, as before).
- Update of 2026-11-08: both have run. The `pckern-win` job's step "Collect the third-party license texts of the three platforms" passed in `ci` run 42520388279 (b39c3a6). The `package-win` job no longer exists. `release.yml` (the `licenses`, `mex`, `package` and `install` jobs) passed as a dry run (`workflow_dispatch`, 41912379730, 2026-11-04) and on the `v1.0.0` tag push of the release now named 1.0.0-preview (41925211997).

- 409 tests pass in `cargo test`: 18 unit tests and 16 + 3 integration tests in `pc-cli`; 307 unit tests, 13 corpus, 5 invalid-file, 9 limit and 38 round-trip tests in `pc-ode`. `PROPTEST_CASES=10000 cargo test -p pc-ode --release round_trip` passes with 11 round-trip tests.

The probes back the grammar rulings in SPEC section 2 and `language.md`.

P2 (accepted on 2026-10-28; the numbers below were measured that day at `ab8d987`):

- `crates/pc-ir` has these modules: `math` (the shim table over `libm`, `polygamma`, tables), `dag` (hash-consed DAG with folding), `lower`, `entry`, `error`, `diff` (first-order symbolic derivatives), `multi` (multilinear forms up to order 5), `complex` (real and imaginary split of complex directions), `eval` (straight-line evaluator and running error bound), `interp` (the generic interpreter, nested dual numbers and `Fallback`) and `check` (tolerance and finite-difference checks). `crates/pc-jit` has `flags`, `lower`, `module` (the `JitEntries` guard), `compiled` (lazy, length-checked `CompiledModel::call`) and `check`. `crates/pc-codegen` has the C emitter `c`.
- New dependencies, as approved in the plan: `libm` 0.2.16 and `proptest` 1.12.0 (dev) in `pc-ir`, Cranelift 0.137.0 (five crates) in `pc-jit`, `libloading` 0.9.0 (dev) in `pc-codegen`.
- `pc-cli ir FILE`, `pc-cli check-derivs FILE` and `pc-cli emit-c FILE` work.
- 603 tests pass in `cargo test`: 372 in `pc-ode`, 135 in `pc-ir`, 64 in `pc-cli`, 25 in `pc-jit` and 7 in `pc-codegen`. `PROPTEST_CASES=10000 cargo test -p pc-ir --release` passes 135 tests in about 1 s, and `PROPTEST_CASES=1000000` runs `diff_prop.rs` in about 90 s. `diff_prop.rs` now reads `PROPTEST_CASES` (default 300); before that fix the count was fixed at 300, so earlier 10000-case runs on this branch ran only 300 cases.
- `for f in crates/pc-ode/tests/corpus/*.ode; do target/debug/pc-cli check-derivs "$f"; done`: 19 models pass every check and 2 are refused with their `IrError` (`stochastic.ode`: `ir:simulation-only`; `unsupported.ode`: `ir:delay`). None fails.
- `crates/pc-codegen/tests/corpus.rs` runs the SPEC section 3 and 4 checks on 28 models (the 19 accepted corpus models and the 9 models in `crates/pc-ir/tests/models`) at seed 1 and 6 points per entry kind, in about 16 s in a debug build. The JIT matches the DAG evaluator bit for bit everywhere (a NaN matches any NaN), and the AOT C code matches the JIT at 0 ulp on all 28 models. The AOT test compiles with `gcc (GCC) 16.2.1` and `-O2 -fPIC -shared -ffp-contract=off -std=c99 -Wall -Wextra -Werror` (recorded in `references/aot/compiler.txt`).
- Section 3 tolerance. Four DAG-vs-Fallback rows of the Hodgkin-Huxley model failed the first rule (worst ratio 40.7, in `Dir[P,P,Full,P]`). A double-double reference, exact rational models and a permutation check showed cancellation, not a bug: in v' the terms of about 1e3 cancel, so at the failing points the result sits up to 6.8e4 times below the term size and 1e-13 of the output scale falls under the rounding noise. The owner chose a fifth pass condition, |a-b| <= 8 eps E, where E is Wilkinson's running error bound of the DAG evaluation (Higham 2002, section 3.3). SPEC section 3 defines it. On 40000 points per entry kind it gives no failure, with a worst |a-b|/(eps E) of 0.66. `crates/pc-ir/tests/hh_cancellation.rs` keeps the four vectors and checks that a sign flip and relative changes of 1e-8 and 1e-2 still fail.
- `cargo build --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-jit -p pc-cli` and `RUSTDOCFLAGS="-D warnings" cargo doc -p pc-ir -p pc-jit -p pc-codegen -p pc-ode -p pc-cli --no-deps` pass. The four checks pass.
- macOS arm64 (SPEC section 4, measured on 2026-10-28 on GitHub's `macos-latest` runner, macOS 26.6.2, in the private repo through `.github/workflows/macos-jit.yml`):
  - The four checks pass there, and `cargo test` passes all 603 tests (run 40644502330 at `e65eb3a`), the `pc-jit` tests and the AOT test included. The AOT test compiles with Apple clang 21.0.0 (also called as `gcc`) and the same flags, and matches the JIT at 0 ulp.
  - `crates/pc-jit/examples/jit_probe.rs` compiles a Hopf model with the JIT and runs `F` and `JacU`. The results equal `Fallback` bit for bit, unsigned and under the hardened runtime with each JIT entitlement. The runner has SIP disabled and does not enforce the hardened runtime (`DYLD_PRINT_LIBRARIES` still prints with `--options runtime`), so that matrix only shows the JIT runs.
  - MATLAB R2026b on that runner is not hardened: `bin/maca64/MATLAB` and `matlab_helper` have `flags=0x0(none)` and no entitlements. `cranelift-jit`'s default memory provider (read-write, then executable, without `MAP_JIT`) therefore works inside MATLAB, and no `MAP_JIT` provider is needed. A guard step fails the job if a later release adopts the hardened runtime without `allow-unsigned-executable-memory` or `disable-executable-page-protection`.
  - Not tested: the JIT inside the MATLAB process on macOS. That needs a MATLAB batch licensing token in CI (deferred from P7a, see Open questions).
- The review of the whole branch (2026-10-28) found four minor defects and some stale text. One fix round (`837413c` to `ab8d987`) fixed them, each code fix with a test that fails without it:
  - The x-derivative of `besselj` and `bessely` now shifts the truncated order: for an order between -1 and 1 it used to give the wrong value.
  - An entry request that hits the node cap now restores the DAG, so later requests on the same model still work.
  - The corpus check holds `F` and `Aux` to 4 ulp between the JIT and `Fallback`, as SPEC section 4 says.
  - `diff_prop.rs` compares `F` between the DAG and `Fallback` and uses the shared tolerance, and runs of 100000 cases or more no longer stop on rejected inputs.
  - The docs now state how folding changes the sign of a zero, and the probe README states the rulings on division, `mod` and `if`.
- Known differences, all documented or by ruling:
  - A literal Bessel order that is NaN, infinite or so large that `trunc(a) - 1` and `trunc(a) + 1` round to the same value gives an x-derivative of 0 in the DAG and the JIT, and NaN in `Fallback`. The two shifted calls become one node and `x - x` folds to 0, as `language.md` documents for folding. The function value is NaN in every evaluator.
  - `polygamma(n, -inf)` is NaN. Every evaluator, the AOT code included, calls the same shim, so they agree.
  - A table formula that reads parameters, states or fixed quantities is tabulated from their default and initial values when the model is lowered (`language.md`, statement details). Changing them needs a new lowering; P3 sessions must do that.
  - At a singular point, where a partial is infinite or NaN and the tangent is 0 at run time, the DAG and the JIT can give NaN while `Fallback` gives a finite value. The interpreter skips a zero tangent at run time and the DAG folds only constant zeros. Both are right at regular points, and the checks use regular points.
  - In JIT, DAG and AOT comparisons a NaN matches any NaN, since IEEE 754 leaves the sign and payload of a NaN result unspecified. DAG constants and table data are still compared bit for bit.
- Parked minor findings, none affecting results: `pc-jit` and `pc-codegen` keep their own copies of `children` and `live_set`; `jit_rows` repeats `compare_kind` across the crate boundary; the running error bound of a table ignores the roundings inside `math::table` (all evaluators share it); user-function re-lowering can grow exponentially with nesting; sanitized C entry names can collide (the compile then fails); the module guard's error-path counter covers one injection point; `math::besselj` and `math::bessely` take time linear in the order (about 0.5 s at order 1e8 and 10 s near 2^31 per call), which P3 bounds by capping the order at 10000 (see P3 below).

P3 (the numbers below were measured on 2026-10-28 at `c6ed751` plus the SPEC and status commit):

- `crates/pc-linalg` has `dense` (LU solve with a singularity check, transpose solve, determinant), `eig` (eigenvalues of a real matrix as sorted `(re, im)` pairs), `null` (null vectors of A and A^T, kernel of an n x (n+1) matrix) and `block` (the real 2n x 2n form of complex shifted solves). No faer type leaves the crate.
- `crates/pc-engine` has `model` (the `ModelEval` trait for `CompiledModel` and `Fallback`), `error`, `problem`, `ep` (the EP problem and the equilibrium solve), `palc` (tangent, predictor, corrector and step control), `tests_fn` (ψ_LP, ψ_BP, ψ_H and the neutral saddle check), `locate` (secant with a bisection safeguard along the step), `refine` (the LP, BP and Hopf defining systems), `normal` (a and l1), `switch` (the algebraic branching equation), `branch`, `run` (the worker thread with poll, cancel and stop) and `panic` (the hook that `pc-ffi` reuses in P7a).
- `pc-cli cont FILE PROBLEM.json [--fallback]` runs a continuation and prints the branches as JSON, then one line per special point. On `crates/pc-engine/tests/models/lorenz.ode` with `crates/pc-engine/tests/problems/lorenz.json` it prints BP at r = 1 and NSA at r = 4.644444 on the origin branch, and H at r = 24.73684 with l1 = 2.655190e-4 and ω = 9.624530 on the switched branch. The NSA is real: there the eigenvalue 8/3 pairs with -b, which gives r = 1 + 328/90.
- New dependency, as approved in the plan: `faer` 0.24.4 in `pc-linalg`, without default features and without rayon. `cargo deny check` passes with one ignore in `deny.toml`, RUSTSEC-2024-0436, an unmaintained build-time proc-macro under faer with no upgrade.
- `besselj` and `bessely` with an order of magnitude above 10000 give NaN in every evaluator, as `language.md` states. This bounds the time of one evaluation, which a cancel cannot interrupt.
- The fold coefficient a is computed with q oriented so that p^T F_α > 0, so its sign follows the normal form of Kuznetsov's Theorem 3.1; SPEC section 6 states it and the stored normalization string is `|q|=1, p'q=1, p'F_alpha>0`.
- ψ_H is the sign of the plain product times its smallest scaled factor (SPEC section 5), so it does not underflow on models with many slow modes (`71a453c`). The zero search along a step uses the plain product divided by its modulus at the step start, so a tiny, nearly constant factor does not make the search stop on the wrong pair (`ebb50d2`).
- The review of the whole branch (2026-10-28) found eight minor defects. One fix round (`2a4f504` to `69ff893`) fixed them together with the minor items parked during the task reviews. Among them: the bound landing now uses `tol_refine` in both of its checks, options with an infinite step size are refused, a refined point that moved more than twice the step from its secant estimate is not used, a BP whose state Jacobian does not have the expected rank is refused for n = 1 too, switch names ignore case, and `EngineError::Panicked`, which nothing built, is gone.
- 754 tests pass in `cargo test`: 372 in `pc-ode`, 139 in `pc-ir`, 26 in `pc-jit`, 8 in `pc-codegen`, 16 in `pc-linalg`, 113 in `pc-engine` and 80 in `pc-cli`.
- `cargo test -p pc-engine --release --test acceptance -- --nocapture` runs SPEC section 9 tests 1, 2, 5 and 6 with the JIT and with `Fallback`, and both give the same numbers:
  - Test 1: one LP, at x = 0 and α = 1.7e-41, with a = 1 exactly.
  - Test 2: H at α = 0 with ω = 1, and l1 = 2c for c = 1 and c = -1 with an error of 4.4e-16. No NSA.
  - Test 5: BP at r = 1 exactly. On the switched branch x = y exactly and z = x²/b within 7.1e-15, H at r = 470/19 within 3.6e-15, and l1 = 2.655189725836717e-4 > 0.
  - Test 6: H at b = 1 + a² exactly for a = 1 and a = 2, with ω = a within 6.7e-16.
- `cargo build --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-engine` passes. The four checks pass.
- Known limit: on a long step across a branch crossing, the trial corrector of the zero search can jump to the other branch, which puts the BP at the wrong place (α = 0.04 instead of 0 in one case). `hmax` bounds it; the test models use 0.1 or 0.5.

P4b (branch `phase4b-open-items`, cut from 734c7a8, merged into `main` on 2026-10-31; closes the open items of P4):

- Hopf detection has no dimension limit. `MAX_HOPF_DIM` and its warning are gone. `hopf_is_detected_above_100_variables` runs a 101-variable model (`hopf101.ode`, a planar Hopf normal form plus 99 stable variables) on both evaluators and finds H at al = 0 to 1e-8 with no warning. Release-mode cost of one `psi_h` call against one dense eigenvalue call on the same size: n = 101, 78 us against 1.4 ms; n = 300, 0.83 ms against 15 ms; n = 1000, 8.3 ms against 0.42 s.
- Codim-2 points are polished by Newton's method along the curve (`refine_zero` in `locate.rs`) and carry `refined = true`. CP (Test 7), BT on both curves (Test 8) and GH (Test 9) now sit within 1e-10 of the exact positions, up from 1e-8. The slope of the test is a forward difference along the tangent, so each step costs one extra test evaluation. A point that does not converge keeps its Illinois position with `refined = false`. Unit tests in `locate.rs` cover a capped search that Newton finishes and a test that is undefined next to the point.
- ZH normal form coefficients (c9e0126): b, c, d and e from Kuznetsov (1999) section 8, at ZH points on both the LP curve and the H curve. The sign of q0 makes b > 0, a convention of this project like a > 0 at BT. Tests use our own models with the G values read off by hand, plus a coordinate change by an orthogonal map (the |q0| = 1 normalization allows no other). The warp test asserts b, c, Re d and e.
- HH cubic coefficients (ff890b3): P11, P12, P21, P22 and omega2 from section 9, with the pairs ordered w1 > w2. The warp test asserts all four real parts. The fifth-order coefficients S1 and S2 stay unimplemented: the open copy of the paper has misprints in those formulas and no second source confirms them (rule 9).
- LP and H curves in the C ABI and MATLAB (fcd5db9, cb37405, 10597fa). The engine has a `RunKind`, and `pc-ffi` and `pc-cli` take problem `type` `LP` or `H` with two free names. The ABI struct is unchanged (17 members, 128 bytes, `abi_version` 1); the JSON gained `kind`, `codim2_labels`, `coefficient2` and `coefficients`, and curve rows have their own layout (SPEC section 10). MATLAB has `continuation(..., Type="LP"|"H", Free=[a1 a2])`, `Branch.Type`, `Alpha` (N-by-2), `Codim2`, `Codim2Labels` and `Point.Data.values` (SPEC section 11). `kappa` of the Hopf curve is not exposed, and `Stable` is false on a curve.
- Checks at cb37405: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (939 tests, no failures), `cargo deny check` and `cargo build --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-ffi` pass. Commit 10597fa changed no Rust.
- MATLAB suite at 10597fa: 165 tests, 159 passed, 5 failed and 4 incomplete. All 5 failures are `AotTest`. Windows Smart App Control blocks freshly built unsigned MEX and DLL files here, so `buildtool cargo mex test` does not run green on this machine. The run used `buildtool cargo`, then the previously staged `pckern.mexw64` gateway (24064 bytes) with the fresh `pckern.dll`, then `buildtool test`. The 4 incomplete are the AOT tests and `BuildTest/wslRootElsewhereUsesToolboxBin` (an assumption). `CurveTest` (11 tests) and the 4 new `Rows2BranchTest` tests pass.

P7a (complete on branch `phase7a-matlab-api`, cut from 390bfb1; merged into `main` on 2026-10-29, 714aac9):

- `pc-ffi` C ABI v1: 17 members in 128 bytes, with `model_rhs_t` appended at offset 120. It has the handle registry, the panic barrier, model threads and runs, and a hand-written `pckern.h` with a layout test.
- `pc-cli dll-imports`, the build tasks `cargo` and `mex` with `scripts/build-pckern`, and the MEX gateway in `mex/pckern_gateway.c`.
- MATLAB API: `phasecont.Model`, `Point`, `equilibrium`, `continuation` (blocking, with a live plot), and `Branch` with `plot` and `points`.
- A model that uses `t` compiles and evaluates with `m.rhs(x, p, t)`. `t` has no default and raises `phasecont:model:missingTime`. Runs and equilibria still need an autonomous model.
- Continuation can start from an LP, H or BP point. A BP start raises `badProblem` and names `Switch`.
- Tests at Task 15: Rust 868 passed; MATLAB 105 tests, 104 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped (a BuildTest assumption).
- CI in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has four jobs: rust (ubuntu, windows, macos), pckern-win (cross build, dll-imports, artifact), matlab-windows (a self-hosted Windows runner, prebuilt DLL, no license secret) and asan. They first ran on GitHub on 2026-10-31, for `main` and for the P5 branch: rust on all three runners and pckern-win pass, and matlab-windows and package-win wait in the queue, because the self-hosted runner is offline. P8 moved every job to GitHub-hosted runners (see In progress).

## In progress

Analysis tools, slice 1 (branch `analysis-tools`, from `main` at 0f1e58c, approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 together with slices 2 to 4 and the EE mode; SPEC section 8ac). `Point.Jacobian`, `Point.Type` and `classify(pt, Tol = 1e-9)` classify an equilibrium or fixed point in 1, 2 and 3 dimensions and for maps, with T, D, Disc and the rotation, from the analytic Jacobian of the new C ABI entry `model_jacobian` (appended at offset 184, table now 192 bytes, `abi_version` unchanged). The band flags are a `uint8` set with `bitor` and read with `bitand`. In the phase-plane app a click appends the type and, for n of 3 or less, J to the status, and a 2D ODE saddle gets its two eigenvector lines (`pc-eigenvector`); each side spans one tenth of the window with each axis scaled by its own range, and `setWindow` resizes them. The map status checks in `MapPhasePlaneTest` now include the appended text. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2423 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; `runtests` on AppTest, ClassifyTest, AotTest and MapPhasePlaneTest gives 95 passed, 0 failed, including the fallback and AOT evaluator cases; the full suite (`buildtool test`, 2026-11-16) ran 865 tests: 856 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. Next: slice 2 (Branch Type column and a trace-determinant plot), slice 3 (closed-orbit checks), slice 4 (multistart fixed-point search), then the EE mode.

Analysis tools, slice 2 (branch `analysis-tools`, SPEC section 8ad, no Rust and no C ABI change). `[tbl, J] = classify(br, Tol = 1e-9)` gives the type of every point of an `"EP"` branch of an ODE or a map as a table (Alpha, Type, Hyperbolic, T, D, Disc, Rotation, NStable, NUnstable, NCenter, Borderline), and `tdplot(br)` draws a 2D branch in the trace-determinant plane with the parabola, the axes, the special points and, on a map, the stability triangle. The classification and the Jacobian request moved into `phasecont.internal.classifyJacobian` and `phasecont.internal.modelJacobian`, which `Point.classify` and `Branch.classify` share; `Point.classify` returns what it returned before. A four-agent review found three low items, all fixed: SPEC 8ad now states the axis padding of `tdplot` (10 percent of the width, at least 0.1) and that a `Tol` that is not a real double scalar fails the `arguments` block with its MATLAB validation identifier, as `Point.classify` already did, and the spiral branch test starts at r = -1 so it covers the whole range. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2423 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; `ClassifyTest` 25 passed, 0 failed; the full suite (`buildtool test`, 2026-11-16) ran 874 tests: 865 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (run before the spiral test change; `ClassifyTest` was rerun after it). Next: slice 3 (closed-orbit checks), slice 4 (multistart fixed-point search), then the EE mode.

Security pass (branch `analysis-tools`, 2026-11-16, not pushed). Windows GPU libraries no longer load from MATLAB's current folder: the first loads run inside `SetDllDirectoryW(L"")`, `nvcuda.dll` and `OpenCL.dll` come from System32 and the NVRTC builtins load by absolute path (`crates/pc-ensemble/src/winload.rs`, ace31ba). Before the fix, DLLs planted under those bare names in the current folder loaded; after it they do not, and the CUDA and OpenCL self-tests pass. A folder on `PATH` is still searched. `phasecont.latex.build` no longer runs a `latexmk` from the current folder: `phasecont.internal.findProgram` looks only in absolute `PATH` folders and `build.m` and `runBounded` run the full path (8f9fc4b); the new `LatexTest` case failed on the old `build.m` (the planted `.bat` ran) and passes now. Release 1.0.0-preview has no GPU code and no LaTeX build, so neither applies to it. Also: `.gitignore` covers env, key, log and agent files (a105001); zig and cargo-zigbuild are pinned, release builds use `--locked`, and CI has an advisories job (ffacd87); `SECURITY.md` (26bb232); local user and runner names left STATUS (18969a7). gitleaks found no secrets in 1008 commits. A delay EP run cancelled after its first point now ends `Cancelled` with no points, as SPEC 8s says (696272a). Checks: cargo fmt, clippy, test (2423 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored) and deny ok; FindProgramTest, LatexTest, RunBoundedTest and LatexStudioTest 74 passed, 0 failed. Open: a cumulative table-size limit in the `.ode` loader, private vulnerability reporting (which `SECURITY.md` relies on), dependabot, rulesets, immutable releases and provenance attestation, each an owner go.

P37 (branch `fit-ensemble`, from `main` at 8313202, started 2026-11-13; API approved by the owner on 2026-11-16 ("approve api"), rebased onto `main` at 85c5b23 the same day and merged locally, not pushed). Since `main` now has maps, `fit` refuses a map with `phasecont:fit:mapModel` (new test `aMapIsRefused`), and the size check of `MaxSteps` now raises `ensemble`'s `MATLAB:validators:mustBeScalarOrEmpty`. The branch was started at the owner's "go ahead get as much work done as possible by 7am". `phasecont.fit` fits model parameters to trajectory data with Levenberg-Marquardt in base MATLAB, one `ensemble` call per iteration (SPEC, "Data fitting (P37)"; P37 in the SPEC table). No Rust, C ABI or dependency change. Built 2026-11-13 on `fit-ensemble` (566c20b): `fit.m`, `FitResult.m` and `tests/FitTest.m`; a review pass the same day (three lenses, each finding checked by reading the code) led to f0bec26 and b53d67e: an `FdStep` that cannot move a parameter raises `badOption`, `History` grows by doubling so a large `MaxIter` allocates nothing up front, `Fitted` comes from the stored model values, a start state must be a vector, and the SPEC and help text state the `T0`, `jacobianFailed` (also at the start point), 100000-step and exact-zero-column rules. The fit tests grew from 37 to 58, with `FdScale` and `MaxSteps` to 71, and after a second review to 74; all pass in R2026b (`runtests('tests/FitTest.m')`, 74 passed, 0 failed). The full suite (`buildtool test`, 2026-11-13, at 5fe4395) ran 721 tests: 715 passed, 0 failed, 6 filtered by assumption (the same six as before), and `BaseMatlabTest` still finds base MATLAB only. Both review decisions were settled by the owner on 2026-11-13 ("fix") and are built. A tiny nonzero q_j gave a step of `FdStep` · |q_j| below the noise of the model, so column j now uses `FdStep` · max(|q_j|, s_j) with a required `FdScale` (a positive finite scalar, or one value per name in `Fit`, in the order of `Fit`); the step has no sign term, as the `FiniteDifferenceStepSize` and `TypicalX` rule in the MathWorks Optimization Options Reference reads (checked 2026-11-13; Dennis and Schnabel were not fetched and are not cited), and the `FdStep`² step is gone. With a = 1e-12 the old code raised `unidentifiable`; the Jacobian now has a relative error of 1.4e-7. `ensemble` stops a member after 100000 steps, so the optional `MaxSteps` passes through to `ensemble` unchanged (its checks apply) and the `startFailed` message names `MaxSteps` when the status is `maxSteps`; a rate-2e-4 decay over 2000 time units fits within 7.1e-16 with `MaxSteps` = 300000. A second review of `FdScale` and `MaxSteps` the same day (11 findings, each checked against the code) led to d733e9e and 5fe4395. New tests pin the divisor of the Jacobian to the realized step (q + eta) − q, the order and the entry checks of an `FdScale` vector, a `MaxSteps` of Inf or 2^32, `jacobianFailed` at an accepted step with a defined start Jacobian, and the `startFailed` case as the SPEC states it. Two changes from that review alter documented behavior, and the owner accepted both on 2026-11-13 ("thats fine"): an empty `MaxSteps` counts as not given, as an empty `Dt`, `Rtol` or `Atol` does, and the `jacobianFailed` message names `MaxSteps` when a perturbed point stops at the step limit. The owner's sign-off on 2026-11-16 covers the signature and result fields; the signature now includes `FdScale` and `MaxSteps`; the defaults the tests use for `Tau`, `GradTol`, `StepTol`, `MaxIter`, `FdStep` and `FdScale` (the function itself has none; Nielsen's examples use `GradTol` = `StepTol` = 1e-8, `MaxIter` = 200, `Tau` = 1e-6 for a good start and 1e-3 or 1 otherwise, and 1e-7 for the difference step); and that weights, bounds, a log scale, covariance, parameter scaling, fitting the start state and a drag-target `uifigure` mode are left out of this version. Probe on 2026-11-13 (decay model, a = 1.3): the forward-difference error is the truncation error and equals about 1.95 · `FdStep` for `Dt` = 0.01 and 0.05 and for `Rtol` from 1e-8 to 1e-12, so the adaptive step sequence adds no visible noise for `FdStep` of 1e-7 and larger on this model; at 1e-8 the error of `Dt` = 0.05 is already set by the RK4 truncation (7e-7).

P14a (branch `phase14-homoclinic`, commits 50b8e84 onward, listed by `git log 11cccf9..phase14-homoclinic`, from `main` at 11cccf9). The HOM curve continues an orbit homoclinic to a hyperbolic saddle in two parameters and labels the codim-2 points NSA, BLS, BLU, NSF, NDS, NDU, TLS, TLU, ZES, ZEU, OFS and OFU (HomCont tests 1 to 12). The C ABI is unchanged and `abi_version` stays 1. No new dependency.

- Engine: SPEC section 8b (50b8e84) has the formulation, sources and acceptance numbers. 0a28f8b adds invariant subspaces from the matrix sign function and the basis rotation in `pc-linalg`, 6093341 the non-periodic end rule of mesh equidistribution, 33dc32a the cycle problem hooks for a non-periodic curve, e5e9f93 `HomProblem` and `continue_hom_curve` with the `"orbit"` and `"cycle"` starts, and 7391546 the orbit flip, Belyakov and cycle start tests. `crates/pc-engine/tests/hom_curves.rs` covers SPEC tests 1 to 4; the measured values are in section 8b.
- Interface: 65b8642 adds `"type":"HOM"` and `"start"` to the problem JSON and the HOM row to `pc-ffi`, and 4b01985 tests it through the CLI. The row is `[a2, orbit, T, x0, a1, mesh, eig_re, eig_im, codim2(12), h, iters]`.
- MATLAB (18e2259, 02d26ed): `continuation(m, lc, Type="HOM", Index=i, Free=[a1 a2])` from an LC branch point, or `continuation(m, struct(t, x, p), Type="HOM", Free, NTST, NCOL)` from an orbit. `Index` is a new option. `Branch` gains `X0`, and `cycle(i)` on a HOM branch returns the orbit over T, which does not close. Special-point `Data.x0` is a column. SPEC section 11 has the bullet. `tests/HomCurveTest.m` (Sandstede curve from an orbit, BT curve from a long cycle, option errors, Index checks) and the HOM case of `Rows2BranchTest.m` pass: 19 of 19 with `scripts/matlab-win "addpath('toolbox'); r = runtests({'tests/Rows2BranchTest.m','tests/HomCurveTest.m'}); disp(table(r)); assert(all([r.Passed]))"`.
- Import check ruling (040914d): `pc-cli dll-imports` now allows `dbghelp.dll`. `dlopen2` 0.9.0, pulled in by `opencl3` for the P9c OpenCL backend, imports `SymFromAddrW`, `SymGetModuleBase64` and `SymInitializeW` from it. It is a Windows system DLL, so it fits the allow-list rule of SPEC section 12. Until this commit `scripts/build-pckern win64` failed the check, so no Windows `pckern` DLL was staged between P9c and now, and the staged DLL dated from 2026-11-04.
- Checks at 02d26ed: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1567 passed, 0 failed, against 1540 at P13) and `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok).
- MATLAB suite (first full run since P9): `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"` with the DLL staged on 2026-11-06 and a rebuilt `pckern.mexw64`. The staged MEX dated from 2026-11-04 and rejected the `latex` command (`unknown pckern command`), so a restage has to copy the MEX as well as the DLL: `build-pckern` does not rebuild the MEX. The run found problems in P9 to P12 MATLAB code that had never run, none in P14 code, and all are fixed:
  - `latexJson.m` encoded a mark's `x` and `y` as arrays, which `pc-latex` rejects (3354033, with a test).
  - `ModelEditor` added a second final newline on the Source tab, so the model hash differed from the file's hash (the fix is in the commit after 3354033, with a test); the Equations-tab test looked for a string the equations do not contain.
  - `ModelInputHelperTest/num2odeRoundTrips` passed `Message =` to `verifyEqual`, which takes the diagnostic as a positional argument.
  - `LatexTest/buildTimesOut` and `LatexStudioTest/compileShowsATimeout` hung the run. On this machine `latexmk` is `%USERPROFILE%\bin\latexmk.cmd`, Java cannot start a `.cmd` file, `runBounded` warns `phasecont:latex:noTimeout` and falls back to `system`, and the looping TeX document never ends. Both tests now skip through `fixtures.canBoundLatexmk` (2961e99). Fixed on 2026-11-07: `runBounded` looks the program up on the path with PATHEXT, without a shell, and gives Java the full path, so `latexmk.cmd` starts. Both tests now run on this machine and pass.
  - `EnsembleTest.m` and `ModelInputTest.m` had a name=value pair inside a cell array (`{Backend = "cpu"}`), a syntax error, so MATLAB excluded both files with a warning and every earlier run skipped them. Both are fixed; read the "was excluded" warnings in a suite log. Once they ran, two more problems showed up. `Model.fromEquations` documented `phasecont:model:unknownInit` for an `Init` name that is not a state, but the parser rejected the generated `init` line first, so the caller got `phasecont:engine:model`. The wrapper now reparses without `Init` on that failure only and names the extra entries (17b65eb), so valid models still parse once. The other problem was the `evalc` diagnostic in `selfTestOnAGpu` (see P9d).
- Left for P14b: the adjoint (inclination flips, HomCont tests 13 and 14, and the orientation index), non-central saddle-node homoclinics (IEQUIB=2, tests 15 and 16), heteroclinic orbits and Lin's method.

P13 (branch `phase13-periodic-normal-forms`, commits 9360baf to 55c86fc, Rust and docs only: no MATLAB API change, no C ABI change, no new dependency).

- 9360baf writes SPEC section 8a with the formulas and the acceptance tests. 07b0eda, 6f03089 and 3be6732 add the coefficient b of an LPC point, c of a PD point and d (Re and Im) of an NS point in `crates/pc-engine/src/cycle/normal.rs`, by periodic normalization on the collocation mesh. da31f0d stores the coefficient in `Extras` at LPC, PD and NS special points of a cycle branch (`coefficient` is b, c or Re d, `coefficient2` is Im d for NS). `curve_point_normal_form` evaluates it at any point of an LPC, PD or NS curve.
- Tests: `crates/pc-engine/tests/cycle_normal_forms.rs` has 19 tests and 14 own models `cyc_*.ode` in `tests/models`. All oracles are closed forms with T = pi, so a missing factor of T shows. LPC b = -4 (plain and with a linear change of coordinates), PD c = -1/pi (plain, nonlinear change, nonzero alpha1), NS d = -(2 + i)/pi (four variants with Re d and Im d asserted in each). Errors are at the 1e-14 level on the `ntst = 40` mesh, and each mesh doubling cuts the PD and NS errors by 200 and 285.
- Rossler, a = b = 0.2: the first PD is at c = 2.8324450279 and its coefficient is -0.0037643658, so the flip is supercritical. This is the only check against a model that is not ours, and it checks the sign only.
- Curves (SPEC test 6): the coefficient matches its closed form at every point of five curve runs, to 7e-10 or better, and changes sign at CPC, GPD and CH. The R1 and R2 products of the stored multipliers match their closed forms to 1e-6 and change sign.
- Checks at 55c86fc: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (every suite ok) and `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok), with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`.
- Differences from the plan, for the owner: the polar fold model with Omega = 1 has no shear and cannot test b, so test 1 uses a sheared model; PD, NS and curve oracles are constructed exact models, not an RK4 fit of a Poincare map; R1 and R2 are tests of multiplier products with no check for a Jordan block; the engine writes no criticality text, only the sign.
- E7b was left to the owner at E7a, because it changes the row layout. The owner approved it on 2026-11-06, and it is described next.
- Not run for E7a: nothing in E7a touches MATLAB, so the MATLAB suite was not needed.

P13 E7b (branch `phase13b-cycle-codim2`, commits 1b23b6d to the docs commit, from `main` at 20d1579). The CPC, GPD, CH, R1 and R2 labels are on the cycle curve runs.

- Engine (1b23b6d): `CycleProblem::coefficient` evaluates b, c or Re d at a curve point, and `codim2_tests` of the LPC, PD and NS curves appends it. The label lists are `["LPPD","LPNS","CPC","R1"]`, `["LPPD","PDNS","GPD","R2"]` and `["R1","R2","R3","R4","LPNS","PDNS","CH"]`. R1 is the product of mu - 1 and R2 the product of mu + 1 over the multipliers left after the exclusion rule, and both are NaN when none is left. A curve row is 26 values wide for LPC and PD (was 24) and 30 for NS (was 29) at n = 2, ntst = 2, ncol = 2. `cycle_stride` follows the label list, so no C function signature changes and `abi_version` stays 1.
- Results, all on closed forms with T = pi: CPC at |mu| = 2.4e-15, |nu| = 1.7e-16, |r - 1| < 1e-8. GPD at nu = 0 and beta = 1/4 to 1e-8. CH at |nu| = 6.5e-32. R1 at |nu| = 3.6e-11. R2 at |nu| = 3.6e-12 (and the second R2 at nu = 1 inside 1e-5). The R1 test is NaN on the planar CPC model. `cycle_normal_forms.rs` has 25 tests.
- Checks at 1b23b6d: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (1540 passed, 0 failed, against 1533 at E7a) and `cargo deny check`, with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`.
- Cost: the `cycle_curves` test binary took 29.8 s before and 33.9 s after, a factor of 1.14, so the coefficient runs at every `codim2_tests` call.
- Poles: the coefficients can go singular at a resonance, and `locate_one` already drops a codim-2 zero that ends above `POLE_TOL` (1e-2) times the smaller end value, which covers the new tests. No pole was seen in any E7b model, so that guard has not been exercised on a real pole. A planned `codim2_pole_prone` hook was not added, since the existing guard does the same.
- MATLAB (1099e49, ran on 2026-11-07 and passes): `rows2branch.m` reads the new lists, `Branch.m` and `continuation.m` document them, `latexTable.m` names CPC, GPD, CH, R1 and R2, and `Rows2BranchTest.m` and `CycleCurveTest.m` expect the new widths and labels.
- Owner decision on the build timeout (fe01d61): the owner vetoed the 300 s default and chose `Inf`, so `phasecont.latex.build` and `LatexStudio` set no limit by default, and README and the help say so. With `Inf` the build first ran through `system`, which Ctrl-C cannot interrupt. That changed on 2026-11-07: `Inf` takes the JVM route like a finite limit and only turns the clock off (see the follow-up block before Next). `buildHasNoLimitByDefault` ran and passes.
- MATLAB suite: the E7b label lists, row widths, LaTeX table names and the `Inf` default ran with the P14a suite and pass. The same suite ran the GPU self-test and the ensemble tests on the RTX 5060 and passes. Not run: CI on GitHub.

P11 (VS Code extension and language server) was merged into `main` locally on 2026-11-05 (9ace6ef). P12 (LaTeX companion) was merged into `main` locally on 2026-11-05 (32f1609, fast-forward from `phase12-latex`). The plan is in SPEC section 13. Nothing is pushed.

P12a (`crates/pc-latex` and `pc-cli latex`): no C ABI change and no MATLAB API change yet. The crate has no new third-party dependency (`serde_json` and `proptest` were already in the tree). The `standalone` JSON key and the CLI flag go beyond the approved option list and are reported to the owner.

- Tests: `pc-latex` has unit and `proptest` tests, 12 error tests, 7 golden tests and 3 compile tests. The compile tests ran against TeX Live 2026 `latexmk` with `PC_REQUIRE_LATEX=1`. `pc-cli` has 9 integration tests for `latex` and a parser test.
- MATLAB side: see P12b and P12c below. Both ran on 2026-11-07.

P12b, Rust side (`latex_render` in `pc-ffi`, gateway command `latex`): the C ABI grows from 23 to 24 members and from 176 to 184 bytes, with `abi_version` still 1. The owner approved this append and the gateway command. `pc-ffi` takes `pc-latex` as an in-workspace path dependency, so there is no new third-party crate.

- Ruling: a document the renderer rejects returns `PC_ERR_BAD_PROBLEM` (6) with the error object in `out_json`, and an unknown kind or a bad pointer returns `PC_ERR_BAD_ARG` (2) and leaves the slot NULL. No new error code. This follows the `import` and ensemble precedents. SPEC section 10 has the details.
- Tests: `crates/pc-ffi/tests/latex.rs` (7 tests: figure equals the crate's output, table, external data files, every error kind, unknown kind, null and non-UTF-8 arguments) and `tests/header.rs` (the new member, offset 176, table size 184, and `PC_LATEX_*` against the Rust constants).
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (1514 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored, with `PC_REQUIRE_LATEX=1` and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`) and `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok) pass.
- The gateway command was first only syntax-checked (`gcc -fsyntax-only -Wall -Wextra -std=c99` against the R2026b headers, no output). The MEX gateway was built and ran in MATLAB on 2026-11-07 (the `latex` command works).

P12b, MATLAB side (commits dd5c90b and 6927797, tests in 7d16cc7): `phasecont.latex.{figureFromBranch,pgfplots,specialTable,build}` and the internal helpers `latexJson`, `latexRender`, `latexWrite` and `latexTable`. `tests/LatexTest.m` checks that the API text equals the Rust output for the same figure, that a missing `Digits` raises, and the build and error paths. Nothing here has run in MATLAB, and the `latex` gateway command has not run in `mex`. Tests that need the gateway or `latexmk` are filtered when they are missing.

P12c (commits f52e5d4 and fbe8860): `phasecont.app.LatexStudio` and `tests/LatexStudioTest.m` (20 tests that drive the app through its methods, 22 after P12d). They ran in MATLAB on 2026-11-07 and pass, except `compileShowsATimeout`, which skips when Java cannot start `latexmk`. The Branch menu and the toolbar callbacks are not driven by any test.

- Scope cut: the optional pdf.js preview is not built, so `LatexStudio` has no `Preview` option and only opens the PDF in the system viewer. No pdf.js code is bundled, so `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` has no new entry.
- Error "jump" is a context pane that shows the lines around the error. The `uitextarea` cursor API is not confirmed, so the editor does not move its cursor.
- Insert always writes inline data, and Compile blocks the window.
- Beyond the approved list, for the owner to rule on: the `Standalone` option (CLI flag, JSON key, MATLAB option); the `Executable` option on `build` and `LatexStudio`; the `Basename` option (defaults "figure" and "table"); the `Color` option of `figureFromBranch` (default black, `#000000`); the MATLAB error ids `phasecont:latex:{writeFailed,engineNotFound,badEngine}` and `phasecont:app:{badOpener,openFailed,saveFailed,empty,noSuchError,noPdf,noBranch}`.
- Choices, accepted by the owner on 2026-11-06 ("use the best recommendation"): `specialTable` columns are the free parameters plus the state variables for EP, LP and H, with row labels like `LP1` and type names from a small map; `Folder` is optional, and external data without `Folder` writes nothing; a one-point series renders invisibly; `build` leaves its temp folder; axis labels and titles are always escaped, so raw math labels are not supported; `compat=1.18` needs pgfplots 1.18 or newer; non-ASCII text passes through unescaped.

P12d, time limit for `phasecont.latex.build` (branch `p12-build-timeout`): `build` takes `Timeout` (seconds, default `Inf` for none, set by the owner on 2026-11-06 after vetoing 300) and returns `TimedOut`. `LatexStudio` takes the same option and shows "stopped after N s". The new internal function `phasecont.internal.runBounded` starts `latexmk` through `java.lang.ProcessBuilder`, polls with `pause` (so Ctrl-C works), and on expiry or on Ctrl-C stops `latexmk` and its child processes. No MATLAB API change beyond this option and field, which the owner asked for. Tests: five new ones in `tests/LatexTest.m` (a looping `\def\a{\a}\a` document stops after 3 s with `TimedOut` true and, on Linux, no process left in the build folder; `Timeout = Inf` and `Timeout = 120` still build; bad values raise) and two in `tests/LatexStudioTest.m`.

- MATLAB: the five new tests in `LatexTest.m` and two in `LatexStudioTest.m` ran on 2026-11-07. The two that need a finite limit on a looping document skipped here until the `.cmd` lookup of 2026-11-07, and run and pass now. Open risks, each with a fallback and a warning: the Java route to a non-public `ProcessImpl` class, a program that Java cannot start even by its full path (`build` warns `phasecont:latex:noTimeout` for a finite limit and runs with no limit), and a JVM older than Java 9 (`phasecont:latex:killIncomplete`, only the main program stops).
- Default is `Inf`: the owner vetoed 300 s on 2026-11-06. Since 2026-11-07 `Inf` runs through the JVM wait loop too, so Ctrl-C stops the program and its children (owner approval, "do everything"). Without a JVM (`matlab -nojvm`) it falls back to `system`, silently for `Inf` and with `phasecont:latex:noTimeout` for a finite limit.

P11a (`crates/pc-lsp`, commits 5be3cff and e9c5cc5): no C ABI change, no MATLAB API change. New direct dependencies: `lsp-server` 0.10.0, `lsp-types` 0.97.0 and `serde_json` (already in the tree), all with licences on the allowed list.

- The server is `lsp-server` plus a loop of its own: requests before `initialize` are refused, queued messages are applied in order and each changed document is analysed once, and a request flushes the queue first. Open buffers are served to `#include` before disk, and includes are read only inside the workspace folders or the document's directory. A diagnostic in an included file goes to that file, and an edit to an included file re-checks the open documents that read it. `shutdown` then `exit` gives code 0, `exit` alone gives 1. Every handler and every analysis runs under `catch_unwind`.
- Features: hover, completion (trigger `@`), document symbols, go to definition, quick fixes from fix-its.
- `pc-ode` change: `options::NAMES` and `options::NUMBERED` are public, so completion can list option names.
- Checks at e9c5cc5: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (1460 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` (advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok) pass, with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`. `pc-lsp` has unit tests, `tests/diagnostics.rs` (every file in `pc-ode/tests/invalid`), `tests/overlay.rs`, `tests/session.rs` (14, over an in-memory connection) and `tests/stdio.rs` (6, over the real binary's pipes).
- `#![allow(clippy::mutable_key_type)]` is set for the crate: `lsp_types::Uri` holds a `Cell`, but its `Hash` and `Eq` read only the text.
- Known limits: an include that changes on disk outside the editor is picked up at the next edit of the file that includes it, and an include that did not exist is not seen as a dependency until then. Malformed message headers are resynchronised on a best-effort basis. Windows `file:///C:/...` handling has not run on Windows.
P11b and P11c (`editors/vscode/`, commit 9e9ebd7 and the commits after it): TypeScript client, TextMate grammar written from `crates/pc-ode/docs/language.md`, language configuration, `README.md`, `LICENSE` and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt`. No C ABI change and no MATLAB API change.

- npm packages, installed only under `editors/vscode/` with exact versions: `vscode-languageclient` 9.0.1 (runtime); `@types/node`, `@types/vscode`, `esbuild`, `typescript`, `vscode-oniguruma` and `vscode-textmate` (dev). `vscode-languageclient` 10.1.2 was refused: it pulls `minimatch` 10.2.6 (BlueOak-1.0.0) and an advisory. Version 9.0.1 has only MIT and ISC packages and `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities. `esbuild` bundles the client into `dist/extension.js`, so the extension ships no `node_modules`.
- `npm test` runs `tsc` and 34 `node --test` tests: server lookup (9), grammar (16, including a check against every builtin in `builtins.rs`), manifest and licence files (9). The grammar tests were checked against a deliberately broken grammar and failed as they should.
- CI job `vscode` builds `pc-lsp`, checks `--version`, runs `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and `npm test`, builds the linux-x64 `.vsix` with `vsce package --no-dependencies --target linux-x64`, checks that the `.vsix` holds the server and `dist/extension.js` and no `node_modules`, and uploads it. The job has not run on GitHub.
- `@vscode/vsce` 4.1.0 is a dev dependency, approved by the owner on 2026-11-06 (option a) as a build-tool exception to the licence list. It never ships: `.vscodeignore` leaves out everything except the listed files, so the `.vsix` has no `node_modules`. The licences of its tree, from `package-lock.json`: proprietary `@vscode/vsce-sign` and its nine platform packages ("SEE LICENSE IN LICENSE.txt", not used by `vsce package`), Artistic-2.0 (`binaryextensions`, `editions`, `istextorbinary`, `textextensions`, `version-range`), BlueOak-1.0.0 (`sax`, and `minimatch` inside `vsce`) and 0BSD (`tslib`). `npm audit` reports 2 moderate advisories, both the `minimatch` denial of service inside `vsce`; they affect the build machine only. The runtime dependency set is unchanged (`vscode-languageclient` and its MIT and ISC packages).
- Checked here: `npm ci --ignore-scripts` in a clean copy, then `vsce package --no-dependencies --target linux-x64` built a 10-file, 869 KB `.vsix` with `extension/server/linux-x64/pc-lsp` (mode 755) and `extension/dist/extension.js`. `vsce package` runs `npm run bundle` through `vscode:prepublish`, and its `--out` folder must exist already.
- Installed 2026-11-07: after `cargo build --release -p pc-lsp`, `npm test` in `editors/vscode` (34 passed, 0 failed) and `vsce package --no-dependencies --target linux-x64`, `code --install-extension` put `phasecont-ode-linux-x64-0.1.0.vsix` (10 files, 869.59 KB) into the WSL VS Code, and `code --list-extensions --show-versions` lists `nub04.phasecont-ode@0.1.0`. I did not open an `.ode` file in the editor, so the diagnostics, hover and completion in the UI are not checked by me.
- Scanned and reinstalled 2026-11-10: the installed server was one build behind `main` (`pc-ode` diagnostic changes), so I rebuilt `pc-lsp`, ran `npm test` in `editors/vscode` (34 passed, 0 failed), packaged `phasecont-ode-linux-x64-0.1.0.vsix` (10 files, 869.69 KB) and installed it with `code --install-extension --force`. Scan of the package: no `node_modules`, no dotfiles, keys or tokens, and no home path or email in any text file. `extension.js` is byte for byte the one already installed, and the only difference in `package.json` is VS Code's own `__metadata` block. `pc-lsp` links only libc and libgcc_s, imports no socket, `execve`, `system` or `popen` symbol, and holds no URL or shell command string. The only `child_process` and `net` uses in the bundle come from `vscode-languageclient`; `src/` has none. A stdio handshake (`initialize`, `didOpen` of a file that uses an undeclared `q`, `shutdown`, `exit`) returns diagnostic ODE0039 on `q`, and the server exits with code 0. Still not checked: the diagnostics, hover and completion UI inside VS Code.
- Updated 2026-11-17 (branch `vscode-languageclient-11`, replaces Dependabot PR #7): the runtime package is `vscode-languageclient` 11.0.0. The owner approved BlueOak-1.0.0 for its runtime tree on 2026-11-17, so the two lines above that refuse 10.1.2 and call the runtime set MIT and ISC are history. The bundle now holds nine packages: `vscode-languageclient` 11.0.0, `vscode-jsonrpc` 10.0.0, `vscode-languageserver-protocol` 3.18.5, `vscode-languageserver-types` 3.18.5, `vscode-languageserver-textdocument` 1.0.16, `brace-expansion` 5.0.13 and `balanced-match` 4.0.4 (MIT), `semver` 7.8.6 (ISC) and `minimatch` 10.2.6 (BlueOak-1.0.0). `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt` was rebuilt from the license files in those package folders. Version 11 needs a `LogOutputChannel`, so the extension creates its channel with `{ log: true }`, and the client then logs the server traffic only while that channel's log level is Trace; `phasecont.trace.server` picks how much, and at Trace its `off` value logs the messages. Checked here: `npm ci --ignore-scripts` and `npm test` (34 passed, 0 failed), and `vsce package --no-dependencies --target linux-x64` built a 10-file, 886.88 KB `.vsix` with the server and `dist/extension.js` and no `node_modules`. Not checked: the client starting inside VS Code with version 11.
- Open, owner (2026-11-17): `minimatch` 10.2.6 is now in the shipped bundle, and GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249 (moderate, >= 7.0.0 and < 11.0.1) covers it; `npm audit --omit=dev` reports it. The advisory needs `optimizationLevel` 2 or more. `vscode-languageclient` builds its matchers with `dot` and `nocase` (`lib/common/fileOperations.js`) or `noext` (`lib/common/utils/globPattern.js`), so the default level 1 applies, and the globs come from `pc-lsp`, which registers no file operations. Options: (a) accept and record that the code path is not reached; (b) an npm `overrides` entry for `minimatch` 11.0.1 or newer, which is outside the `^10.2.6` that version 11 asks for and adds `@isaacs/nonrecursive-glob-match` (BlueOak-1.0.0); (c) stay on 9.0.1. The branch is held until the owner picks one.
- Not run: the VS Code UI, the `win32-x64`, `darwin-arm64` and `darwin-x64` server builds and their `.vsix` files.

P9 (GPU ensemble engine) and P10 (model input routes and the editor app) had an approved plan. The stacked branches `phase9-gpu-ensemble`, `phase10-model-input` and `phase9d-ensemble-api` were fast-forwarded into `main` locally on 2026-11-05 (835ff12), with the version kept at 1.0.0. Nothing is pushed: `origin/main` is still at efd172e. The plan approval covered the appended C ABI functions, the MATLAB API additions, the new dependencies and the new hosted CI jobs. Nothing in P9 or P10 has run on MATLAB, a GPU or Windows yet. Only `cargo` on the CPU has run.

P9a (core types, CPU backend, kernel text, device list, self-test and the CLI) is committed on `phase9-gpu-ensemble`, with no C ABI change and no MATLAB API change.

- `pc-ensemble` has the job types, a rayon CPU backend in `f32` and `f64` with fixed-step RK4 and adaptive Dormand-Prince 5(4), the C-dialect kernel text with a bit-exact shim test against the CPU backend, `list_devices`, `find_device`, `run_on` and the self-test. `pc-cli gpu list`, `gpu selftest` and `ensemble` (with `--bench`) sit on top of it.
- Checks at e6f4860: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (no failures) and `cargo deny check` pass. These ran on the CPU backend only.
- Rulings made while building P9a:
  - A job has a `nonfinite` status in addition to ok, bounds, step_too_small and max_steps. A member whose state becomes NaN or infinite stops and reports it, and the run still succeeds.
  - Time runs forward only (`t0 < t1`). Samples are strictly increasing in (t0, t1] and the last one is `t1`.
  - The RK4 `dt` is a maximum step, and the last step of an interval is shortened to land on it. The Dormand-Prince steps are cut to land on each sample time.
  - The single-precision unit roundoff is 2^-23. A Dormand-Prince `rtol` below `13 * UROUND` for the job precision is `ToleranceTooTight`, an error and not a clamp.
  - A NaN error estimate shrinks the step by 5 until the step-size check fails, and the member then reports `nonfinite`.
  - The ensemble kernels use `libm::pow`, so the CPU backend and the shim agree bit for bit. `emit_c` in `pc-codegen` is not changed.
  - `refine(i)` (P9d) will run member i again through the CPU backend in `f64`.
  - The self-test reports `bit_exact: null` for the CPU backend, because it is the reference. A backend that is not built, or a device index that does not exist, gives exit 3 and nothing on stdout, never a pass. A device that refuses a job gives exit 1.
  - The tier limits of the self-test come from measurements on the CPU backend, with the margins written next to each limit in `selftest.rs`.
  - `gpu selftest` needs `--backend` and `--precision`. `--device` defaults to 0, and the JSON names the device that ran.
  - `ensemble --bench` runs both precisions on every device, labels each row and prints its sizes (1000, 10000 and 100000 members unless `--members` says otherwise). A device without double precision gets a `skipped` row.
  - The job file needs every key except `device`. `bounds` is a positive number, or `null` for no limit. Unknown keys are errors and each error names its key.
  - The self-test JSON has a `commit` field that is the value of `PC_GIT_COMMIT` at build time, or `null`. There is no `build.rs`.
- Not done in this stage: the CUDA, OpenCL and Metal backends (P9b, P9c, P9e), the C ABI and MATLAB API (P9d) and the hosted CI jobs. The sections below cover each.

P9b (the CUDA backend) is written and tested on the CPU side only. Nothing ran on the GPU, MATLAB or Windows then, because the owner asked for no local hardware runs on 2026-11-05. The CUDA self-test through MATLAB on the RTX 5060 passed on 2026-11-07 (see P9d).

- `crates/pc-ensemble/src/cuda.rs` adds the NVRTC lookup, the PTX or cubin route, the probe, `compile_ptx_text`, `run_cuda` and the chunked launch loop. `list_devices` and `run_on` use it. The error type has `GpuUnavailable`, `GpuCompile`, `GpuLaunch` and `GpuTimeout`.
- `cudarc` 0.19.10 is a new dependency (MIT or Apache-2.0, `cargo deny check` passes), and `libloading` moved from a dev-dependency to a dependency.
- Checks, run with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (no failures) and `cargo deny check`. `tests/cuda_compile.rs` ran for real against the WSL toolkit's NVRTC. The dispatch tests print `SKIPPED: no cuda device (cuInit: CUDA_ERROR_NO_DEVICE)`.
- `pc-cli dll-imports` already rejects every DLL that is not on its allow-list, so `nvcuda`, `nvrtc` and `OpenCL` fail it. A unit test now says so. The staged `pckern.dll` was not rechecked.
- `run_cuda` first ran on a device through MATLAB on 2026-11-07 (the RTX 5060, see P9d). Open risks: the macOS build of `cudarc` is unverified. A test that calls `list_devices` starts CUDA when `libcuda` is visible, so a machine with a GPU and a driver runs those tests on the device.

P9c (the OpenCL backend) is written and tested on the CPU side only. Nothing ran on an OpenCL device, MATLAB or Windows then, and WSL has no OpenCL runtime. The OpenCL self-test through Windows MATLAB on the RTX 5060 passed on 2026-11-07 (see P9d).

- `crates/pc-ensemble/src/opencl.rs` adds the platform and device probe, `run_opencl` and the OpenCL launch code. `gpu.rs` holds what `cuda.rs` and `opencl.rs` share: `guard`, `kernel_err`, the chunk sizing and the `drive` launch loop. `kernel.rs` has the OpenCL entry point, and the OpenCL prelude in `pc-codegen` has `#pragma OPENCL FP_CONTRACT OFF`.
- `opencl3` 0.12.4 is a new dependency (`dynamic` feature, default features off). `cargo deny check` passes with its transitive crates (`cl3`, `opencl-sys`, `dlopen2`).
- Checks, run with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (1249 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check`. `tests/opencl_compile.rs` ran for real: clang 23.1.4 parsed every model and variant as OpenCL C 1.2, and it rejected a deliberately broken kernel.
- `pc-cli gpu list` now reports OpenCL as `available` or `unavailable`, not `not_built`. Only Metal is `not_built` (on every platform but macOS since P9e).
- `run_opencl` first ran on a device through MATLAB on 2026-11-07: OpenCL device 0 on this laptop is the RTX 5060 (NVIDIA driver 621.14), and device 1, the Intel Arc 140T (driver 32.0.101.9150, OpenCL 3.0 NEO), ran the self-test on 2026-11-08 and passed all four checks in single and double precision (largest errors 2.6e-6 and 1.5e-9, `rotation`). Open risks: a driver may ignore `FP_CONTRACT OFF`. The 1.5 s `gpuTimeout` rule is a guess. The macOS build of `opencl3` is unverified.

P9d (the ensemble C ABI and the MATLAB API) is written and tested on the CPU side only, on branch `phase9d-ensemble-api`. Nothing ran on MATLAB, a GPU or Windows when it was written, and the gateway C had not been built with `mex`. All of that ran on 2026-11-07 (see below).

- C ABI (d6077d3, 0e47795, c9776f5): five functions are appended after `import`, `ens_devices` (offset 136), `ens_start` (144), `ens_poll` (152), `ens_copy` (160) and `ens_cancel` (168). `abi_version` stays 1 and the table has 23 members in 176 bytes. Error codes 9 to 13 are gpuUnavailable, gpuCompile, gpuLaunch, gpuTimeout and unsupportedModel. The job parser moved from `pc-cli` into `pc-ffi`, and the CPU backend reads the cancel flag every 65536 steps.
- A run is an `EnsHandle` in `crates/pc-ffi/src/ensemble.rs`. A worker thread runs under the panic barrier, a panic on it reaches MATLAB from `ens_poll` or `ens_copy`, and freeing the handle cancels the run and joins the worker. A bad job and a tolerance that is too tight both map to `badProblem` and are told apart by the message prefix.
- Gateway (0bd376a): `ens_devices`, `ens_start`, `ens_poll`, `ens_copy` and `ens_cancel` commands, and the five error IDs `phasecont:engine:<kind>`. It was first checked only with `gcc -fsyntax-only`, and it was built with `mex` and ran in MATLAB on 2026-11-07.
- MATLAB (5942b85, b53ddbe, 3f7a19e, 6147d05): `phasecont.gpuDevices`, `phasecont.ensemble` with `EnsembleResult` and `refine`, and `phasecont.gpuSelfTest`, with `tests/EnsembleTest.m` and the helper tests. `Precision`, `t0`, `t1` and `Rtol` with `Atol` (or `Dt`) have no defaults and raise named errors. A tolerance below what the precision can resolve raises `phasecont:ensemble:toleranceTooTight`. GPU test cases honour `PC_REQUIRE_GPU`.
- SPEC sections 10 and 11 describe the ABI and the API (b3d4035).
- Checks at b3d4035, run with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (1353 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass.
- Rulings: `Backend` defaults to `"auto"` and `Device` to 0, the 0-based index within a backend. `MaxSteps` (100000), `Hmax` (Inf) and `Bounds` (Inf) default to the limits `simulate` uses. The method is inferred from the options (`Dt` gives RK4, `Rtol` with `Atol` gives Dormand-Prince). `refine` returns an `EnsembleResult`, not a `Trajectory`. Ensemble results are not saved in sessions.
- `gpuSelfTest` runs in MATLAB with no extra ABI call. Its pass thresholds (5e-4 and 1e-3 in single precision, 1e-8 and 1e-7 in double) are my own and unvalidated. `pc-cli gpu selftest` runs the full tiers.
- First MATLAB run (2026-11-07): `tests/EnsembleTest.m` passes, 37 tests, none filtered. The step-count ranges, the `gpuDevices` shapes and the error IDs held. The suite ran `gpuSelfTest` on this laptop's GPU through the CUDA and OpenCL backends in single and double precision, and all four pass, so the self-test thresholds hold on that one card and driver. Nothing was run on AMD, Intel, Apple or a second NVIDIA card, and the GPU speed numbers are still missing. One test bug had hidden the verdict: the diagnostic argument of `selfTestOnAGpu` added two `evalc` char rows of different length with `+`, which errors before the verdict is read (the fix wraps both in `string`).
- The GPU speed numbers are in `benches/RESULTS.md` (measured 2026-11-08 on an RTX 5060 and an Arc 140T). The CUDA and OpenCL backends keep one context per device and up to 8 compiled kernels per device between runs (SPEC, GPU ensemble engine). With both kept, the RTX 5060 beats the 16-thread CPU at every size measured: 0.9 ms against 5.9 ms on CUDA at 1000 members in single precision, 6.7 against 55.5 at 10000 and 69.6 against 567.7 at 100000. The first run of a new model or precision still pays one compile (about 11 ms on CUDA, 5 to 7 ms on OpenCL for the RTX 5060), and the first run in a process also creates the context (112 to 163 ms on the RTX 5060). The kept context and kernels hold device memory until MATLAB exits, and a `reset(gpuDevice)` between ensemble calls makes the next call fail once. The path that removes a kept entry after a launch error, a timeout or a panic has never run on a device: no test forced one.
- Poll schedule (SPEC section 11, `phasecont.internal.pollDelay`, MATLAB only, no Rust, API or ABI change): `ensemble`, `continuation`, `simulate` and the session replay used to pause 50 ms after the first poll, so every call took at least about 50 ms. They now wait 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 and 19 ms after the first six polls and 50 ms after that, so the polls include every time the old loop polled. Warm calls on 2026-11-08 in Windows MATLAB: a 2-member `ensemble` took 4.1 ms at the median on the CPU, 6.3 ms on the Arc 140T and 4.0 ms on the RTX 5060 (was 51 to 57 ms), and a 20-step fold `continuation` took 5.1 ms (was 52.3 ms, measured on the old loops, `benches/RESULTS.md`, MATLAB call latency). Tests: `PollDelayTest` (7) and one loose latency test each in `EnsembleTest` and `ContinuationTest` (fastest of 8 warm runs under 45 ms; both fail on the old loops, which took 51.6 ms and 52.2 ms at best). The nine suites that touch the loops passed (119 of 119, no skips), and the full `buildtool test` ran 521 tests with 516 passed, 0 failed and 5 skipped by assumption (build, package and oracle tests that do not use the loops). The first `gpuDevices` call in a process still costs about 790 ms. A profile on 2026-11-08 found about 305 ms of that in a bare Windows process (CUDA 203 ms, OpenCL 103 ms), 25 ms saved by probing in parallel, and about 340 ms inside MATLAB that was not split further; no change was made (`benches/RESULTS.md`, MATLAB call latency).

P9e (the Metal backend) is written and type-checked only, on branch `phase9d-ensemble-api`. Nothing was linked or run on a Mac, and Metal has never compiled the kernel text.

- First run on a Mac (2026-11-08, `ci` run 42466164455 at 88750fc, both `gpu-macos` legs green): the hosted `macos-15` (macOS 15.8.2) and `macos-26` (macOS 26.7.2) images each show one Metal device, "Apple Paravirtual device" (about 5.0 GB, `fp32` true, `fp64` false). Apple's `metal` tool compiled every kernel. The self-test passed in single precision on both images: `bit_exact` true, `analytic-rk4` max error 7.15e-7 (limit 1e-5), `analytic-dp5` 4.25e-5 (limit 1e-3), `determinism` 0. Apple's OpenCL lists no device there (`CL_INVALID_VALUE`), so that case printed SKIPPED. This settles the first four open risks below for Metal (hex-float literals, the pragmas, `ulong`, safe-math compile). Still unverified on Metal: the watchdog error code, whether `FP_CONTRACT` is honoured on a real GPU (a paravirtual device passes the bit-exact tier, which is weaker evidence than a physical Apple GPU), and speed, since the device is virtual. `ci.yml` now sets `PC_REQUIRE_GPU: metal` on the self-test step, so a runner that loses the device fails the step. The job stays non-blocking because GitHub chooses the hardware.

- `crates/pc-ensemble/src/metal.rs` holds what builds on every platform: the entry-point name, `group_for` (threads per threadgroup), `command_error` (a watchdog stop is `gpuTimeout`, any other failure is `gpuLaunch`) and a stub that reports `not_built` and `unsupported` off macOS. `metal/host.rs` is built on macOS only. It lists devices with `MTLCopyAllDevices`, compiles the kernel with safe math, runs the launches through `gpu::drive` and reads the results back from shared buffers. A double-precision job on Metal is `gpuUnavailable`, since MSL has no `double`.
- `objc2`, `objc2-foundation` and `objc2-metal` are macOS-only dependencies (MIT, Apache-2.0 or Zlib; `cargo deny check` passes). `list_devices` and `run_on` route to the backend, and `pc-cli gpu list` shows `not_built` on Linux.
- Checks, run with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=""`: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (1364 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check`.
- The macOS build can be type-checked on Linux: `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1 cargo check -Zbuild-std=std,panic_abort --target aarch64-apple-darwin -p pc-ensemble`, and the same with `clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`. Both pass. They cover the macOS builds of `cudarc`, `opencl3` and the objc2 crates. They link and run nothing.
- Open risks, all unverified on a Mac: whether Metal accepts hex-float literals, the `fp math_mode` and `fp contract` pragmas and `ulong` at its default language version, whether a watchdog stop arrives as error code 2 or as "Impacting Interactivity", and whether `FP_CONTRACT` is honoured. An Objective-C exception is not a panic, so `guard` cannot catch it. The calls used return `nil` or an `NSError` on failure.

The hosted CI jobs (a1e0576, 08f1998) are written on `phase9d-ensemble-api` and have never run on GitHub. GitHub-hosted runners have no GPU, and there is no self-hosted runner.

- `gpu-pocl` (ubuntu-latest, blocking): installs `pocl-opencl-icd`, `clinfo` and `clang`, then runs `device_selftest`, `dispatch`, `opencl_compile`, `msl_syntax` and `kernel_cache`, and `pc-cli gpu selftest --backend opencl` in single and double precision. `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=opencl,clang-opencl,clang-msl` and `PC_REQUIRE_FP64=opencl` make a skip or a missing `cl_khr_fp64` fail the job. This is the only job that runs the OpenCL host path end to end, and it runs it on a CPU.
- `gpu-nvrtc` (ubuntu-latest, blocking): downloads NVIDIA's `cuda_nvrtc` 13.5.37 redistributable archive, checks its SHA-256 and runs `cuda_compile` with `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=nvrtc`. It compiles the kernel to PTX and needs no driver or device. The same recipe passed locally against the pinned archive (6 tests).
- `gpu-macos` (macos-15 and macos-26, `continue-on-error`): lists devices, writes the Metal kernels with `PC_DUMP_MSL`, compiles each with `xcrun metal`, then runs `device_selftest` with `--include-ignored` and `pc-cli gpu selftest --backend metal`. `PC_REQUIRE_GPU` is not set, so a runner with no Metal device prints `SKIPPED` and stays green.
- Open risks: the PoCL package names and ICD loader on the runner image, whether the bit-exact tier passes on PoCL (its divide and contraction behaviour is unknown), whether the Metal toolchain downloads on the hosted Macs, and whether the hosted Macs show any Metal device. The `gpu-pocl` job is blocking, so it may fail on the first push.
- New test: `tests/device_selftest.rs` runs the self-test on device 0 of each backend, in every precision the device supports. It skips loudly with no device. On macOS the OpenCL and Metal cases are `#[ignore]`d for the blocking `cargo test`. `msl_syntax` writes the kernels as `.metal` files when `PC_DUMP_MSL` names a folder.

P10b (`pc-import` and the `import` C ABI function) is written and tested on the CPU side only, on branch `phase10-model-input`. Nothing ran on MATLAB or Windows then, because the owner asked for no local runs on 2026-11-05. `ModelInputTest` drives `fromFunction` and the SBML and `.m` file routes through the `import` command on 2026-11-07, and passes.

- `crates/pc-import` reads a MATLAB function (the text of `func2str(h)` or of a function file), SBML (with a MathML reader) and turns each into `.ode` text. Input is read as text and never run. A refusal names its kind: syntax, unsupported, undeclared, missingValue, mismatch, nameCollision, tooLarge, noDynamics or xml, with a line and column when the position is known. SBML events, delays and algebraic rules are refused by name. `roxmltree` is the only new dependency and it refuses a DTD.
- `import` is appended to the C ABI after `model_rhs_t`: `int32_t import(uint32_t format, const char *bytes, size_t len, const char *opts_json, char **result_json)`. `abi_version` stays 1. The table has 18 members and 136 bytes, and `import` is at offset 128. `PC_IMPORT_SBML` is 1 and `PC_IMPORT_MATLAB` is 2.
- A refused import returns `PC_ERR_MODEL` and a JSON object with the importer's id (`phasecont:import:<kind>`), so no new error code is needed. Bad arguments return `PC_ERR_BAD_ARG` and leave the slot NULL. Input over 17 MiB is a bad argument. The import runs on an 8 MiB thread under the panic barrier.
- The gateway has an `import` command (`mex/pckern_gateway.c`). It returns the JSON for a refusal without raising, and MATLAB raises the importer's id from it. The gateway C was not compiled in WSL, since there is no `mex.h` there. It was built with `mex` on Windows on 2026-11-07.
- Tests: `cargo test -p pc-ffi --test import --test header` gives 14 and 6 passing. The import tests cover both formats, options, every failure kind, bad arguments, NULL pointers, oversize input and deep nesting (20 000 parentheses, 50 000 nested XML tags) without a panic.
- Not done yet: the CellML reader.

The MATLAB side of P10 first ran on 2026-11-07 and passes (`ModelInputTest`, `ModelInputHelperTest`, `ModelEditorTest`), after the fixes listed under P14a. SPEC section 11 describes it.

- `Model.fromEquations`, `fromTable`, `fromFunction` and `fromFile` are in `toolbox/+phasecont/Model.m`. The helpers are in `toolbox/+phasecont/+internal/`: `odeText`, `num2ode`, `tableToEquations`, `readSbml`, `importRequest` and `importText`.
- `phasecont.app.ModelEditor` is the editor app, with tabs for equations, a table and `.ode` text, a diagnostics list, file open for `.ode`, SBML and `.m`, and a button that opens the last good model in the phase plane.
- Tests: `tests/ModelInputHelperTest.m` (no engine needed), `tests/ModelInputTest.m` (engine) and `tests/ModelEditorTest.m` (app). None has run.
- Things the first MATLAB run may break on: `uitable` cell data with `[]` in numeric columns, `fileread` with an `Encoding` option, `native2unicode` on odd bytes, the exact id `MATLAB:validators:mustBeMember` that `ModelEditorTest` expects from `selectTab`, and the `uigetfile` and `inputdlg` calls, which no test covers.

P8 (v1.0 packaging and the release workflow, plan approved) was built on branch `phase8-release` and is now on `main`. It was accepted on 2026-11-04, when the tagged `release.yml` run published a `.mltbx` that installs and runs on all three platforms.

- 1ec01ec: `pc-cli licenses` takes several `--target` triples, so one file covers win64, glnxa64 and maca64.
- 3204096: version 1.0.0 with the same toolbox UUID. `packageToolbox` ships `bin/win64`, `bin/glnxa64` and `bin/maca64` and fails with `missingBinary` naming an absent file. `PackageTest` checks the six files and the path list with fake binaries. `cleanInstallCheck.m` takes the child MATLAB from `PHASECONT_CHILD_MATLAB`.
- 5175d78: `scripts/build-pckern` remaps the repo and cargo home paths. `scripts/check-host-paths` fails on `/home/`, `/Users/` and `C:\Users` in a binary, and `scripts/check-glibc` fails on a symbol version above `GLIBC_2.28`.
- c67211c: the gateway pins `dlopen`, `dlsym`, `dlerror`, `dladdr` and `dlclose` to `GLIBC_2.2.5` on Linux x86_64. No C ABI change.
- 8e7219c: `ci.yml`, `macos-jit.yml` and the new `release.yml` run on GitHub-hosted runners only, with every action pinned by SHA and `persist-credentials: false` on every checkout. The self-hosted Windows runner is no longer used by any workflow.
- Docs: README rewritten in run order (install, upgrade, first run, build from source). SPEC section 12 describes the hosted jobs and `release.yml`. The Windows user path is gone from `docs/` and the spike scripts; the recorded logs in `spikes/results/` keep it.
- Verified here at e288173 on 2026-11-03. `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo deny check` and the MinGW win64 build of `pc-ffi` pass. `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool cargo mex"` builds both binaries, and `check-host-paths` finds no host path in `pckern.dll`. The whole `tests/` folder, MEX suites included, gives 295 tests: 291 passed, 0 failed and 4 skipped by assumption (the three `references/*.json` oracle tests and `BuildTest/wslRootElsewhereUsesToolboxBin`). `checkcode` gives 13 warnings on 54 files, none of them an error or a bug. Every workflow parses as YAML, every `uses:` is pinned by SHA, every checkout sets `persist-credentials: false`, and no `self-hosted` label is left.
- The self-hosted runner (id 21) was deleted from the repo on 2026-11-03, and the repo lists no runner. Its folder `C:\actions-runner` was deleted from the laptop on 2026-11-04, after a check found no runner service and no runner process. The recreated repo has never had a runner.
- First hosted run of `ci.yml` (run 41787381298, 2026-11-04, at 56aa95f): `rust` on all three runners and `pckern-win` passed, and `macos-jit.yml` passed. The three `matlab` legs failed, for three reasons, each fixed after the run:
  - Linux and macOS: `AotTest` failed because `mex` links with an export list that keeps only `mexFunction`, so the engine could not find `pc_aot_init`. `internal.aotBuild` now passes empty `LINKEXPORT` and `LINKEXPORTVER` there, and the build flags are part of the cache key. A `failure()` step in `ci.yml` lists the symbols of the AOT libraries if it fails again.
  - Linux and macOS: `PackageTest` refused every POSIX stage path, since `packageToolbox` only knew drive and UNC paths. It now treats a path that starts with "/" as absolute off Windows and refuses `\` in a segment, and on macOS it also compares the current folder with the stage as `cd` resolves it (`/var` is a link to `/private/var`). A new test covers the POSIX rules.
  - Windows: the install check started its example MATLAB with `matlab-batch` from inside the licensed MATLAB, and that MATLAB had no license. `cleanInstallCheck.m` now runs one step per `PHASECONT_INSTALL_STEP` (install, example, remove), and `ci.yml` and `release.yml` run each step with its own `run-command`.
  - Found while testing that fix here: an install made by a MATLAB started with `-batch` is not registered for later sessions, though its files stay in the Add-Ons folder.
