A later `-batch` MATLAB and a later `-r` MATLAB both list nothing. An install from a `-r` MATLAB is listed, and on the path, in the next session. The remove step therefore installs the same `.mltbx` again when the toolbox is not listed but its folder exists, and then uninstalls it. The three steps, each in its own `-batch` MATLAB, passed here on win64 (Hopf at I = 1.4187187 and 0.33128134), and the Add-Ons folder was empty afterwards.
- Second hosted run of `ci.yml` (run 41792490823, 2026-11-04, at ee109cc) passed on every job. The `matlab` legs gave 290 passed, 0 failed and 6 incomplete on ubuntu and macos, and 291 passed, 0 failed and 5 incomplete on windows. The incompletes are assumption filters, among them the three oracle tests whose `references/*.json` files are missing. `AotTest` passes on Linux and macOS, and the install check passed on all three runners with the same Hopf values. The `-batch` install was not registered for later sessions on any of the three runners either, so the reinstall in the remove step ran on each.
- `release.yml` dry runs on 2026-11-04. Run 41795127149 (ee109cc) failed in `lib-mac`: `check-host-paths` found `/Users/runner/` in `libpckern.dylib`. With the full string printed (baddf87), run 41796036606 showed that it is the dylib's install name, which the macOS linker sets to the absolute build path. `lib-mac` now links the cdylib with `cargo rustc` and passes `-install_name @rpath/libpckern.dylib` to the final link (21fc71e). The gateway opens the library by path, so the install name has no effect at run time.
- Run 41912379730 (21fc71e) passed every job except `release`, which runs on a tag only. `lib-linux` needs at most GLIBC_2.28 and the Linux MEX file at most GLIBC_2.14, and `check-host-paths` finds no host path in any library or MEX file. The `mex` legs gave the same test counts as run 41792490823. `package` built `PhaseCont-1.0.0.mltbx` (11.5 MB artifact), and the install, example and remove steps passed on windows, ubuntu and macos with Hopf 1 at I = 1.4187187 and Hopf 2 at I = 0.33128134, omega = 0.27550681.
- Tagged run 41925211997 (`v1.0.0` at b045897, the release now named 1.0.0-preview) passed every job, `release` included. The Release holds `PhaseCont-1.0.0.mltbx` (11516604 bytes) and `SHA256SUMS`, and `sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS` on the downloaded files gives OK.
- Not run yet: any test on a real Mac outside CI. The maca64 binaries carry the linker's ad hoc signature and are not signed or notarized by Apple. `scripts/check-adhoc-signature` checks that signature in the CI `matlab` job and the release `mex` job, and the README tells Mac users how to remove the quarantine attribute.

P5 (limit cycles, plan approved on 2026-10-31) was built on branch `phase5-limit-cycles` and is now on `main`. All 10 packages are done:

- 7bd793e: `cycle/basis.rs`, Gauss-Legendre nodes and weights by Newton on the Legendre polynomials, and the Lagrange basis on m + 1 base points.
- ad4e1ec: `pc-linalg` `condense.rs`, the condensation solver for the bordered collocation block system, with the monodromy matrix from the condensed blocks. rayon is a `pc-linalg` dependency and faer's `sparse-linalg` feature is on, both approved in the plan.
- f5b9a4f: `cycle/problem.rs`, `CycleProblem` and `periodic_orbit`. `Problem` gains `jacobian_len` so a problem can keep its Jacobian in its own storage. SPEC section 9 Test 3 passes on both evaluators (radius error 1.4e-14, period error 9.1e-14, |mu1 - 1| 3.3e-15, mu2 error 2.5e-15 against exp(-2 pi)), and Test 4 gives observed orders 3.96 to 3.99 (m = 2), 5.89 to 5.97 (m = 3) and 7.92 to 7.98 (m = 4). `cargo test`: 960 passed, 0 failed.

- 4551dd4: `cycle/continuation.rs`, `continue_cycle` from a Hopf point or a guessed orbit, with Floquet multipliers per point and LPC, PD and NS detection. `Problem` gains an `accept` hook that moves the phase reference to the last accepted orbit. On the Hopf normal form (N = 40, m = 4, alpha from 1e-4 to 0.5) the radius error is at most 1.4e-13 and |mu1 - 1| 6.8e-15. The Brusselator cycle at b = 2.5 closes under an independent RK4 integration over one period with a gap of 2.4e-10. `cargo test`: 965 passed, 0 failed.

Rulings in package 4:

- PALC metric. The profile unknowns enter PALC multiplied by 1/sqrt(N m), so the arclength approximates the L2 norm of the orbit (Doedel, Keller and Kernévez 1991). T and alpha are not scaled. The factor stays fixed for the whole run, also after mesh adaptation. Points and tangents are returned unscaled. A side effect is that tol_x on profile entries is looser by sqrt(N m); tol_f still applies in full.
- A Hopf start always continues away from the equilibrium, so `direction` < 0 is ignored with a warning.
- The trivial multiplier check warns when a point has no multiplier within 1e-6 of 1. The Hopf normal form cannot trigger it, since its rotation symmetry keeps the multiplier at 1 on any mesh; the test uses the Brusselator on a 3 by 2 mesh, where the trivial multiplier is 1.39 at b = 2.5 and moves to 0.99999 at 20 by 4.

- 0b64f3f: `cycle/mesh.rs`, mesh adaptation ported from AUTO-07p (EQDF, NEWMSH and INTERP, see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). The N + 1 mesh points move so that the jumps of the m-th derivative are equidistributed, and the orbit and the tangent are interpolated onto the new mesh. `Options::mesh_adapt` sets the number of accepted points between adaptations (default 3, 0 turns it off), and a failed corrector adapts at most once per point. Every `Point` carries its mesh. On the Brusselator at b = 3.5 with N = 20 and m = 4 the largest mesh-point error against RK4 drops from 3.8e-3 (uniform mesh) to 1.1e-5. `pc-cli licenses` now also copies the `NOTICE*` files of workspace crates, so `crates/pc-engine/NOTICE` reaches `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.txt`. `cargo test`: 970 passed, 0 failed; MATLAB 165 tests, 164 passed, 0 failed, 1 filtered by assumption.

Rulings in package 5:

- The plan said mesh adaptation would be written from the papers with no notice entry. SPEC section 7 names AUTO-07p for it, so `mesh.rs` is a port of AUTO's routines with a header comment, an entry in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and the BSD text in `crates/pc-engine/NOTICE`.
- When every m-th derivative is below 1e-7 the mesh stays, as in AUTO. A new mesh that is not strictly increasing or not finite is refused and the old one kept.
- The interpolated orbit is not an exact solution on the new mesh; the next corrector step fixes it, as in AUTO. The PALC scale factor stays as it was.

- eff901a: `CycleStart::Pd(PdStart)`, the switch to the period-doubled branch. The eigenfunction psi of the multiplier -1 is solved from the condensed system of the PD orbit, the orbit and psi (with -psi on the second half) are doubled onto [0, 1] and interpolated onto the new N and m, and the first orbit is corrected at h0 along that direction, halving it up to 4 times. On Rössler (a = b = 0.2, N = 40, m = 4) the run from an RK4 orbit at c = 2.5 finds one PD at c = 2.8324450, period 5.7698. The switched run (N = 60, m = 4) goes to c = 3 with no warnings, and there its period 11.5310741 matches the sum of two RK4 crossing times (6.0085632 + 5.5225106) to 1e-4, it closes under RK4 over the period with a gap of 6.8e-9, and the gap at half the period is 1.38. `mesh::interpolate` now takes the number of points per interval on the new mesh. `cargo test`: 973 passed, 0 failed.

Rulings in package 6:

- This test stands in for the Rössler oracle of SPEC section 9, as the plan says, until `references/rossler.json` exists.
- The PD start warns when the multiplier closest to -1 is more than 1e-6 away from it and refuses a complex one. Like a Hopf start, it ignores `direction` < 0 with a warning, because both directions give the same orbits half a period apart.
- The new mesh takes its N + 1 points evenly in the index of the doubled mesh, interpolated linearly between its points, so the adapted spacing of the PD orbit carries over to both halves.

- d18e718: the rayon threshold. `CondenseShape::parallel_pays` holds when the interval elimination work, N nm ni (ni + 2n + k + 1) multiply-adds, is at least `PARALLEL_WORK` = 2e7, and `CycleProblem` then condenses on the current rayon pool. The ignored test `timing_sequential_and_parallel` (`cargo test --release -p pc-linalg timing -- --ignored --nocapture`, 12 logical CPUs, m = 4) measured: on the default pool of 12 threads, parallel was 1.20 times faster at n = 10 and N = 320 and 1.47 times at N = 640, but 1.0 at n = 4 and N = 640, 0.55 at n = 2 and N = 640, and 0.02 at n = 2 and N = 20. Pools of 4 or 6 threads break even earlier (about N = 160 at n = 2), with at most 1.6 times. The parallel and sequential solves give the same bits, so the threshold changes only the time. `cargo test`: 974 passed, 0 failed.

Rulings in package 7:

- The threshold is set for the default pool, which has one thread per logical CPU, since that is what a MATLAB session gets. It is conservative for smaller pools.
- The plan's memory check is the fallible allocation already in place: the blocks, the condenser store and the sparse workspaces use `try_reserve` or `MemBuffer::try_new` and return `OutOfMemory` instead of aborting.

- 5711651: limit cycles in the run machinery, the C ABI and the CLI. `RunKind::Cycle` takes a `CycleStart`, and the problem JSON `{"type": "LC", "free", "ntst", "ncol", "start", "mesh"}` builds one. An LC row is `[alpha, u (n (N m + 1)), T, mesh (N + 1), mult_re (n), mult_im (n), LPC, PD, NS, h, newton_iters]`, so its stride is n (N m + 1) + N + 2n + 8, and the status JSON reports `kind` "LC", `ntst`, `ncol` and the labels LPC, PD and NS. The C ABI struct and `abi_version` are unchanged. CLI points and special points gain `eigenvalues` and `mesh`. On Rössler (a = b = 0.2, c = 2.5, free a) the CLI run from the Hopf point at a = 0.0278973 (N = 40, m = 4) finds a PD at a = 0.2261598 with period 5.6796 in 75 points, and a `pd` start built from that special point's CLI output begins the doubled branch at period 11.3592, twice the old one. `cargo test`: 984 passed, 0 failed; MATLAB 165 tests, 164 passed, 0 failed, 1 filtered by assumption.

Rulings in package 8:

- `start` is required for LC and has no default. `hopf` takes the equilibrium as `u0`; `orbit` and `pd` take the orbit at the base points followed by the period, and the CLI gives them no default `u0`. `mesh` is required for `pd` and refused for the other starts. `orbit` assumes a uniform mesh.
- For `pd` the old N comes from `mesh` and the old m from the length of `u0`, so the new `ntst` and `ncol` may differ from the PD orbit's. A wrong `u0` length keeps error code 2.
- `detect` is refused for LC, since LPC, PD and NS are always tested; `mesh_adapt` is refused for the other types.
- A problem whose stride would overflow `usize` is refused when it is parsed ("ntst: ntst times ncol is too large"). `stride` still maps an overflow to `usize::MAX` as a second guard, so the `count * stride` check in `pc_run_points` fails instead of wrapping.

- 9363fdf: limit cycles in the MATLAB API. `phasecont.continuation(m, start, Type = "LC", NTST = N, NCOL = m)` starts from a Hopf point (or any equilibrium point or vector, which the engine checks) or from a PD point of an earlier cycle branch. A cycle `Branch` has `Period`, `Mesh`, `NTST` and `NCOL`, keeps the multipliers in `Eigenvalues` and the LPC, PD and NS test values in `Tests`, and `cycle(i)` returns the orbit of point i as times and states over one period. `plot` draws the maximum and minimum of the chosen state, and `Plot = true` draws the maximum while the run goes and ends with the same lines. `tests/CycleTest.m` runs the Brusselator from its Hopf point at b = 2 (first period 2 pi within 1e-3, trivial multiplier within 1e-6 at every point, all points stable) and Rössler (a = b = 0.2, c = 2.5, free a) from its Hopf point to a PD point with a in (0.2, 0.25) and a multiplier within 1e-4 of -1, then the doubled branch from that point with its first period twice the old one within 1e-3. `cargo test`: 984 passed, 0 failed; MATLAB 173 tests, 172 passed, 0 failed, 1 filtered by assumption.

Rulings in package 9:

- `NTST` and `NCOL` are required for LC and have no default. Other types refuse them, and LC refuses `Detect` and `Switch`. `Var` works as for EP.
- A cycle point is a start only when its label is PD. Any other start goes to the engine as a Hopf start, and a state with no complex pair fails the run with `phasecont:engine:badProblem`.
- A cycle point is stable when every multiplier except the one closest to 1 has modulus below 1. A point with a NaN multiplier is not stable.
- The Rössler checks are self-consistent, since no oracle file exists (SPEC section 14).

- 8ea7bcc and d6f916a: package 10. `CycleTest/rosslerOracle` compares the first PD point of the Rössler run with `references/rossler.json` to 1e-6 relative and is skipped as "oracle missing" while that file does not exist; its header gives the file format. A temporary file with the engine's own PD value checked the comparison path once and was deleted. SPEC sections 7, 10 and 11 now describe the cycle problem as built: fixed N and m, the PALC scaling, the rayon threshold, the LPC test, the AUTO-07p mesh port, both starts, the LC problem and status JSON, the row layout and the MATLAB API. MATLAB 174 tests, 172 passed, 0 failed, 2 filtered by assumption (the oracle test and `BuildTest/wslRootElsewhereUsesToolboxBin`).

- Read-through of the whole branch. One finding, now fixed: a Hopf or PD start with a huge NTST that does not overflow (2^40, for example) made orbit-sized vectors before `CycleProblem::new` checked its largest allocation, and a failed infallible allocation aborts the process. Those vectors and the interpolation of the PD switch now use `try_reserve_exact`, so the start returns engine:badProblem with "not enough memory for a cycle of this size; use a smaller NTST or NCOL". A mesh adaptation that cannot allocate the new orbit keeps the old mesh. Test `starts_with_a_huge_mesh_give_an_error` uses NTST = 2^46, whose orbit needs more bytes than the address space holds, so it fails under any overcommit setting. On Linux with `vm.overcommit_memory = 1` (this WSL has it) a reservation larger than memory but inside the address space can still succeed, and the process is killed when it fills the pages; Windows refuses it at the commit limit.
- A second check of that fix found one more orbit-sized copy before the check: the PD switch cloned the doubled orbit into its setup. The orbit now moves into the setup and back out after `CycleProblem::new`, so no copy is made. No test can hit the narrow window where two orbits fit in memory and a third does not, so the existing PD tests cover the change.
- One MATLAB run on 2ea90fd, the first after a DLL rebuild, failed after about 35 seconds with an error that escaped the test runner (the stack ends in `JUnitXMLOutputPlugin.runSession` and `TestTask.runTests`). Only the last 15 lines of its log were kept, so the failing test is not known. A full `cargo test` was running in WSL at the same time. Four later runs passed with 172 passed and 0 failed, two of them with `cargo test` running alongside and one of those right after a DLL rebuild, so the failure has not been reproduced. Keep the full log of every MATLAB run until it shows up again.

P6 (LPC, PD and NS curves of limit cycles; SPEC section 8) was built on branch `phase6-cycle-curves` and is now on `main`.

- e100230: package 1. The condenser can keep its factorization (`factor`) and reuse it for a solve and for a solve with the transposed matrix. On random bordered block systems with k = 1 to 4, both match a dense LU to 1e-10, and parallel and sequential runs give the same bits.
- 505703c: package 2. `CycleProblem` takes a list of extra unknowns after T, and its condensed system has one border row per unknown after the profile: the phase row first, the rows a curve adds in between, the PALC row last. `Scaled` is generic over the problem and takes the profile length. The size of the boundary block is checked for overflow, which also covers audit item 4. All six P5 cycle continuation tests give bit-identical branches before and after the change (the full `Debug` output of every branch was compared).

- 0b27328: package 3. The LPC curve borders the (profile, T) Jacobian of the cycle problem, with its phase row, by one row and one column and continues its last solution component G. On the Bautin model `bautin_lpc.ode` the extended Jacobian matches central differences to about 1e-10 on both evaluators, and the curve matches the exact fold β1 = -β2²/4 to 4e-15 with 3 Newton iterations per point. c3b9cc2 records the updated gcc in the AOT reference.
- 766b909: package 4. The PD curve borders [D - T f_x; q(0) + q(1)] (DGK 2003) by one row over the profile and one column on the boundary rows. The bordered systems of LPC and PD now share one helper, `cycle/bordered.rs`, and the LPC tests give the same numbers as before. On the twisted model `pd_twist.ode` an RK4 monodromy confirms the multipliers -e^{Tλ1}, 1 and -e^{Tλ2} to 2.2e-9, the extended Jacobian matches central differences to 1.5e-10, and the curve matches a1 = a2² to 5.6e-15 with 3 Newton iterations per point. A start off the curve is first corrected at fixed a2. Each of two deliberate sign errors (the sign of G_x, the sign of q(1) in the boundary row) makes at least two PD tests fail.

- 5820ade: package 5. The NS curve writes the DGK condition v(0) - 2κ v(1) + v(2) = 0 on [0, 1] as a doubled state (v_a, v_b) with v_b(0) = v_a(1) and v_a(0) - 2κ v_a(1) + v_b(1) = 0, borders it by two rows over the doubled profile and two columns on the boundary rows, and continues G11 and G21. κ starts from the complex pair of multipliers closest to the unit circle. On the 4D model `ns4.ode` an RK4 monodromy confirms the multipliers to 1e-7, the extended Jacobian matches central differences within 1e-6 on both evaluators, on and off the curve, and the curve matches be = -c al to 7.3e-15 and κ = cos 2π(w2 + e al) to 8.0e-14, with 3 Newton iterations per point on both evaluators. A start off the curve is first corrected at fixed al. A start with no complex pair returns BadProblem. Each of three deliberate errors (the sign of G_κ, a dropped v_b half in G_x, a wrong v_b(1) coefficient in the boundary row) makes at least two NS tests fail.

Rulings in P6 package 5:

- The curve continues G11 and G21, as sign-off item 4 says. The warning for |κ| > 1, where a neutral saddle can give a spurious zero, comes with the codim-2 tests in package 7.
- The first borders are two fixed Weyl sequences, orthonormalised, followed by two steps of inverse iteration, since constant borders are not independent in a 2D span. Later borders are V_1, V_2 and the boundary parts of Y_1, Y_2, orthonormalised at each accepted point; a pair that is not independent to 1e-10 keeps the old borders.

- package 6. A curve starts from a special point of an LC branch through `CycleCurveStart::from_point`. On each analytic model an LC run in a1 at fixed a2 finds its point (the Bautin LPC at b1 = -1/4, the pd_twist PD at a1 = 0.04, the ns4 NS at be = -0.1, each to the 12 digits printed), and the curve from that point matches the exact curve to 5e-15 in a1 and 8.1e-14 in κ on both evaluators. Three special points of the wrong type return BadProblem: a PD curve from the Bautin LPC point, an LPC curve from the ns4 NS point, and an NS curve from the pd_twist PD point.

Rulings in P6 package 6:

- The engine's `Point` has no label, so the engine checks the type of a start through the start itself. The correction at fixed a2 returns BadProblem when it fails, hits a singular matrix or leaves the finite numbers, and the NS start returns BadProblem when the multipliers have no complex pair. Before this package a singular matrix at the start came back as `Singular`; the PD curve from the Bautin LPC point hit that case. The label check ("start an LPC curve from an LPC point") goes into the MATLAB layer in package 10, where the branch keeps its labels.

- package 7. Each curve has its own codim-2 tests, computed from the Floquet multipliers left after removing the trivial multiplier and the curve's own critical ones (`cycle/codim2.rs`). The LPC curve has LPPD = Re Π(μ+1) and LPNS = Re Π(μiμj - 1), with the complex-pair veto from the LC branch. The PD curve has LPPD = Re Π(μ-1) and PDNS, with the same veto. The NS curve has R1 = κ-1, R2 = κ+1, R3 = κ+½, R4 = κ, LPNS = Π(μ-1) and PDNS = Π(μ+1). The cycle branch tests now use the same helpers, and its numbers are unchanged. Unit tests on synthetic multiplier sets cover every label and the veto. On ns4 the NS curve from al = 0.06 to 0.5 finds R4 at al = 0.1 and R3 at al = 4/15, both to the 12 digits printed, on both evaluators, and LPNS and PDNS match e^{-4π al} - 1 and e^{-4π al} + 1 to 7.8e-14. On pd_twist the PD curve has LPPD = -e^{-T} - 1 to 4.1e-14 with PDNS undefined. The planar Bautin model has all LPC curve tests undefined. R1 and R2 are not exercised by a model, because ns4 is rotationally symmetric and κ only touches ±1. An NS curve run warns when points with |κ| > 1 lack one of the roots μ, 1/μ of μ² - 2κμ + 1, because such points are not neutral saddles. CPC, GPD, CH, R1 on the LPC curve and R2 on the PD curve were deferred to E7 (sign-off item 6) and are labeled since P13 E7b.

Rulings in P6 package 7:

- A curve removes its critical multipliers by nearest match: the two closest to 1 on an LPC curve, the ones closest to 1 and -1 on a PD curve, and the closest to 1 and to each root of μ² - 2κμ + 1 on an NS curve. A test over an empty rest set is NaN (undefined), and so is a pair product over fewer than two multipliers, so locate skips it.
- The neutral saddle warning counts a root as missing when the nearest remaining multiplier is more than 1e-6 away, relative to max(|root|, 1).

- package 8. The model tests of SPEC section 14 are in `tests/cycle_curve_models.rs`, with our own `morris_lecar.ode` (the type II set, I and phi first). `tests/common` has an RK4 shooting oracle that shares no code with the collocation: Newton on (u0, T, a1) with periodicity, the section f(u_ref)·(u0 - u_ref) and det[M - I, f(u0); f(u_ref)ᵀ, 0] = 0 for a fold or det(M + I) = 0 for a flip, with M from the variational equations and the exact f_x. The Morris-Lecar LPC curve in (I, phi) runs from the fold at phi = 0.04 (I = 88.2932505431) to phi = 0.06 with no warnings, ends on the fold that an LC run at phi = 0.06 finds (I = 91.2171895691, T = 101.4575045510), and matches the oracle in T to 7.4e-11 and in I to 8.9e-14 at its middle point and to 5.3e-12 and 1.9e-14 at its end. The Rössler PD curve in (c, a) from the PD at a = 0.2 (c = 2.8324450280) runs to a = 0.25 (c = 2.2814357594) and to a = 0.15 (c = 3.9157034124), ends on the PD points that LC runs at those values of a find, and matches the oracle in T to 5.4e-12 and in c to 2.0e-10. The LC runs and the curve ends agree within the 1e-6 of the test, and u0 matches the oracle within 1e-6 too. The file takes 3.5 s in release and 119 s in debug.

Rulings in P6 package 8:

- On an LPC curve the multiplier 1 is double and splits by about the square root of the discretization error, so the mesh warning of `floquet_warning` checks the mean of the two multipliers closest to 1 there. The LPC curve passes multiplicity 2 and the PD and NS curves pass 1. Before this, every Morris-Lecar point gave a false warning.
- Morris-Lecar uses NTST 160 and NCOL 4. At NTST 80 and 120 the mean of the double multiplier is more than 1e-6 from 1, which is real mesh error, and the warning is correct there.
- The `references/*.json` oracle tests of the plan go into `tests/CycleCurveTest.m` in package 10, skipped as "oracle missing", where the P5 oracle test also lives.

- a400496: package 9. `RunKind::CycleCurve` runs the three curves. The problem JSON takes the types "LPC", "PD" and "NS" with `free` [a1, a2], `ntst`, `ncol` and the `mesh` of the start orbit, and refuses `start`, `detect`, `switch` and `start_is_special`. A row is [a2, profile, T, a1, (κ), mesh, mult_re, mult_im, codim-2 tests, h, iters], with stride n(Nm+1)+N+2n+8 for LPC and PD and n(Nm+1)+N+2n+13 for NS, and the status names the curve's own codim-2 labels and its `ntst` and `ncol`. JSON round trips, the row layouts and a CLI run of each curve are tested.
- 78d4adc: package 10. `phasecont.continuation(m, pt, Type="LPC"|"PD"|"NS", Free=[a1 a2])` starts a curve from a matching special point of an LC branch. NTST and NCOL default to the grid of the start point, and other values regrid its orbit. `Branch` gains `Kappa` for NS curves, and `rows2branch` unpacks the curve rows. `tests/CycleCurveTest.m` runs the exact Bautin LPC curve on the start mesh and on another mesh, the Rössler PD curve, the Morris-Lecar LPC curve, the start and option errors, and the two `references/*.json` oracle tests, skipped as "oracle missing".
- Package 10 evidence. `scripts/matlab-win` with `checkcode` on the changed files and `Rows2BranchTest`: `checkcode` gives two messages, both on lines older than P6 (a suppression in `continuation.m` that is no longer needed and an `isscalar` hint in `Branch.m`); 12 of 13 tests passed, and `unknownFreeNameWithARealModel` errored because Smart App Control blocks the freshly built `pckern.mexw64` (MATLAB:mex:ErrInvalidMEXFile). The owner keeps Smart App Control on, so `CycleCurveTest` and every other suite that loads the MEX were not run locally and are not counted as passed. The settings of `CycleCurveTest` rest on `pc-cli` runs of the same problems: the Bautin LPC curve at NTST 40, NCOL 4 has 20 points, ends at b2 = 1.5 and matches the exact curve to 4.2e-15 in b1, 2.2e-14 relative in T and 2.7e-10 in r², with |μ - 1| at 8.9e-8; at NTST 20, NCOL 3 it ends at b2 = 1.1; the Rössler PD curve ends at a = 0.25, c = 2.281435759357531 and at a = 0.2, c = 2.8324450280634625; the Morris-Lecar LPC curve starts at I = 88.29325054302073 and ends at phi = 0.06, I = 91.21718956908252, T = 101.45750455220272. `bash checks.sh` (fmt, clippy, `cargo test` with 1030 passed and 0 failed, deny, Windows release build) and `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool cargo"` (exit 0) pass.
- package 11. SPEC section 8 describes the curves as built, section 10 the problem JSON, the status and the row layouts, and section 11 the MATLAB API.

- Read-through of the whole P6 branch. No defect needs a fix before acceptance. Low notes, parked: the `references/*.json` oracle files are missing; `Free(1)` of a curve is not checked against the parameter the LC branch varied; NTST has no upper bound other than the overflow and allocation checks; no test checks that mesh adaptation moves the mesh points.
- Audit of seven reported bugs, each checked against the code:
  - Items 1 and 2 (f685c3a): two entry names that map to the same C name, or a C name that is reserved, gave C that failed to compile or bound the wrong symbol. `emit_c` now returns `CNameError::Duplicate` or `CNameError::Reserved`. Reserved means a C23 keyword or `asm`, `main`, `linux`, `unix`, `i386`, a leading `_`, the `pc_` or `PC_` prefix, an `int*_t` or `uint*_t` name, or a `<stdint.h>` macro prefix. Other C library names such as `sin` are not checked. The product cannot reach either case, since `entry_symbol` fixes the names (`f`, `aux`, `jac_u`, `jac_p`, `d_*`).
  - Item 3 (f685c3a): the report called `pc_math` process-global. It is a static in each AOT library, and a test loads two libraries with different tables and checks that each keeps its own. The real issue was a write race when a library is initialized again while a worker reads `pc_math`; `pc_aot_init` now writes only when the table changes.
  - Item 4: covered by the boundary block overflow check of package 2 (505703c).
  - Item 5 (899b668): `Lu::new` scales the determinant so a finite nonsingular matrix cannot give 0, infinity or NaN.
  - Item 6 (abc719d): `eig_len` checks 2n for overflow.
  - Item 7a (c81271b): `run_copy` with `count == 0` now reaches the library, so a freed run id or a bad branch gives an error. `GatewayTest` has both cases; it loads the MEX, so it was not run locally (Smart App Control).
  - Item 7b (6bce632): the engine accepted a buffer longer than `count * stride`, so a gateway call with a wrong `stride` was not caught. On the owner's choice, `run_copy` now needs `buf_len` to equal `count * stride` and returns badArg otherwise. The table and `abi_version` are unchanged, and `pckern.h` and SPEC section 10 state the rule. `GatewayTest` has a stride + 1 and a stride - 1 case; it loads the MEX, so it was not run locally.

P6 is done except for formal acceptance. SPEC section 14 accepts it on the `references/*.json` oracles, which the owner produces with AUTO-07p or MatCont; until then the LC self-consistency checks and the RK4 shooting oracle of package 8 stand in. The MATLAB suites that load the MEX need a run on a machine without Smart App Control, or on CI.

P7b (trajectories, sessions, replay and the phase-plane app; SPEC sections 10 to 12 and 14) was built on branch `phase7b-sessions` and is now on `main`. The plan was approved on 2026-10-31. Every package is done in code. The acceptance criterion of SPEC section 14, that a session round trip reproduces a full Morris-Lecar diagram, holds in Rust: `crates/pc-cli/tests/session_ml.rs` builds the diagram, writes a session and replays it with `identical` true on all six runs.

Commits after the P6 work, oldest first:

- 6a63952: `pc-engine/src/ivp.rs`, a Dormand-Prince 5(4) integrator with FSAL and the step-size controller of Hairer, Nørsett and Wanner (SPEC section 15), with beta = 0.04. Tests check order 5 for the step and order 4 for the embedded estimate, exact solutions, backward runs and the stops.
- 545462c: the IVP run kind in the engine and in the problem JSON, `{"type":"IVP","tspan":[t0,t1],"options":{...}}`. Rows are `[t, x(1..n)]` with stride n + 1. The last stop is `End`.
- ed8e855: `model_info` reports `source_sha256` (an in-repo FIPS 180-4 implementation, checked against the NIST vectors) and `options` (the `@` options, lower-case names, last value wins).
- 52a8f54: the gateway command `model_rhs_batch` (n-by-k states), `Model.Options` and `Model.fromSource`.
- df20c4f: a run record on branches and points, so a session can store the exact problem string and the raw rows. The poll and copy loop moved into `phasecont.internal.runLoop`.
- 43de77d: `phasecont.simulate` and `phasecont.Trajectory`.
- e70de20: `pc-cli session replay`, the Morris-Lecar acceptance test, and the dev profile with `opt-level = 2`.
- dbce551: `phasecont.Session` with `save` and `load`, and `phasecont.session.replay`.
- d095978: `equilibrium()` no longer needs a free parameter (see the ruling below).
- 82c47c4: `phasecont.app.PhasePlane` and `AppTest` (33 tests).
- The docs commit adds SPEC sections 10 to 12 and 15, this entry, and the Morris-Lecar example (`toolbox/examples/morris_lecar.ode` and `morris_lecar_session_example.m`, with a test in `ExampleTest`).

Contracts. The C ABI is unchanged (`PcApiV1` has 17 members, 128 bytes, `abi_version` 1). The IVP problem type and its row layout, the two new `model_info` keys and the gateway command `model_rhs_batch` are JSON and gateway contracts, listed in SPEC section 10.

Rulings and choices:

- An IVP run refuses `free` ("an IVP run has no free parameter") and any unknown key in `options`. Other problem types refuse `tspan`. The defaults are rtol 1e-8, atol 1e-10, max_steps 100000, and no cap on the step.
- Behavior change. A plain Newton `equilibrium()` no longer needs a free parameter, so a model without parameters can run `phasecont.equilibrium`. Before d095978 it failed with "parameter index 0 is out of range". Continuation still needs a free parameter. The tests `equilibrium_does_not_need_a_free_parameter` (pc-engine) and `a_model_without_parameters_runs_equilibrium` (pc-ffi) pin the new rule, and SPEC section 10 states it.
- The dev profile builds the dependencies, `pc-linalg` and `pc-engine` at `opt-level = 2` (e70de20). A 300-step LC run in the test build drops from 18 s to 1 s. Release builds do not change. The small fixture `tests/+fixtures/ml_small.pcsession.json` is written by the Rust tests and read by `SessionFormatTest`.
- Session JSON v1 stores the problem and status as exact strings and every f64 array as row-major little-endian base64, so NaN, Inf and -0 survive. SPEC section 11 has the layout.
- Replay passes when the row counts, labels with indices and stop reasons match and no value differs by more than 1e-8 relative (floor of 1). `identical` (every bit the same) is reported next to it.
- The app integrates with Dormand-Prince only and says so when the model sets `meth`, `dt` or `bounds`. It passes `Hmax = |T|/500`. It never picks a T: without `@ total` a click raises `phasecont:app:missingTime`. A change of a parameter removes the trajectories and equilibria. Clear removes every item. A model that uses `t` can integrate but gets no equilibria, field or nullclines. Branches from a session are kept and saved again but not drawn.

Verification:

- `bash $SP/checks.sh` (fmt, clippy with `-D warnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo deny check`, Windows gnu release build of pc-ffi) ended with `all ok` and EXIT 0 on the tree of d095978 and 82c47c4.
- `AppTest` passed 33 of 33 on the owner's machine with `pckern` loaded. Smart App Control blocks that load on some runs, so a local MEX run can fail on the first load.
- The MEX suites ran on the owner's machine through `scripts/matlab-win` (runtests, full logs kept): `SessionFormatTest` 24, `SessionTest` 10, `TrajectoryTest` 20 and `ExampleTest` 2 passed (before the review fixes below) with 0 failed and 0 incomplete, and `ModelTest` 26 and `GatewayTest` 18 passed with 0 failed and 0 incomplete. `checkcode` reports nothing on the changed files.
- The first run of these suites found three bugs, all fixed: `Trajectory.plot` failed after its Model was deleted (`variableNames` now checks `isvalid`), the MATLAB Morris-Lecar EP run in `SessionTest` and the example used default step options and missed the Hopf point (they now pass `MaxSteps = 400, Hmax = 2, H0 = 0.5`, as the Rust test does), and the blow-up test asserted `t < 1` where explicit DP5 can land within 1e-6 of the pole (it now checks `|t - 1| < 1e-6`, as in Rust).
- A read-through of the whole branch found four robustness gaps, now fixed with tests. The base64 reader accepted whitespace, missing padding and nonzero trailing bits that the Rust reader refuses, so it now requires the canonical form. A replay stopped at the first run that could not be rerun, so it now gives that run `Pass = false`, warns with `phasecont:session:runFailed` and replays the rest, as `pc-cli` does. `Session.load` let a raw MATLAB error escape from a damaged record, so it now raises `phasecont:session:badFile` with the item number. `m.rhs` on an n-by-0 batch went down the single-point path, so it now returns `zeros(n, 0)`. `SessionFormatTest` (25), `SessionTest` (12) and `ModelTest` (27) passed with 0 failed and 0 incomplete after the change, and `checkcode` reports nothing new.

Open risks and questions from P7b:

- Replay across platforms is untested. libm differences between Windows, Linux and macOS can change step counts and fail a replay. The Rust replay on one machine is bit-exact, which is not evidence for another platform.
- The Morris-Lecar test uses a coarse mesh (NTST 40, NCOL 4), so the engine warns that the mesh is too coarse. The replay repeats the warning.
- A replay has no deadline and no step cap of its own. It trusts the options stored in the file, so a file with a huge `max_steps` can run for a long time. Cancel from the MATLAB prompt works.
- `Model.toOptions` turns `@` option names into field names with `matlab.lang.makeValidName`, so two names that differ only in characters MATLAB cannot use would collide. The names the app reads (`total`, `xp`, `yp`, `xlo`, `xhi`, `ylo`, `yhi`) are not affected.
- Not covered by P7b: events and Poincaré sections, dense output, continuation from the app, and nullclines for n > 2 beyond the slice view.

Engine fix: the first-point zero of codim-2 tests (branch `engine-first-point-zero`, from `main` at cce54e1; Rust only, no MATLAB API change, no C ABI change, no new dependency).

- `detect_inner` in `locate.rs` skips the warning for a codim-2 sign change at the first step when the search fails and the start value is at most `POLE_TOL` times the value at the second point. SPEC section 8a has the rule (first-point rule). It adds no label and drops none, and only a warning goes away. (Superseded on 2026-11-07, see the last bullet of this block.)
- Evidence: the PDNS test of `cyc_r2` at nu = 0.5 is -2.9e-14 at the first point, against 0.0998 at the second, so the run downwards warned "could not locate PDNS between points 0 and 1" and the run upwards did not (rounding fixes the sign). The R2 runs now start at nu = 0.5 and find R2 at nu = 0 and nu = 1 as before, and the nu = 0.45 workaround is gone.
- Four new unit tests in `locate.rs` (`codim2_tests`): an exact zero and a rounding-level zero at the first point give no label and no warning, the same rounding-level value after the first step still warns, and a zero 1e-3 of a step after the start that the search places is still a label. The first one fails with the rule switched off.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1570 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. MATLAB did not run.
- Not done: a noise threshold on codim-2 values in general, and a new `POLE_TOL`. Neither has a source in a reference.
- Superseded on 2026-11-07 (d4075f2), after the owner said "sure" to labeling the start: a test that is exactly zero at point 0, and not zero or NaN at point 1, gets its label at index 0, for the equilibrium and the codim-2 tests. A codim-2 value that is zero only up to rounding is labeled too when the search fails on the sign change. A run that switches onto a branch at a branch point still has no start label. SPEC section 8a (first-point rule) has the text. The `cyc_r2` runs start at a PDNS zero with no sign change in either direction, so they still give no start label and no warning. Changed tests: `locate.rs` unit tests, `crates/pc-engine/tests/locate.rs` and `cycle_normal_forms.rs`. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1593 passed) and `cargo deny check` pass.

P15, step 1 of E9a: characteristic roots of a linear delay equation (branch `dde-roots`, from `main` at 375d3c4, commits edc3358 to 5ee9362; Rust only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no MATLAB API change, no new dependency).

- Scope: `x' = A0 x + sum_j A_j x(t - tau_j)` with constant matrices and positive delays. `pc-engine` gets a `dde` module (`nodes.rs`, `roots.rs`) with `characteristic_roots`, and `pc-cli` gets `dde-roots problem.json`. SPEC section 8c has the formulation, the JSON layout, the exit codes and tests 1 to 9.
- Method: a pseudospectral matrix of order d(n+1) on Chebyshev extreme nodes of [-tau_max, 0] gives the candidates. Each candidate is refined by Newton on the bordered system [Delta(l) v; c^H v - 1], where Delta(l) = l I - A0 - sum A_j exp(-l tau_j), in a real embedding of order 2(d+1). A root is kept when the scaled residual is at most 1e-12 and Re l is at least `re_min`. A candidate that fails goes to `unrefined`. `n` and `re_min` are required keys with no default.
- Tests: 7 integration tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_roots.rs`, 5 unit tests in `nodes.rs`, 3 unit tests and 1 usage test in `pc-cli`, and 4 integration tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_roots.rs`. The root counts are checked against a winding-number count of det Delta on a rectangle (test code only), and they agree: 9 = 9 for the scalar test at n = 20 and n = 21, 4 = 4 and 6 = 6 for test 5, 18 = 18 for d = 2 at r = -2, 30 = 30 for d = 3 at r = -2.5. The largest residual is 6.8e-15. The CLI check x'(t) = -(pi/2) x(t-1) finds the roots at +-i pi/2 to 1e-12.
- Limit, recorded in SPEC section 8c and test 7b: the pseudospectral matrix resolves only modes with |Im l| below about n pi / tau_max. The strip Re l >= `re_min` holds roots up to a height that grows as `re_min` falls, so n must grow with it. A too-small n returns a genuine subset of the roots with small residuals and an empty `unrefined`, so nothing flags the missing roots. Measured on the d = 2 test system with delays 1.0 and 1.7: at `re_min` = -3, n = 40 gives 50 of 76 roots and n = 80 to 200 give 76. The user chooses n, and the winding count is the check.
- Source status (rule 9): the scalar form is from arXiv 2006.13810, which I read. Breda, Maset and Vermiglio 2005 was not read. The block generalization to a system with several delays, the bordered refinement and the dedupe tolerance 1e-11 are my own and are checked by the tests above, not by a reference.
- Checks at 5ee9362: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1590 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. MATLAB did not run.
- Not done: a MATLAB front end, steps 2 and later of E9a (stability of the nonlinear delay equation, continuation of steady states and Hopf points), and neutral delay equations. The code refuses a zero or negative delay.

Follow-ups of 2026-11-07 (owner answers: "sure", "pad", "push install and do everything"; no C ABI change, no change to the signature of a public MATLAB function, no new dependency).

- Padding of old session rows (74ed4ee). `rows2branch` reads the rows of a v1.0.0-preview LPC, PD or NS curve by their stride and fills the tests that are new since then with NaN (LPC: CPC, R1; PD: GPD, R2; NS: CH), so `Session.load` no longer fails with `phasecont:session:badFile` on those files. New tests: `Rows2BranchTest/oldCurveRowsArePaddedWithNaN` and `SessionTest/loadPadsTheRowsOfAVersion1CurveOfCycles`. Both ran in MATLAB and pass (29 of 29 in the two classes). `sessionCodec.compare` compares the stride, so replaying an old LPC, PD or NS record reports a structure change. That is expected and is not fixed.
- `Inf` Timeout through the JVM. `phasecont.internal.runBounded` starts the program through `java.lang.ProcessBuilder` for every `Timeout`, and `Inf` only turns the clock off. The wait loop pauses between checks, so Ctrl-C reaches it, and the cleanup stops the program and its children. On Windows a new lookup (`resolveProgram`) finds a `.cmd` or `.bat` wrapper through PATH and PATHEXT without a shell and gives Java the full path. Without a JVM, or when Java cannot start the program, the call falls back to `system`: silently for `Inf`, with `phasecont:latex:noTimeout` for a finite limit. `build`, `LatexStudio`, the README and SPEC section 13 (LaTeX companion bullet, compile driver) describe this. Tests: the new `tests/RunBoundedTest.m` (seven tests, one of them Windows only), and `fixtures.canBoundLatexmk` now runs `latexmk -v` through `runBounded`. `RunBoundedTest`, `LatexTest` and `LatexStudioTest` pass in MATLAB (67 of 67). The full suite (`scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"`, DLL restaged from the engine at d4075f2 first) ran 512 tests: 507 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption (three oracle files missing, two path rules for POSIX and WSL), and no test file was excluded. Ctrl-C itself is not tested: `matlab -batch` cannot send it, so the claim rests on the `pause` loop and needs one interactive check.
- JVM on other machines. No JVM install is needed on a machine that runs MATLAB desktop, since MATLAB ships its own. Only `matlab -nojvm` lacks one, and it takes the `system` fallback above. The VS Code extension does not use the JVM (the Rust server and TypeScript client).

E8b, internal slice: the adjoint of a HOM orbit and the inclination-flip tests (commits 86b3ba2, cf9f66f and 242dd63 on `main`, from a5c7f6c; Rust only, no label, row, stride, JSON key, C function or MATLAB change, no new dependency; pushed with 83b6261).

- SPEC section 8b-2 has the sources, which formulas are confirmed against the code and a second source, and the acceptance list. Every formula it marks confirmed rests on `homcont.f90`, the HomCont manual and the Utrecht slides. Champneys, Kuznetsov and Sandstede 1996 was not read.
- `pc-linalg` has `BasisTracker`, with one slot for the bases of A^T (the orbit rows) and one for the bases of A (the adjoint rows), each holding its own stable and unstable Beyn reference. AUTO keys this state by ITRANS, and a homoclinic orbit has one equilibrium. `HomProblem` reads its orbit-row bases from the first slot, so a HOM run is unchanged.
- `pc-engine` has `adjoint_residual` (collocation rows and the boundary rows of A for a given orbit, adjoint and gamma) and `inclination_flips` (IFS and IFU from right eigenvectors with AUTO's sign rule, NaN for a complex leading pair). No HOM run calls either one.
- Tests: 4 tracker tests in `pc-linalg`, 4 unit tests in `hom_adjoint.rs` and 6 integration tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/hom_adjoint.rs`. On the closed-form orbit of Sandstede's test 1 at T = 20 with 4 collocation points, the collocation residual of the exact adjoint is 2.8e-5 at 40 intervals and 1.0e-6 at 80, and the boundary rows are 2.3e-8. The two evaluators agree. A nonzero gamma, a constant phi_z and a shifted phi_x each raise the residual by more than a factor of 1000. At mu = 0.9, where A is not symmetric, the boundary rows vanish for a phi built orthogonal to the subspaces of A and not for one built orthogonal to those of A^T, so the adjoint rows use A. In the flip test at mu = 0, |IFS| and |IFU| are 5.4327, 5.6553 and 5.6568 for T = 10, 20 and 30, which tends to 4 sqrt 2 = 5.6569. It is a generic point of the curve, not a flip.

E8b, solve slice (df47eb1 on `main`; Rust only, no label, row, stride, JSON key, C function or MATLAB change, no new dependency; SPEC section 8b-3).

- `hom_adjoint.rs` has `solve_adjoint` (the collocation rows, the boundary rows of A and a normalization row, solved through the condenser with one border), `orient_adjoint` (the sign rule from the end vectors) and `inclination_flips`. No HOM run calls them.
- Tests: `solved_adjoint_matches_the_closed_form` (largest difference from the closed form 8.4e-5, 1.5e-6 and 4.0e-8, and gamma 3.3e-6, 2.4e-8 and 2.6e-10 at 20, 40 and 80 intervals), `solved_adjoint_satisfies_the_residual` (1e-10 at mu = 0.9), `solved_adjoint_is_deterministic`, and unit tests for the seed, the sign rule and bad input. `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` passed (2030 tests, exit 0) and `cargo deny check` passed.

P16, step 2 of E9a: `.ode` delays, DDE equilibria and their stability (commits a137e99 to 6c664a9 on `main`, from ed06b76; Rust only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency; pushed as a352bc4 on 2026-11-07, CI passed on every job).

- Scope: SPEC section 8d. A model may call `delay(x,tau)` for a state and a delay time that is a number or a `par`. `pc-ir` gets `lower_dde`, which turns each distinct (state, delay time) pair into a hidden parameter slot after the declared parameters and before the `t` slot. `IrModel` gets `n_user_params` and `delays`. `lower` and `build` still refuse every delay with `IrErrorKind::Delay`, so simulation, continuation, ensembles, code generation, the C ABI and the MATLAB API are unchanged. The only ways in are `lower_dde`, `build_dde` and `pc-cli dde-eq`.
- `pc-engine` has `build_dde`, `dde_equilibrium` (Newton on f(x, x, p) with the Jacobian JacU plus the hidden JacP columns), `dde_linearize` (A0 and the A_j at an equilibrium), `dde_stability`, `dde_residual` and the existing `characteristic_roots` from P15.
- `pc-cli dde-eq problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `x0`, `n` and `re_min`, all required, and prints `x`, `residual`, `delays`, `roots` and `unrefined`. Exit 2 is a bad problem file or a `BadProblem` engine error, exit 1 is a model error or a failed run. A successful run still prints warning ODE0054 on stderr, whose text now says that `delay` is read only by `pc-cli dde-eq` (7cfe302).
- Tests: the acceptance tests 1 to 10 of SPEC section 8d. They include the delayed logistic equation, where the roots at r tau = pi/2 include +-i pi/2, and 9 integration tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_eq.rs`. The test file had a race: parallel tests rewrote one shared model file, so a reader saw it half written. `write` now writes a temporary file and renames it (831050c). The file passed 30 runs in a row after the fix.
- Checks at 6c664a9: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1650 passed, 0 failed) and `cargo deny check` pass. MATLAB did not run, and none of this touches it.
- Not done: Hopf detection on a branch, continuation of a delay equation, normal forms, a MATLAB front end for `dde-roots` and `dde-eq`, and keys for the Newton options in the problem file (they are fixed at the `Options` defaults).

P17, step 3 of E9a: Hopf points along a branch of delay equilibria (commits 8e35627 to 4f8f5a7 on `main`, from a352bc4; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency; pushed on 2026-11-07 as 4f8f5a7, CI passed on every job with `asan` skipped as before).

- Scope: SPEC section 8e. `dde::continue_equilibrium` follows a branch of delay equilibria in one free parameter (a declared parameter or a delay time) with the existing predictor-corrector. At each point it counts N, the characteristic roots with positive real part, through `characteristic_roots`. Where N changes between two accepted points it bisects the interval and classifies the crossing as `H` when |Im| > 1e-8 and as a real crossing otherwise. A real crossing is explained by an LP or BP found at the same step, and without one it gives a warning. The refinement pass is not run and `detect_h` is forced off, because the root count replaces the eigenvalue test. A non-negative `re_min` is `BadProblem`, and a free delay time needs a positive lower bound.
- `pc-cli dde-branch problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `free`, `x0`, `n` and `re_min`, all required, and an optional `options` object with the keys of `cont`. It refuses `detect.h` and `mesh_adapt` with exit 2. It prints `stop`, `points` (`alpha`, `x`, `unstable`), `specials` (`label`, `alpha`, `x`, `omega`, `between`, `unstable_before`, `unstable_after`) and `warnings`. `omega` is null for LP and BP. Exit codes follow `dde-eq`: 2 for a bad problem file or `BadProblem`, 1 for a model error or a failed Newton start, and a run that ends on `StopReason::Error` exits 0 with the message on stderr, as `cont` does. `dde-eq` and `dde-branch` share one model loader (`load_dde`), and the behavior and error order of `dde-eq` did not change.
- Tests: the acceptance tests 1 to 10 of SPEC section 8e. `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_branch.rs` has 11 tests on both evaluators and `branch.rs` has 7 unit tests. The Hutchinson equation at tau = 1 has one H at r = pi/2 with omega = pi/2 (both to 1e-9, counts 0 then 2). The Hayes equation has its H at tau = 2 pi/(3 sqrt 3) with omega = sqrt 3. The sigmoid model has its H at a = 7.409583945668119. A fold gives an LP at mu = -0.0625, a pitchfork a BP at mu = 0.5, and one step over two crossings finds both. `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_branch.rs` has 8 integration tests, `ddebranch.rs` has 5 unit tests and `args.rs` one.
- Checks at 4f8f5a7: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1682 passed, 0 failed) and `cargo deny check` pass. MATLAB did not run, and none of this touches it.
- Not done: continuation of a delay equation along a cycle, normal forms at the Hopf point, a MATLAB front end for `dde-roots`, `dde-eq` and `dde-branch`, and keys for the Newton options in the `dde-eq` problem file. The omega threshold 1e-8 is a fixed constant (SPEC section 8e), which the owner may want as an option.

P18, step 4 of E9a: the Hopf curve of delay equilibria in two parameters (commits 876cb8c to dabbff5 on `main`, from 827538f; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).

- Scope: SPEC section 8f. `dde::continue_hopf` (exported as `dde_continue_hopf`) takes a Hopf point of a delay equation and continues it in two declared parameters, one of which may be a delay time. The unknowns are the equilibrium, the real and imaginary parts a and b of the null vector, the two parameters and kappa = omega^2, so the curve passes through omega = 0 without a break. The residual is exact and the Jacobian uses central differences. It labels `BT` (kappa changes sign) and `ZH` (det Delta(0) changes sign, defined for kappa > 0 only). The base system is the Hopf determining system of DDE-BIFTOOL (Sieber et al., arXiv 1406.7144, section 10.1, equation 14). The kappa form, the normalization rows and the labels are my own and are checked against closed forms only, as section 8f says.
- `pc-cli dde-hopf problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `free` (two distinct declared names), `x0` and `omega`, all required, and an optional `options` object with `h0`, `hmin`, `hmax`, `max_steps`, `tol_x`, `tol_f`, `max_newton`, `direction` and `bounds` (`bounds` applies to the second name in `free`). It exits 2 on `detect`, `mesh_adapt`, an unknown key, a missing key or a name twice in `free`. It prints `stop`, `points` (`alpha` as [first, second], `x`, `kappa`, `omega`, null where kappa is not above 0), `specials` (`label`, `alpha`, `x`, `kappa`, `index`) and `warnings`. Exit codes follow `dde-branch`.
- Tests: the acceptance tests 1 to 7 of SPEC section 8f. `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_hopf.rs` has 10 tests and `hopf.rs` has 3 unit tests. The Hutchinson curve r tau = pi/2, the Hayes curve, the Bogdanov-Takens model (one `BT` at (a, tau) = (-2, 1), x = 1/2) and the zero-Hopf model (one `ZH` at (a, tau) = (0, pi/2)) pass at the tolerances of the section. `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_hopf.rs` has 9 integration tests, `ddehopf.rs` has 6 unit tests and `args.rs` one.
- Checks at dabbff5: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1711 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. P18 does not touch MATLAB. After the ODE0054 text change (next bullet) I restaged the Windows DLL (`scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64`) and ran `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"`: 512 tests, 507 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption (the same counts as before P18), 224.69 s. CI on 7ef83ba (runs `ci` 42375422516 and `macos-jit` 42375422378) passed on every job, `asan` skipped as before.
- The text of warning ODE0054 said that only `pc-cli dde-eq` reads `delay`. It now names `dde-eq`, `dde-branch` and `dde-hopf` (pc-ode checker and diagnostic table, the two golden files, the language reference, SPEC section 2 and the xpp-probes README). The four Rust checks pass after the change (1711 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored).
- Not done: a root count along the curve (the pair on the axis is not checked to be the rightmost, so a curve can pass points where another pair matters), the normal forms at BT and ZH (the first Lyapunov coefficient at Hopf points is P19), continuation of a fold or a cycle of a delay equation, a MATLAB front end for the four `dde-*` commands, and an option for the omega threshold 1e-8. With a fixed vector c in the normalization, the null vector can turn on a long curve until c^T v reaches zero, which section 8f states as a limit.

P19, step 5 of E9a: the first Lyapunov coefficient of a Hopf point of a delay equilibrium (commits 98bbb89, 1b0e0bf and 49799ae on `main`, pushed; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).

- Scope: SPEC sections 8g and 8g-2. `dde::lyapunov` computes c1 and l1 at a Hopf point from the polynomial formula of Bosschaert, Janssens and Kuznetsov (arXiv 1903.08276 v1, which I checked against the paper text): Delta(z) = zI - sum of M_j e^{-z tau_j}, normalization Delta(i omega) q = 0, p Delta(i omega) = 0, p Delta'(i omega) q = 1, H2000 = Delta(2 i omega)^-1 B(phi,phi), H1100 = Delta(0)^-1 B(phi,phibar), and c1 = 1/2 p (B(phibar,H2000) + 2 B(phi,H1100) + C(phi,phi,phibar)). The last step, l1 = Re c1 / omega, is my own by analogy with the paper's l2. The convention that q has 2-norm 1 comes from the null-vector routine of DDE-BIFTOOL (formulas and that convention only, no code copied). A different scale of q changes the value of l1 but not its zeros.
- `pc-cli dde-lyap problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `x0` and `omega`, all required, and prints `omega`, `l1`, `c1`, `null_residual` and `equilibrium_residual`. It evaluates one point and does not follow a curve.
- `dde-hopf` has an optional boolean key `lyapunov`, default false. With true, every point gets an `l1` (null where it is not defined) and a sign change of l1 on the curve is labeled `GH`. With false or absent the output is byte-identical to P18 (a test checks that). `dde::continue_hopf` is unchanged, and `dde_continue_hopf_with_lyapunov` is the new entry point. The cost with the option on is several times a plain run.
- Oracle: Wright's equation with a cubic term, `u' = -a delay(u,1) (1+u) + b u^3`. The Hopf line is a = pi/2, omega = pi/2 for every b, and l1(b) = l1(0) + s b with s = 6 / (pi (1 + pi^2/4)) and l1(0) = -0.21413092244177392. The slope I derived by hand. The engine tests and the `dde-hopf` command tests check this line and the GH point. The `dde-lyap` command tests check the Wright value of SPEC section 8g, l1 = 2(2 - 3 pi) / (5 (4 + pi^2)), and the engine tests also check the FHN value of SPEC section 8g (0.398075831491134, published as 0.3980). The generalized Hopf point is at b = 0.38875. The tests also check that BT and ZH on their curves give no spurious GH.
- Checks at 49799ae: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1745 passed, 0 failed) and `cargo deny check` pass. P19 does not touch MATLAB. I restaged the Windows DLL (`scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64`) and ran `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"`: 512 tests, 507 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption, 180.5 s, the same counts as before P19. I did not rebuild `pckern.mexw64`, since P19 does not change the gateway. CI: 1b0e0bf passed on `ci` and `macos-jit`. 49799ae and fbf2997 failed on `ci` and `macos-jit`, only in the ZH case of `bt_and_zh_are_still_found_and_their_poles_give_no_gh` on Windows and macOS. The old ZH model had l1 equal to zero up to rounding, so the locator saw noise at a zero and labeled GH on some platforms. Commit 8ccc51b gives the ZH test model a term that makes l1 nonzero, and `ci` (run 42383055060) and `macos-jit` (run 42383055008) pass on every job. A test model for the l1 locator must have a nonzero l1.
- Owner items:
  - The constants in `lyap.rs` are my choices, with no source: `NULL_RESIDUAL_MAX` 1e-4 (the check that omega is a Hopf frequency), `PAIRING_MIN` 1e-8 (p Delta' q), `RESONANCE_MIN` 1e-8 (a root at 2 i omega) and `OMEGA_MIN` 1e-8.
  - `GH` is only a sign change of l1 in P19. P20 adds the second coefficient, so a GH special now carries `l2`.
  - l1 has poles at BT, ZH and at a 1:2 resonance. The locator drops a sign change through a pole with the existing `POLE_TOL` of 1e-2, which has no source. BT and ZH are tested. A 1:2 resonance on a curve is not.
  - `dde-lyap` and `dde-hopf` leave out parameters that keep the model value, as `dde-eq` and `dde-branch` do. The `lyapunov` key defaults to off, so it does not change a result.
- Not done: the second Lyapunov coefficient (P20), the normal forms at BT and ZH, a double Hopf label, the cycles of a delay equation, and a MATLAB front end for the five `dde-*` commands.

P20, step 6 of E9a: the second Lyapunov coefficient at a generalized Hopf point of a delay equilibrium (commits bf81e3a to 05eeb61 on `main`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).

- Scope: SPEC section 8h. `dde::lyapunov2` returns c1, l1, c2 and l2 at a Hopf point from the polynomial formulas of Bosschaert, Janssens and Kuznetsov (arXiv 1903.08276 v1, checked against the paper text). Delta(z) is the matrix of P19, the term list of c2 comes from my own enumeration of the set partitions, and H2100 comes from a bordered system with a second vector gamma, because Delta(i omega) is singular. l2 = Re c2 / omega with the same 2-norm of 1 for q. l2 means something only where l1 is zero. The function does not refuse a point with l1 not zero, and the caller reads l1 first.
- `pc-cli dde-lyap` has an optional boolean `l2`, default false. With true the output has the seven keys c1, c2, equilibrium_residual, l1, l2, null_residual and omega. Without it or with false the keys are those of P19. The tests compare absent with false and check the key set, not a fixed text, because `null_residual` is about 3e-17 and differs between platforms. `dde-hopf` with `lyapunov` true gives each GH special an `l2` at the located zero (not polished), or null where `lyapunov2` returns an error (a refusal is the usual case). Other specials and all points have no `l2`. `DdeHopfSpecial.l2` is `Option<f64>` and is `Some` only for GH. A non-boolean `l2` exits 2 with `"l2"` in the message.
- Oracles, from a numpy script written for section 8h: Wright's equation with a cubic and a quintic term, `u' = -a delay(u,1) (1+u) + b u^3 + c u^5`, at a = omega = pi/2 and b* = 0.3887604649348081 gives l2 = -0.0997996276332862 for c = 0 and c2 = 2.7272395029181444 - 6.505645604977195 i, l2 = 1.7362145915396248 for c = 1. FitzHugh-Nagumo at its generalized Hopf point on the Hopf curve gives l2 = -15.673336803892045 (published as -15.6733). A two-delay model gives -0.03292845151439567 for c = 0 and 2.437708203242944 for c = 1. The engine tests also check invariance under a change of coordinates, the scale and the phase of q, the pairing of p and q, and that the H2100 pairing without gamma equals gamma, which is not small at most of the test points.
- Checks at 05eeb61: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1770 passed, 0 failed) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB for P20, since it changes no MATLAB file, no C signature and no gateway. CI on 91faf3c (the push of all six commits, counting 91faf3c itself): `ci` and `macos-jit` passed, `asan` is skipped as before.
- Owner items:
  - The constants of `lyap.rs` that `lyapunov2` reuses are my choices with no source (`NULL_RESIDUAL_MAX`, `PAIRING_MIN`, `RESONANCE_MIN`, `OMEGA_MIN`), and the check on Delta(3 i omega) uses the same ratio as the one on Delta(2 i omega). The 2-norm of 1 for q is a convention, and l2 changes with it away from the zero of l1. The sign of l2 does not.
  - A point where Delta(0), Delta(2 i omega) or Delta(3 i omega) is singular is refused, so l2 is null on the GH special of a model with a root at 3 i omega. A 1:2 resonance on a curve is still untested, and so is the order of the three refusals when two of the three matrices are singular at once (a scalar block cannot give a purely imaginary root at 2 i omega for omega = pi/2, so I found no small model).
  - A model whose l1 is zero up to rounding along the whole curve can get GH labels from noise on some platforms (see P19). A test model for the locator must have a nonzero l1.
  - l2 is read at the located zero of l1, so its error follows the error of that zero (the tests of the GH special allow 1e-4).
- Not done: l3 and the normal forms at BT and ZH, a double Hopf label, the cycles of a delay equation, and a MATLAB front end for the five `dde-*` commands.

P21, step 7 of E9a: the critical normal-form coefficients at a zero-Hopf point of a delay equilibrium (commits 11cbef9, e599ad4, 7ce6703 and the review fixes f8fce48, a3c0286, f927c9a and b1a3bef on `main`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).

- Scope: SPEC section 8i. `dde::zero_hopf` takes a point with a simple root at 0 and a simple pair at +-i omega and returns g200, g110, g011 and the cubic g300, g111, g210, g021 from Bosschaert, Janssens and Kuznetsov (arXiv 1903.08276 v1, Sec. 6.2.1, checked against the paper text). The real pair (q0, p0) comes from Delta(0) and the complex pair (q1, p1) from Delta(i omega), with Delta(z) as in P19. The bordered solves follow the paper's Lemma 4, and H02000 is Delta(2 i omega)^-1 B(phi1, phi1). It also returns s_product = Re(g200 g011), s (its sign, 0 if exactly zero), theta = Re g110 / Re g200 and the quantity e of the paper. theta is null where Re g200 is zero, and e is null where it is not finite. The unfolding scenario, the parameter predictors and the location of ZH points are not computed.
- `pc-cli dde-zh problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `x0` and `omega`, all required, and prints the fifteen keys of SPEC section 8i in alphabetical order. A `None` prints as null. A bad problem file, a `BadProblem` and an omega that is not a zero-Hopf frequency exit 2, and a model error or any other engine error exits 1. It evaluates one point as given and does not polish it.
- Oracles: numpy scripts written for section 8i (not in the repo) compute every coefficient at two Hindmarsh-Rose points of the paper (r = 0.001 and r = 1.4) from the definitions, independent of the Rust code. The engine tests and the `dde-zh` tests match them to 1e-8 relative. A closed-form model, `x' = -(a+y) x - b delay(x,tau)`, `y' = a - y^2` at (a, tau, b) = (0, pi/2, 1), gives g011 = 0, s = 0, e = null and theta = 0.288400439142001. The tests also check the scaling of every coefficient under a change of the scale of q0 and q1 (a unit test), invariance under a change of coordinates, the ODE limit (no delay terms) against the homological equation, that each refusal names its matrix (Delta(0), Delta(i omega) or Delta(2 i omega)), and that a Jordan block at 0 is refused.
- Review: a three-lens review (correctness, tests, docs) of 11cbef9 to 7ce6703 found four real problems, and all four are fixed. The SPEC section 8i test 8 bullet named Delta(0) for point 1 with r = 0.01, but r factors out of the third row of Delta(0), so that case names Delta(i omega) (a3c0286), and b1a3bef adds a test for it that also checks the message does not name Delta(0). Warning ODE0054 and the places that copy its text did not list `dde-zh` (f8fce48). The `dde-hopf` help broke the antecedent of "it" in the l2 sentence, and the model path test of `dde-zh` could not tell the problem file's directory from the working directory (f927c9a). One more claim, that the delay-time check in `bad_inputs` is untested, did not hold when the check was removed on a copy.
- Checks at b1a3bef: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1797 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB for P21, since it changes no MATLAB file, no C signature and no gateway. CI on 698c97e, 70933bb and 3e4cf53 (the P20 follow-up commits): `ci` and `macos-jit` passed on all three. CI on 0399482 (the push of the P21 commits): `ci` (run 42399617744) and `macos-jit` (run 42399617741) passed.
- Owner items:
  - The paper does not state a sign rule for q0. g200, g110 and g011 match the paper only up to a sign. The code makes the first component of q0 with |q0_i| >= 1/(2 sqrt(n)) positive, which can flip for a vector whose components sit near that threshold. theta, e and s_product do not depend on the sign.
  - The 2-norm of 1 for q0 and q1 is a convention. s_product changes with the norm of q1 (by |d|^2) and e with the norm of q0 (by c^2), so only their signs and theta are scale free.
  - The constants of `lyap.rs` that `zero_hopf` reuses (`NULL_RESIDUAL_MAX`, `PAIRING_MIN`, `RESONANCE_MIN`, `OMEGA_MIN`) have no source, as for P19 and P20.
  - The order of the refusals when two of the three matrices are singular at the same time is untested.
  - `dde-zh` leaves out parameters that keep the model value, as the other `dde-*` commands do.
- Not done: the unfolding classification and the predictors at a ZH point, the location of ZH points along a Hopf or fold curve, the transcritical-Hopf normal form, l3, a double Hopf label, the cycles of a delay equation, and a MATLAB front end for the six `dde-*` commands.

P22, step 8 of E9a: the critical normal-form coefficients at a Hopf-Hopf point of a delay equilibrium (commits 36dd515, f83b5c6, 712c104, e56f9e4, 987a8da, e117395, 2093634 and 79fcd9a on `main`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::hopf_hopf` returns g2100, g1011, g1110 and g0021 for a pair (omega1, omega2) with omega1 > omega2, plus theta, delta, the product Re g2100 Re g0021 and its sign. The g values are monomial coefficients with |q1|_2 = |q2|_2 = 1. The g formulas are those of Section 6.3.1 of arXiv 1903.08276 v1. theta and delta are defined in Section 8.3 of the paper and its supplement S3.2 (the first versions of the module doc and the `dde-hh` help cited the wrong section, fixed in 987a8da, 2093634 and 79fcd9a). SPEC section 8j has the formulas, the refusals and the tests.
- `pc-cli dde-hh problem.json` takes `model`, `params`, `x0`, `omega1` and `omega2`, all required, and prints the thirteen keys of SPEC section 8j in alphabetical order (P35 added a fourteenth, `case`, see the end of this block). A `None` prints as null. A bad problem file, a `BadProblem` and a frequency that is not a root exit 2, and a model error or any other engine error exits 1. It evaluates one point as given and does not polish it. The frequencies must come in the order omega1 > omega2, and nothing is sorted.
- Oracles: numpy scripts written for section 8j (not in the repo) compute the four coefficients at the active-control-system point of the paper (zeta = -0.01622437043055295, tau = 5.898023025699427, omega1 = 7.6449266134492575, omega2 = 4.52748691549689) from the definitions. The engine and CLI tests match it to 1e-8 relative: theta = -1.7008825602708522, delta = -2.351720273599037, product = -0.016584567690847526, sign -1. A closed-form model of two oscillators coupled through their radii gives g = 2a, 2b, 2c, 2d. At (a, b, c, d) = (0.7, -0.4, 0.55, -0.9) it gives theta = 4/9, delta = 11/14, product -2.52, and with d = 0.9 it gives theta = -4/9, product 2.52, sign +1. With no delay term the four coefficients also match `normal::double_hopf_at`, the separate ODE routine (a unit test in `hopfhopf.rs`, SPEC 8j test 3). That routine returns Kuznetsov's G, so G2100 and G0021 are halved to get the monomial coefficients. The tests also check the scaling under a change of the scale of q1 and q2, invariance under a change of coordinates, each refusal naming its matrix, and the order of the checks.
- Review: a review of 36dd515 to e117395 found one real engine problem. A semisimple double root (two identical oscillators at omega1, so a null space of dimension 2) passed the pairing test, because Delta' is the identity there, and the function returned coefficients that depend on an arbitrary null vector. A test model with that structure returned `Ok` with all coefficients 0. 79fcd9a refuses it with `Singular` and the text "null space of dimension 2 or more", naming omega1 or omega2, from the ratio of the second smallest to the largest singular value of Delta(i omega_k) against `RESONANCE_MIN`. The review also found test gaps (a positive product, a non-zero `equilibrium_residual`, a one-sided `None`, the order of the per-root checks and a `params` value that overrides the model default), and all are now tests. The module doc and the `dde-hh` help also had a scale sentence that did not match SPEC (theta and delta are scale free, and only the g values and the product scale), and that is fixed. `classify` is now a function of its own, so the sign, theta and delta are unit tested, including `-0.0`.
- Checks at 79fcd9a: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1832 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB for P22, since it changes no MATLAB file, no C signature and no gateway.
- Owner items:
  - The coefficients of the normal form at BT are not computed. The formulas are not in arXiv 1903.08276 v1 or in the Bosschaert thesis. The Janssens 2010 thesis could not be retrieved, and Faria and Magalhaes 1995 is unread.
  - Partly superseded by P35 (below): `hopf_hopf` now names the Guckenheimer and Holmes case from the four real parts. The predictors and the Kuznetsov numbering are still not computed. The note that follows is from 2026-11-07, before P35. The unfolding cases of a Hopf-Hopf point and the predictors are not computed. A source search on 2026-11-07 did not confirm a complete case table, so nothing is implemented. What it found:
    - The "twelve cases" (Ia, Ib, II, III, IVa, IVb, V, VIa, VIb, VIIa, VIIb, VIII) are Guckenheimer and Holmes lettering (section 7.5). I have the table only through Du et al., arXiv 1804.03775, Table I, and the related papers by the same group copy it, so it is one source. The book is unread. Kuznetsov's book (2nd ed., read from a page scan of an unofficial copy) has 5 simple and 6 difficult cases and never says twelve. The 3rd edition main text is unread, and its 2025 errata change no case inequality. Which label set the engine should print is an owner decision.
    - Confirmed by two or more sources: theta = Re g1011 / Re g0021 and delta = Re g1110 / Re g2100 (the paper's "g1101" in section 8.3 is a typo for g1110); simple means (Re g2100)(Re g0021) > 0 and difficult means < 0; simple case I and III; difficult case VI. The simple case needs no fifth-order terms. The difficult cases I, II and VI do, through the sign of l1 along the Hopf curve, which needs S1 and S2.
    - Not confirmed: simple II, IV and V and difficult I to V (one source each, 2nd ed. only); the list for l1 > 0 (not printed anywhere I read); the reduction of the sign pattern p11 < 0 < p22 to the printed form; the l1 sign formula (the 2nd ed. and the 2025 errata disagree, and only my own numerical checks support the errata form); the ordering convention (Kuznetsov fixes theta >= delta, the paper's examples do not settle it, and a swap exchanges theta and delta); closed formulas for the fifth-order coefficients.
    - `hopf_hopf` already returns theta, delta, the product and its sign, which is all that is confirmed. A copy of Kuznetsov 3rd ed. section 8.6 or of Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5 would settle the table.
  - The nonresonance assumption k omega1 != l omega2 for k + l <= 5 is enforced only for the 1:1 and 1:2 cases, through the four second-order matrices.
  - The paper has a g1101/g1110 typo in Section 8.3 and in S3.2, a swapped omega1/omega2 labelling in its supplement, and says "theta delta > 1" in Section 8.3 but "theta delta > 0" in S3.2. The code follows the definitions, and the numpy oracle was built from them.
  - The g values are monomial coefficients, which is a convention. They agree with an independent ODE-limit route and with `double_hopf_at` after the halving.
  - The semisimple-root test reuses `RESONANCE_MIN`, which has no source. P23 below adds the same refusal to the first Lyapunov coefficient and to the zero-Hopf coefficients.
  - `NULL_RESIDUAL_MAX`, `PAIRING_MIN`, `RESONANCE_MIN` and `OMEGA_MIN` have no source, and the 2-norm of 1 for q1 and q2 is a convention.
  - The order of the `BadProblem` checks in section 8i differs a little from the list in the SPEC wording. Section 8j matches the code.
  - `dde-hh` leaves out parameters that keep the model value, as the other `dde-*` commands do.
- Not done: the unfolding classification and the predictors at a Hopf-Hopf point, the BT coefficients, the location of ZH and HH points along a curve, a Hopf-Hopf label on the curves of `dde-hopf`, the cycles of a delay equation, and a MATLAB front end for the seven `dde-*` commands.

P23, a follow-up to P19 to P22: the point functions refuse a semisimple multiple root (SPEC sections 8g, 8g-2, 8h, 8i and 8j; Rust only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no public MATLAB signature change, no new dependency).
- `lyap::pair`, which the first Lyapunov coefficient and the second one at a GH point both use, and `zerohopf::vectors` now test the null space of Delta before the pairing, as `hopf_hopf` already did. A null space of dimension 2 or more gives `Singular` with the text "null space of dimension 2 or more", naming `Delta(i omega)` or `Delta(0)`. The test is the ratio of the second smallest to the largest singular value against `RESONANCE_MIN`, and it is skipped for n = 1. Before this change a semisimple double root (two identical oscillators, so Delta' is the identity) passed the pairing test. The l1 routine returned `Ok` with c1 = 0 and l1 = 0, and l2 and the zero-Hopf coefficients failed later with the generic LU message "matrix is singular to working precision".
- Order of the refusals: in `pair`, the null residual (`BadProblem`), then the dimension, then the pairing. In `zerohopf::vectors`, the residual of Delta(0), the residual of Delta(i omega), then the dimension and the pairing at the root 0, then the same two at the root i omega. A Jordan block still fails the pairing test alone, with "not simple" and without the dimension text.
- Tests: two unit tests in `lyap.rs` (a double root and a Jordan block, both evaluators), one l2 test in `dde_lyap2.rs`, and three in `dde_zerohopf.rs` (a double zero root, a double Hopf root, and the order of the two roots with a Jordan block on one side and a double root on the other). Five of the six were red before the code change. The Jordan-block unit test was already green and guards the pairing path.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1838 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Owner items: the threshold is `RESONANCE_MIN`, which has no source. On a `dde-hopf` curve `l1_at` maps any error from `lyapunov` to NaN, so a point of a curve that sits on a double root gets l1 = NaN and the run goes on. No curve test passes through a double root. Whether a refusal inside a run should abort is a design choice for the owner.

P24, step 9 of E9a: the Hopf-Hopf label on a Hopf curve of delay equilibria (SPEC section 8k, commits dd3aadf, 0bd9f65, eac7550, 3bc0215 and 02827de on `main`, then 5a238e0 for the stiff-block fix below; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::continue_hopf_with` takes `HopfExtras { lyapunov, hh }`, and `continue_hopf` and `continue_hopf_with_lyapunov` call it. With `hh: Some(RootOptions { n, re_min })` the run computes the characteristic roots at every point, counts the roots within `CRIT_TOL (1+omega)` of i omega, and tracks the real part of the largest non-critical root with a positive imaginary part. A sign change of that real part is an `HH` special, and the special carries `omega2`. Each point carries `hh` (null without the option). The label list is BT, ZH, then GH and HH when asked for. Without the option the points, the specials and the warnings are bit for bit those of a run without it (test `off_equals_absent`).
