- The test is NaN for kappa <= 0, for sqrt(kappa) <= `OMEGA_MIN` (1e-8), when the linearization or the roots fail, when the critical count is not one, and when no non-critical root has a positive imaginary part. The `OMEGA_MIN` rule came from a failing test: the locator probes kappa near 1e-17 at a BT point, where nothing can be linearized, so the first version reported two lost evaluations on a run with no HH point. A floor decides only which of these NaNs count as lost: it is `COLLAPSE_MARGIN` (10) times the larger of sqrt(eps)*s and eps*s^2/`SHIFT`, with s the largest entry of the matrices (at least 1). At or below the floor a roots error or a critical count other than one is NaN and not counted, and a pair the roots do show is used. The first version of the floor skipped the test below it. Since s is the largest entry of all the matrices, a stiff block elsewhere (s = 1e4, floor about 22) hid every HH point without a warning. Test 12 of SPEC 8k covers that, and it failed on the first version. Near a BT point one rounding of a matrix entry turns the pair at +-i omega into two real roots, and the shifted LU of the root refinement goes singular for the same nearly defective pair. The floor is 2.2e-7 at s = 1 and 2.2e-5 at s = 10. SPEC 8k says so.
- Warnings: `hh_unrefined` counts evaluations where the roots had an unrefined entry, and `hh_lost` counts evaluations where the linearization failed, and those above the floor where the roots failed or the critical count was not one. The start check refuses a start with a critical count other than one (`BadProblem` naming `hh`) and returns a roots error unchanged.
- `pc-cli dde-hopf` takes `hh: {"n", "re_min"}`, both required, no defaults. A bad value exits 2 with "hh" in the message. Every point prints `hh`, and an HH special prints `omega2`.
- Oracles (numpy, 16 digits): the coupled model `x' = c y - a delay(x, 1)`, `y' = -d x - b delay(y, 1.5)` with c = 1 and d = 0.1 has its HH point on the curve in (a, b) at a = 1.454526952022688, b = 1.1844194631859732, omega1 = 1.5160279413134377, omega2 = 1.1308210684126256. The decoupled case c = d = 0 has the Hopf curve a = pi/2 and the HH point at b = pi/3 with omega2 = pi/3. Eleven engine tests in `dde_hopf_hh.rs` cover the pair selection on hand-made root sets, the roots at the oracle points, both oracle runs, the other direction (no HH, h negative everywhere), a good degree (no warning), BT and ZH runs unchanged, `off_equals_absent`, the run with `lyapunov`, the option errors, and the decoupled model with a stiff block `w' = -10000 w`. Two CLI integration tests and one reader unit test cover the key.
- Checks before the commits: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1853 passed, 0 failed, 3 ignored) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Owner items:
  - `CRIT_TOL` (1e-6) has no source, and the root-based test is my own choice, not taken from a paper.
  - A crossing can be missed when the second root is lost in `unrefined`, lies outside the window or the degree `n`, or when omega1 and omega2 are within `CRIT_TOL`. Only the largest non-critical real part is tracked, so a crossing while another pair stays positive is missed. A jump across zero is dropped by the locator.
  - `COLLAPSE_MARGIN` (10) has no source. The two terms of the floor come from my analysis of a nilpotent 2x2 block. A unit test checks them for s = 1, 10 and 1e3 with one rounding error of up to four units in one entry, and nothing larger or more spread out. An HH point closer to a BT point than the floor is detected only if the roots still show the pair at +-i omega.
  - When s is large the floor can cover the whole curve, and then `hh_lost` never fires along it: a lost pair is NaN and not counted. Test 12 therefore checks only `hh_unrefined` on the stiff model. Only the start check, which has no floor, still guards the pair. Whether the floor should use the entries of the block that holds the pair is a design choice for the owner.
  - The shifted LU in `refine` (`roots.rs`) goes singular for a nearly defective pair, for omega below about eps*s^2/`SHIFT`. A retry with a larger shift would shrink the undefined zone, but it changes SPEC 8c, so I left it for you.
  - If the corrector stops at a residual well above rounding, the zone where the pair is lost may be wider, roughly sqrt(residual). I did not measure it.
  - The label is untested at a resonance and at a double critical root.
  - The HH normal-form coefficients of P22 are not wired to the curve.
  - The cost is one root computation at every point and every locator trial.
  - The CLI reader repeats the engine checks on `n` and `re_min` so the message names `hh`.
- Not done: the unfolding classification and the predictors at a Hopf-Hopf point, the BT coefficients, the location of ZH points, the cycles of a delay equation, and a MATLAB front end for the seven `dde-*` commands.

P25, step 1 of E9b: periodic solutions of a delay equation, the corrector and the Hopf start (SPEC section 8l, commits a1c2d22, 33829e6, 1a0d7b0, 8986b69 and 7e64a2d on `main`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::correct_cycle` solves the boundary value problem of SPEC section 8l from an orbit guess: Gauss collocation on `ntst` intervals of degree `ncol` (2 to 7), the delayed arguments at the shifted points c - tau/T mod 1, `u(1) = u(0)`, and an integral phase condition against the start profile. The Jacobian is dense, and `MAX_DIM` (3000) caps the unknowns. `hopf_cycle` starts from an equilibrium, omega and a required `eps` (no default, no retries). The formulation is my own from the DDE-BIFTOOL manual (section 3.1.1) and arXiv 1406.7144, and no code was copied.
- Oracle: `x' = -delay(x,tau) + mu*x*(1 - x^2 - delay(x,tau)^2)` has x = sin t with T = 2 pi at tau = pi/2 + 2 pi K for every mu (`references/dde-cycle-oracle.json`). Newton takes 6 iterations for K = 0 and 7 for K = 1. From the Hopf point (N = 20, m = 4) the shifts are delta alpha / eps^2 = 0.1476 and delta T / eps^2 = 0.977.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle FILE` takes `model`, `params`, `free`, `ntst`, `ncol`, exactly one of `orbit` and `hopf`, and an optional `options` with only `max_newton`, `tol_x` and `tol_f` (any other key exits 2, because the corrector reads nothing else). It prints `alpha`, `period`, `iters`, `residual`, `ntst`, `ncol` and `profile`. Exit 2 is a bad problem and 1 is a failed run. It does not follow a branch, compute Floquet multipliers or label points.
- Tests: 8 engine tests (`crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle.rs`) and 11 command tests (`crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`). The command output matches `correct_cycle` and `hopf_cycle` bit for bit with the JIT evaluator.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (1879 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored, exit 0) and `cargo deny check` pass. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Warning ODE0054 and every copy of its text now name `dde-cycle` (pc-ode checker, diagnostic table, builtins comment, checker test, corpus golden, CLI golden, language reference, xpp-probes README, SPEC section 2 table). The legacy copy at SPEC section 2, line 535, still says `dde-eq` only.
- Not done (slice 3 of E9b): the LPC, PD and NS labels. The Floquet multipliers came in P26 and the continuation of cycles in P27, both below.
- Owner items:
  - The collocation, the phase row normalization (in `DdeCycleProblem`) and the Floquet discretization planned for slice 2 are my own formulation. The multiplier-count rule for slice 2 is not decided, and it will be an explicit key with no default.
  - The shifted-point derivative at a mesh node uses a floor convention (one-sided), and the half-integer shift with odd `ncol` (N = 10, m = 3) is covered by the Jacobian and residual tests only at the tolerances in the tables.
  - The tolerance tables in the tests are measured from the code, not derived. The bounds are 2.1 to 2.9 times the measured values, except the two N = 40, m = 4 bounds on |T - 2 pi|, which are 1e-12.
  - The dense Jacobian has no condensation or sparse path, so large `ntst` times `ncol` times n is capped by `MAX_DIM`.
  - `hopf_cycle` does not check that `x` is an equilibrium (the `dde-cycle` help says so).
  - `cycle_residual` and `cycle_jacobian` are new public Rust functions (Rust API only).
  - SPEC section 14, row P23, reads only "See `docs/STATUS.md`" (a stub I added).

P26, step 2a of E9b: Floquet multipliers of a delay cycle (SPEC section 8m, commits 909574f (SPEC), 6f34625 (engine), eaf5022 (CLI key), b503706 and 6cefdc7 (SPEC tables and the P26 row) and this STATUS entry; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::floquet_multipliers` stores the history on `[-tau_max, 0]` at `nhist + 1` Chebyshev extreme points, which turns the variational equation into an ODE `W' = G(t) W` of dimension `n (nhist + 1)`. It builds `Phi(T)` with Gauss collocation Runge-Kutta on the `ntst` mesh intervals of the orbit (one dense LU stage system of order `ncol n (nhist + 1)` per interval) and returns the `count` eigenvalues of largest modulus, plus `trivial_error`, the distance of the nearest multiplier to 1.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` takes an optional `floquet` object `{"nhist", "count"}` (both integers of at least 1, no default, off when the key is absent). It prints `multipliers` as `[re, im]` pairs and `trivial_error`, taken at the corrected value of the free parameter on the corrector's profile and period. A malformed object, a `count` above `n (nhist + 1)` and a stage system above 1000 unknowns exit 2 with a message that names `floquet`.
- Oracle: the cycle x = sin t of `references/dde-cycle-oracle.json` (T = 2 pi at tau = pi/2 + 2 pi K). For K = 0 and K = 1 the leading multipliers agree with the oracle rows to the bounds in SPEC section 8m (1.5e-10 to 1.2e-4, from N = 40, m = 4 and nhist 16 and 24), and `trivial_error` is below 1e-11. The error falls with `nhist` and with the mesh until it reaches `ERROR_FLOOR` (2e-7). A model with a block triangular variational equation matches its closed form multiplier exp(-0.1 T) to 1e-15.
- Tests: 1 unit test in `floquet.rs`, 7 engine tests and one ignored table printer (`crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_floquet.rs`), 2 unit tests in `ddecycle.rs` and 6 new command tests (17 in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`). The command output matches the engine bit for bit, from an orbit start and from a Hopf start.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1895 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored (the new ignored test is `print_tables`), exit 0, and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- SPEC section 8m tests 1 and 2 are `oracle_rows_and_trivial_multiplier`, test 3 is `error_falls_with_nhist_and_with_the_mesh`, test 4 is `count_is_a_prefix`, test 5 is `block_triangular_model_has_the_closed_form_multiplier`, test 6 is `literal_delay_agrees_with_a_parameter_delay`, test 7 is `both_evaluators_agree`, test 8 is `bad_input_is_bad_problem` and test 9 is the `floquet` tests of `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`.
- Owner items:
  - The discretization is my own derivation. I read no source for it, and the oracle, the closed form and the convergence tables are the only checks.
  - The oracle rows are numerical (about nine to ten digits, from a solver of my own), not closed form. The K = 0 bound on the leading error is close to the ten decimals of the oracle file.
  - The tolerance tables are measured from the code, not derived. `TRIVIAL_BOUND` is 1e-11, about 20 times the largest measured value (5.1e-13), because the quantity is at rounding level. That deviates from the 2 to 3 times rule used for the other tables.
  - `count` is an exact number with no default. A cut at `count` can split a conjugate pair.
  - The dense stage system is capped at 1000 unknowns (`MAX_STAGE`) and the corrector at `MAX_DIM` 3000, with no condensation or sparse path.
  - Multipliers of small modulus are not accurate in relative terms (the help text says so).
  - Opt-in keys default to off (the same precedent as the other `dde-*` keys).

P27, step 2b of E9b: continuation of periodic solutions of a delay equation (SPEC section 8n, commits 73344cf (SPEC), 45f542a (engine) and af28b89 (CLI key) and this STATUS entry; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::continue_cycle` (exported as `dde_continue_cycle`) runs the pseudo-arclength continuation of SPEC section 5 on the rows of SPEC section 8l, on a fixed mesh, with the phase row built again from each accepted profile. It first corrects the start by Newton's method at the fixed alpha of `setup.params[free]`, then follows the branch. Each point has `profile`, `period`, `alpha`, `h` and `newton_iters`. It reports no special points and no multipliers; P28 adds the LPC specials, and with `floquet` the multipliers and the PD and NS labels. `detect_*` and `mesh_adapt` are ignored. A delay-time `free` needs `bounds.0 > 0`, otherwise `BadProblem`.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` takes an optional boolean `branch`, off when absent. With `branch: true` it continues the orbit of `orbit` or `hopf`. An `orbit` start goes straight to the continuation at the given alpha (no prior corrector, because that one leaves alpha free and would move it). A `hopf` start runs `hopf_cycle` first and continues from that cycle at its alpha, with one `options` object feeding both. `options` goes through the reader of the `cont` problem files, and `detect` and `mesh_adapt` are refused by name. `branch` with `floquet` was exit 2 here and is allowed since P28.
- Oracle: the cycle x = sin t of `references/dde-cycle-oracle.json` at tau = pi/2 + 2 pi K. Test 1 follows the exact family along mu (K = 0 and 1, both directions) to 5e-11 in the period and 1.4e-7 in the profile. Test 2 follows the Hopf branch at mu = 0.3 from the eps = 0.05 cycle to the exact orbit at tau = pi/2 (period to 5e-11, extremes to 1e-12). Test 3 runs it backwards to the Hopf cycle (1e-12 and 3e-12).
- Tests: 10 engine tests and one ignored table printer in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_branch.rs`, 3 new unit tests in `ddecycle.rs` and 6 new command tests (23 in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`). The command output matches the engine bit for bit, from an orbit start and from a Hopf start. Every point is a solution (residual at most 1e-9, and `correct_cycle` from the point returns the same period in at most 3 iterations).
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1914 passed, 0 failed, 6 ignored, exit 0, and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. CI on 3e86193 (P26) passed on `ci` and `macos-jit`. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed. CI on 09ec3ec passed on `ci` (run 42424506835) and `macos-jit`, so the 1e-12 extreme-value bounds held on both platforms.
- Owner items:
  - The formulation is my own. I read no source on how DDE-BIFTOOL continues periodic orbits.
  - The mesh is fixed. There is no adaptation and no estimate of the discretization error along the branch, and a steep part is resolved only as well as the start mesh allows.
  - The phase reference moves to each accepted profile, and the tangent of a new point uses the reference of the previous point's corrector.
  - Branch points of the discretized problem are not detected, and a run may follow either branch there.
  - The Hopf-to-exact oracle (test 2) comes from a scratch calculation of my own, not kept in the repository. Its period agrees with `hopf_cycle` to 4.7e-14, so it is not an independent check at the 1e-12 level.
  - The extreme-value bounds of test 2 (1e-12) are about 3 times the measured value and may need widening on another CI platform.
  - The dense Jacobian costs O(dim^3) per Newton step. A run near the limit `MAX_DIM` 3000 with hundreds of points is untimed.
  - `branch` is opt-in and off by default. `detect` and `mesh_adapt` with `branch` are refused by name. The refusal of `branch` with `floquet` was lifted in P28.
  - With a Hopf start one `options` object feeds the corrector and the continuation, so `max_newton` applies to both.

P28, step 3 of E9b: LPC, PD and NS labels along a branch of delay cycles (SPEC section 8o, commits 0390025 (SPEC), 6f40814 (engine and tests) and 8db3dca (CLI) and this STATUS entry; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::continue_cycle_floquet` (exported as `dde_continue_cycle_floquet`) is `continue_cycle` with optional Floquet options. Without them it reports the fold only. With them each point carries `multipliers` and `trivial_error`, the monodromy matrix of P26 goes to the engine through `Problem::state_jacobian`, and the PD and NS tests use its eigenvalues. The ODE conventions are copied: LPC is the last component of the tangent (the alpha component), PD is the shifted product of the nontrivial multipliers at 1, and NS is the pair product, labeled only where the nearest pair is complex. Without multipliers the tests are `[lpc, NaN, NaN]`. `DdeCycleBranch` gains `specials` (label, index, point) and `DdeCyclePoint` gains `multipliers` and `trivial_error`. `continue_cycle` calls the new function with `None`, so its points are unchanged.
- Floquet options are checked before the run, and a bad option is `BadProblem` with a message that starts with `floquet: `. An error from `state_jacobian` would abort a run, so nothing is left to fail late.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` accepts `floquet` together with `branch`. Every run with `branch` prints `specials` (`label`, `index`, `alpha`, `period`, `profile`). With `floquet` the points and the specials also carry `multipliers` and `trivial_error`. A run without `branch` is unchanged. `detect` and `mesh_adapt` with `branch` stay exit 2. A bad `floquet` is exit 2 and the message names `floquet`.
- Oracles are closed forms of my own, with the cycle x = sin t and T = 2 pi at tau = pi/2. NS: the oscillator plus a second state `y' = -s y(t - 1)`, with the NS at s = pi/2 where theta = 4 pi - pi^2. PD: the same oscillator plus two states that turn with the cycle, with a multiplier -1 at s = sqrt(0.34) for gamma = 0.3. LPC: `x' = -x(t - tau) + x (a - (x^2 + x(t - tau)^2 - 1)^2)`, folds at a = 0 with rho^2 = 1 plus or minus sqrt(a). Measured in release at N = 20, m = 4: NS alpha error 8.9e-16, PD alpha error 3.3e-12, LPC alpha 5.8e-12, rho^2 - 1 3.8e-11 and T - 2 pi 2.3e-11. The JIT and fallback evaluators agree on alpha exactly. The bounds are in the test file and in the SPEC 8o tables.
- Tests: 7 engine tests and one ignored table printer in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_labels.rs`, 5 new command tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs` (28 in the file when P28 was pushed, one P27 case removed from the exit-2 table; the review fixes below add 3). The command output matches the engine bit for bit, including the multipliers and `trivial_error` of every point and special.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1926 passed, 0 failed, 7 ignored, exit 0 (117 `test result` lines), and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed. ODE0054 and its copies only name `dde-cycle`, so no copy needs a change.
- Owner items:
  - Spurious multipliers are tiny (below about 1.5e-3), measured on one model only and not proven. The infinite-product convergence behind the PD and NS products is not proven either.
  - The closed-form PD and NS oracles are my own constructions (the PD one through a rotating frame).
  - Phi(T) is computed at every point and at every locator trial. The cost is untimed.
  - The cancel flag inside `state_jacobian` is never set, so cancellation only takes effect between steps.
  - `DdeCyclePoint` and `DdeCycleBranch` changed (Rust-only API). `DdeCyclePoint` derives `PartialEq`, but `trivial_error` is NaN without floquet, so `==` on such points is false.
  - An empty eigenvalue list (a failed eigenvalue computation) gives empty multipliers, a NaN `trivial_error` (printed as `null`) and a warning.
  - LPC specials now appear on every `branch` run, so the P27 output gains a `specials` list. The points are unchanged.
  - The no-delay check in `check_floquet` cannot be reached through `build_dde`, which refuses a model without a delay. The test reaches it by clearing `model.delays`.
  - A bad `floquet` with a Hopf start is refused before the Hopf corrector runs (`check_cycle_floquet`, see the review fixes below).
  - The `floquet: ` prefix is added in the engine for `continue_cycle_floquet`, while the CLI adds it for `floquet_multipliers`.

P28 review fixes (SPEC section 8o, commits 8c82697, 943750d and b185355; Rust and CLI only, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency). CI passed on b185355 (`ci` and `macos-jit`).
- `pair_product` rescales by exact powers of two (2^500, with the exponent tracked), so a product that overflows in the plain loop saturates to a signed infinity instead of NaN. The result has the plain loop's bits whenever the plain loop neither overflows nor underflows into subnormals. The locator skips a test whose value is infinite at either end of a step, on every run kind, and warns with the slot name (`<name> is not finite between points k and k+1; the test was skipped there`). A test that stays infinite warns at every step. NaN stays a quiet skip (SPEC section 5, item 5).
- `pc_engine::dde::check_cycle_floquet` (exported as `dde_check_cycle_floquet`) runs the checks on the floquet options alone. `pc-cli dde-cycle` calls it before the Hopf corrector, so a bad `floquet` is exit 2 also when the start would not converge. A new Rust function, not a C ABI or MATLAB API change.
- `DdeCycleSpecial.index` is documented as the first point at or after the special, and the SPEC says that `h` is the part of the step up to the special.
- Tests: the engine comparisons use `to_bits`, so NaN and the sign of zero count. `ns_point_at_pi_over_two` asserts the label list is exactly `NS`. The CLI file gains `a_branch_prints_the_ns_point_bit_for_bit`, `a_branch_prints_the_pd_point_bit_for_bit` and `a_bad_floquet_with_a_hopf_start_is_exit_2_before_the_run` (31 in the file). The last one was checked by removing the pre-check, which gave exit 1. Two `pair_product` unit tests and one locator test (`an_infinite_test_value_is_skipped_with_a_warning`) are new.
- The SPEC tolerance wording said about 2 to 3 times the measured values. The ratios are 2.6 to 3.7, so it now says at most about 4 times.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1933 passed, 0 failed, 7 ignored, exit 0 (117 `test result` lines), `cargo deny check` ok. MATLAB not re-run: no MATLAB test reaches an infinite test value, while the code ships in the DLL.
- Owner items: the new public Rust function `check_cycle_floquet`; the saturation of `pair_product` changes a result only where the plain loop overflows or underflows, and the locator warning applies to any infinite test value on any run kind (ODE curves included), at every step where it stays infinite; no test run reaches either; the `.vsix` of 6cf9b46 is installed in the WSL VS Code, but the diagnostics, hover and completion UI were not checked there; the P28 tolerance bounds are up to about 3.7 times the measured values.

P29, step 4 of E9b: mesh adaptation for cycles of a delay equation (SPEC section 8p, commits ccf2d7f (SPEC), 6a27b2d (engine and tests), f5dbfad (CLI and tests) and 84b6898 (measured tables), STATUS 8a7d32b, and 8033c38 for the last test; pushed, CI green on 8033c38: `ci` and `macos-jit`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::continue_cycle_adapt` (exported as `dde_continue_cycle_adapt`) is `continue_cycle_floquet` with a moving mesh. `DdeMeshAdapt { every }` sets how many accepted points pass between adaptations, and a failed corrector adapts once per point. The mesh comes from `cycle/mesh.rs` (the AUTO-07p port, already listed in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`) applied to the profile. The collocation rows, the delayed lookups and the monodromy stage steps use the interval widths of the mesh of the point. On the uniform mesh the old formulas run and give the same bits. `DdeCyclePoint` gains `mesh`, empty without adapt, and the first point of an adapted run has the uniform mesh.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` takes `adapt: {"every": k}` with `branch: true`. `every` is required and has no default, and `adapt` is off when absent. With it each point and special prints `mesh`. `options.mesh_adapt` is still refused, and the message now names `adapt`. A bad `adapt` is exit 2 before a Hopf start is corrected.
- Oracles: the manufactured model STEEP (exact orbit `x = tanh(20 cos t)`, `y = (cos t, sin t)`, steep parts of width about 1/20) and the smooth oscillator of P27. Measured in release at N = 20, m = 4: the last-point error of STEEP falls from 7.8e-2 (uniform mesh) to 8.5e-3, the period stays within 2.5e-8 of 2 pi, the second multiplier stays within 1.4e-9 of the closed form, and `trivial_error` stays at or below 1.5e-14. On the smooth orbit the adapted mesh is about 4 times less accurate than the uniform one (2.2e-7 against 5.3e-8). The bounds are in the test file and in the SPEC 8p tables.
- Tests: 6 engine tests and one ignored table printer in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_adapt.rs`, 7 unit tests in `dde/cycle.rs` (4) and `dde/floquet.rs` (3), 2 unit tests for the key reader in `crates/pc-cli/src/ddecycle.rs` and 9 command tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs` (the STEEP problem of test 1 among them, without and with `floquet`). The command output matches the engine bit for bit, meshes included. A mutation check (adaptation switched off) made test 1 fail.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1956 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored, exit 0, and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. The output of `pc-cli dde-cycle` for three problems without `adapt` (the mu-line with `branch`, the same with `floquet`, and STEEP with `branch` and `floquet`) was saved before the change and is byte-identical after it, in the debug build on this machine. That is a one-time check and not a test. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Owner items:
  - New public Rust items: `DdeMeshAdapt`, `continue_cycle_adapt` and the `mesh` field of `DdeCyclePoint`.
  - The start mesh is always uniform. There is no restart from an adapted mesh, and `floquet_multipliers` and `monodromy` of a single point stay on the uniform mesh.
  - The mesh monitor is the ODE one and ignores the delayed arguments. I read no source on how DDE-BIFTOOL or AUTO choose mesh points for a delay problem.
  - The collocation row `du/h_j - T f` on a non-uniform mesh and the non-uniform stage steps are my own. So is the STEEP orbit.
  - On a smooth orbit the adapted mesh loses accuracy (about 4 times) and I have not found the cause.
  - A failed corrector adapts the mesh even with a huge `every`, as the ODE cycles do. The cost of the monitor on a long run is untimed.
  - `adapt` needs `ntst` of at least 2, and the CLI key is my own.

P30, step 5 of E9b: branch points of cycles of a delay equation (SPEC section 8q, commits 0ece99d and 4a3bd9b (SPEC), d2a9d73 (`Lu::min_pivot`), 5c7cd4f (`bp_label` hook), bdae05e (engine and tests), 7eff9b9 (CLI and tests), 9900871 and 94678e7 (measured values); pushed, CI green on 94678e7: `ci` and `macos-jit`; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::continue_cycle_bpc` (exported as `dde_continue_cycle_bpc`) is `continue_cycle_floquet` or `continue_cycle_adapt` with the BPC test on. The test value is `sign(det B_k) * min_i |u_ii|` of the bordered LU, since the determinant of a matrix of dimension up to 3000 saturates and the sign test of the locator then fails. A sign change between two accepted points is located as in SPEC section 5 and reported as a special with the label `BPC`. The corrector never reads the test value, so the points of a run are the same bit for bit with and without it.
- Pieces: `Lu::min_pivot` in `pc-linalg`, `Problem::bp_label` (default `Some("BP")` where `ep_tests()` is true, so every existing problem keeps its label and slot values), `dense_bordered_lu` shared by the default and the cycle `bordered_solve`, and a `RunKeys` struct in `dde/cycle.rs` that replaced three extra arguments of `continue_cycle_on`.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` takes `bpc: true` with `branch: true`, also with `floquet`, `adapt` and both. It is off when absent. A `bpc` that is not a boolean and `bpc: true` without `branch` are exit 2 naming `bpc`. `bpc: false` without `branch` is accepted and does nothing.
- Oracle: ZBLK (`x' = -x(t-tau) + mu x (1 - x^2 - x(t-tau)^2)`, `z' = (mu - m0) z - z^3`), whose exact orbit `x = sin t`, `z = 0` has a branch point of the discretized problem at `mu = m0`. Measured in the debug build: the error of `alpha` is 6.6e-15 or less on uniform meshes (both directions, off-grid start, two step sizes, `floquet`, N = 100, a nonlinear block, the fallback evaluator) and 7.4e-11 with `adapt`. The sequence `BPC`, `LPC`, `BPC` is found on a cubic model with a decoupled block, and no `BPC` appears at a fold, a PD point or an NS point. The tables are in SPEC 8q.
- Tests: 11 engine tests and one ignored table printer in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_bpc.rs`, unit tests in `pc-linalg` (`min_pivot`), `problem.rs` (2) and `dde/cycle.rs` (2), 1 unit test for the key reader in `crates/pc-cli/src/ddecycle.rs` and 7 command tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`. Five mutation checks of the engine code and two of the CLI code (one of them removes the `bpc` without `branch` error) each made a test fail.
- Checks on 94678e7: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean (also on each earlier commit of the step), `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 1981 passed, 0 failed, 9 ignored, exit 0, and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Owner items:
  - New public Rust items: `continue_cycle_bpc`, `Lu::min_pivot` and `Problem::bp_label`. All are Rust only.
  - The scaled test `sign(det) * min pivot` is my own choice. I read no source on how DDE-BIFTOOL or AUTO-07p detect branch points of cycles.
  - A branch point where an even number of branches cross is missed, and test 8 pins this. Switching at a `BPC` came with P31. The label is not checked against a second multiplier at +1. ODE cycles have no `BPC`.
  - The location bounds (1e-13, and 3e-10 with `adapt`) come from decoupled-block models on one machine. They also hold in the `macos-jit` job, where the measured values are not recorded. A branch point from a coupled mode is not measured.
  - That the Gauss collocation matrix of a scalar linear periodic problem is singular only at a = 0 is not proven for every m. The tests use m = 4 and m = 6.
  - The CLI bounds (1e-11, and 3e-10 with `adapt`) and the JIT against fallback bound (1e-9) are looser than the measured values.

P31, step 6 of E9b: branch switching at a `BPC` of a delay cycle (SPEC section 8r, commits 95d8943 and 4c69166 (SPEC), 57c837b (`svd_null`), 917898e (sign tolerance argument of `branching_root`), 54b6cc6 (engine and tests), 1d1a7d4, a4c85b8 and 351bff3 (CLI and tests); Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::switch_cycle_bpc` (exported as `dde_switch_cycle_bpc`) takes a `BPC` special of a branch from `continue_cycle_bpc` and returns the start and the unit tangent of the other branch of the discretized problem. `continue_cycle_switch` runs from there with `direction` 1 along the tangent or -1 against it. The start is not corrected, and the `BPC` at the start is not labeled again.
- Method: at a simple `BPC` the Jacobian has rank dim - 2. The kernel pair and the left null vector come from one SVD, the form `c_ij = psiᵀ F''[phi_i, phi_j]` comes from a central difference of the analytic Jacobian, and `branching_root` (Allgower and Georg 1990, section 8.3, as in `switch_bp`) solves it. Of the two roots the one that is less parallel to the chord from the point before the `BPC` is taken.
- `pc-cli dde-cycle` takes `switch` with `special` and `direction`, both required. It needs `branch: true`, refuses `adapt`, and prints the second run's branch with a `switch` object (`special`, `alpha`, `period`). It is off when absent. Reader errors are exit 2 naming `"switch"`, and engine refusals are exit 2 with the engine message.
- Oracle: ZBLK, whose second branch `z^2 = mu - m0` lies on the discretized problem exactly. Measured in the debug build: `alpha` of the switch is within 1.6e-15 of `m0`, the switched run has `|z^2 - (alpha - m0)|` at most 2.4e-15 over 57 points in either direction, and the transcritical form (about 84 degrees between the branches) has the same relation to 2.5e-15. The tables are in SPEC 8r.
- Tests: 12 acceptance tests of section 8r. Engine tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_cycle_switch.rs` with one ignored table printer, unit tests in `pc-linalg` (`svd_null`) and `switch.rs` (sign tolerance), 3 unit tests of the key reader in `crates/pc-cli/src/ddecycle.rs` and 7 command tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_cycle.rs`. The tests of sections 8n to 8q pass with no edit to a test or a bound (the `zblk_engine` helper of the CLI file was split into two helpers).
- Checks on 351bff3: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gives 2003 passed, 0 failed, 10 ignored, exit 0, and `cargo deny check` reports advisories, bans, licenses and sources ok. I did not run MATLAB, since no MATLAB file, C signature or gateway changed.
- Owner items:
  - New public Rust items: `switch_cycle_bpc`, `continue_cycle_switch`, `DdeCycleSwitch` and `svd_null`. All are Rust only.
  - Choices of mine without a source: the difference step 1e-5, the sign tolerance 1e-6 (`switch_bp` uses 1e-12), the simplicity thresholds (1e-6 and 1e-8 times the largest singular value), the form floor of 1e-8 times the largest singular value (it compares `c` with a singular value, which has other units), the chord as the way to find the followed branch, and the coincidence threshold 1e-12 (1 + the norm of the point).
  - The chord can be nearer the wrong root when the angle between the branches is small. The tests have 90 and about 84 degrees, so a small angle and the branch of a coupled mode are untested.
  - The switch is not cancellable, and the SVD for dim = 2964 takes 13.6 s on the development machine (values only, in a scratch test).
  - A branch from `adapt` is refused, and the start of the second branch is not corrected.
  - Through the CLI the refusal `not a BPC` is reachable only through an `LPC` special, which the cubic model tests.
  - That the discretized branch approximates a branch of cycles of the delay equation is not shown (as for P30). The oracles are represented exactly on the mesh.

P35, the unfolding case at a Hopf-Hopf point (SPEC section 8j-2; Rust and CLI only, no MATLAB part, no C ABI change, no change to a public MATLAB signature, no new dependency).
- `pc_engine::dde::unfolding_case(re_g2100, re_g1011, re_g1110, re_g0021)` returns one of twelve `HhCase` values (Ia, Ib, II, III, IVa, IVb, V, VIa, VIb, VIIa, VIIb, VIII) or `None`. `HopfHopf` has a `case` field, and `pc-cli dde-hh` prints it as the fourteenth key, `case`, with `null` for `None`. `None` means one of the four values is 0 or NaN, or Re g2100 Re g0021 = Re g1011 Re g1110.
- The rule is the sign pattern of the rescaled amplitude system of Du, Niu, Guo and Wei (arXiv 1804.03775, Table I): d0 = sgn(AD), b0 = sgn(AB), c0 = sgn(AC) and e0 = sgn(AD - BC), with A = Re g2100, B = Re g1011, C = Re g1110, D = Re g0021. Simple cases are Ia to IVb (d0 > 0), difficult cases are V to VIII.
- Tests (all in `hopfhopf.rs` and `crates/pc-cli/tests/dde_hh.rs`): one quadruple per case, boundaries and NaN, a 6^4 grid of 1296 quadruples checked against the rescaled system and a second copy of the table, the oscillator swap and positive scalings, 12 closed-form models run through the engine with both evaluators, and the named points of SPEC section 8j-2. The tests were red first (compile errors, then a failing CLI test).
- Committed as afd8496. Checks before the commit: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo deny check` passed, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` ended with `EXIT=0` and `passed 2051 failed 0 ignored 10`.
- Not verified (rule 9, owner items; SPEC section 8j-2 lists them):
  - The table rests on two printed sources that are not independent (Du et al. Table I and Buono and Belair 2003, Table 1, seen only through a secondary citation) and on my own re-derivation of the sign structure. Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5 is unread.
  - The oscillator order. Swapping the order maps (A, B, C, D) to (D, C, B, A) and exchanges II with III and V with VIII. SPEC section 8j fixes omega1 > omega2, and nothing confirms that this is the order of the printed table.
  - The Kuznetsov numbering (5 simple and 6 difficult cases) is not mapped to the lettering.
  - The label gives no margin near a boundary, and it names the case of the cubic amplitude system only, not of the full unfolding. The predictors are not computed.

P34 slice 1, delay equilibrium branches through the MATLAB front end (SPEC section 8s; route A, no C ABI change, `abi_version` stays 1, no new dependency; the owner approved public API changes on 2026-11-08).
- Rust (ea5738a, f0368e7): `model_from_ode` lowers a model with `delay(...)` through `lower_dde`, `model_info` reports `"delay"`, and `run_start` takes a `"type":"EP"` problem with a `roots` key on a delay handle (`Kind::DdeEp`, `RunKind::DdeEq`). The other entries refuse a delay handle with a named error. Checks before f0368e7: the four Rust checks passed, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gave 2065 passed, 0 failed.
- MATLAB: `Model.IsDelay`; `continuation(m, 1, Free=..., Roots=struct(N=.., ReMin=..))`, with `Roots` required for a delay model and refused for an ODE model, and `Type`, `MeshAdapt`, `Switch`, `Detect` with `H` and a special start refused for a delay model; `Branch.RootCount`, with `Eigenvalues` of N-by-0 and `Stable` equal to `RootCount == 0`; `rows2branch` reads the delay row `[alpha, u, count]` from `Model.IsDelay`, so `Session` needs no format change. A delay model built with the `aot` evaluator falls back to the JIT with no warning.
- Tests (the MATLAB tests and code were written before the first MATLAB run, so there was no separate red run on the MATLAB side; the Rust side was red first): `tests/DelayBranchTest.m` (18 tests: Hutchinson Hopf point, delayed fold, delayed pitchfork, option errors, session round trip) and two new cases in `tests/Rows2BranchTest.m`. The first run of `scripts/matlab-win "r = runtests({'tests/DelayBranchTest.m','tests/Rows2BranchTest.m'}); ..."` had one failure (a test expected `Parameters` as a column, but it is a row, as for an ODE model; the test was wrong), and after the fix the same command gave 36 tests, 0 failed. `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"` gave 547 tests, 542 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption, 256.8 s. Acceptance 6 (a session from the release before P34): a worktree at afe1c83 saved a session with an ODE `EP` branch (`old_save.m`, 18 points), and the toolbox at HEAD loaded it (`new_load.m`) with `Alpha`, `U`, `Eigenvalues` (18-by-1), `Stable` and `Stop` equal to the saved values, `IsDelay` false and an empty `RootCount`. Both scripts ran through `scripts/matlab-win --run` and are not in the repo.
- Not done after slice 1: the Hopf curve (slice 2, next block), the coefficients at ZH and HH points and cycles of a delay model (later slices). Warning ODE0054 names the `pc-cli` commands only, and the text is updated at the end of P34. `toolbox/+phasecont/+app/PhasePlane.m` line 612 treats an empty `Eigenvalues` as stable (`all(real(it.Eigenvalues) < 0)` is true for an empty vector), which matters once the app opens a delay branch.

P34 slice 2, the delay Hopf curve through the run entries and `phasecont.continuation` (SPEC section 8s, slice 2; route A, no C ABI change, `abi_version` stays 1, no new dependency).
- Rust (e5f3444, 15aafe4, aa47a07 SPEC; 96133a6 engine; 459894f FFI): `Kind::DdeHopf` (JSON `type "H"` on a delay model), `RunKind::DdeHopf`, row `[alpha2, x(1..n), alpha1, kappa, l1, hh]` of stride n+5, labels `GH` and `HH`. Required key `omega` (above 1e-8), optional `lyapunov` (default false) and `roots` (HH on when given); `detect`, `mesh_adapt`, `switch` and `start_is_special` are refused with named errors. Checks before 459894f: the four Rust checks passed, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` ended `EXIT=0` with 2074 passed, 0 failed (19 delay tests in `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs`).
- MATLAB (dd1f866): `phasecont.continuation(m, start, Type="H", Free=[p1 p2], Omega=w, Lyapunov=tf, Roots=...)`. A `Point` start must be labeled "H" and supplies omega from `Data.omega`; a numeric start needs `Omega` and `Params`; `Omega` with a `Point` is an error; `Omega` and `Lyapunov` are refused for any other run; `Detect`, `Switch`, `MeshAdapt` and `Var` are refused. The curve is a `Branch` of Type "H": `Alpha` N-by-2 `[first second]`, `U`, `Kappa`, `Eigenvalues` N-by-0, `Stable` false, `RootCount` empty, `Tests` N-by-0, `Codim2` N-by-2 (`l1`, `hh`), `Codim2Labels = ["GH" "HH"]`. It arrives when the run ends. `Session` needs no format change (a save and load round trip is a test).
- Tests: MATLAB red run before the production edits, `Rows2BranchTest` plus `DelayBranchTest`: 37 passed, 16 failed, 9 incomplete (the new tests). The first green attempt had 3 failures that were test or ordering mistakes (a local variable named `nan` shadowed `nan(3, 1)`; the Wright Bautin value must be `-L1AtZero / slope` = 0.38876, not the coupled model's `BStar`; the delay Type check ran after the Roots check), and after the fixes the same command gave 53 tests, 53 passed, 0 failed. `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"` (result file `build/test-results/junit.xml`) gave 564 tests, 559 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption, 243.9 s testing time. The SPEC text of slice 2 had said the codim-2 pair sits in `Tests`; the code and tests put it in `Codim2` with `Tests` N-by-0 on a curve, as for every other curve type, and the SPEC now says so. A scratch script (not in the repo) made `plot(br)` on a Wright Hopf curve with `Lyapunov = true`: 25 points, 3 graphics objects, and `Var` raises `phasecont:model:badOption`, as for an ODE H curve.
- Acceptance map (SPEC section 8s, slice 2; all passed in the runs above). Item 1 `a_hutchinson_hopf_run_equals_the_engine_through_the_run_entries`; item 2 `a_wright_hopf_run_labels_gh_with_the_lyapunov_option` and `a_wright_hopf_run_without_the_option_has_no_gh_and_nan_l1`; item 3 `a_coupled_hopf_run_labels_hh_with_roots`; item 4 `bad_delay_hopf_runs_name_the_key`, `the_new_hopf_keys_are_errors_on_an_ode_hopf_run` and `a_hopf_run_cancelled_right_after_the_start_keeps_no_points` (all in `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs`; the cancel test is Rust only, and MATLAB has no cancel test for a delay run, whose curve arrives when the run ends); item 5 `a_delay_hopf_row_is_x_alpha1_kappa_l1_hh` (`crates/pc-ffi/src/run.rs`) and `a_delay_hopf_branch_has_two_codim2_values_and_no_ode_data` (`crates/pc-engine/src/run.rs`); item 6 in `tests/DelayBranchTest.m`: `hutchinsonHopfCurveStartsAtTheHopfPoint`, `aDelayHopfBranchHasTheCurveLayout`, `wrightCurveLabelsTheBautinPointWithLyapunov`, `wrightCurveHasNoLyapunovWithoutTheOption`, `coupledCurveLabelsTheHopfHopfPointWithRoots`, `coupledCurveHasNoHopfHopfWithoutRoots`, `aNumericHopfStartNeedsOmega`, `omegaWithAPointStartIsRefused`, `aHopfRunFromANonHopfPointIsRefused`, `badOmegaAndLyapunovValuesAreRefused`, `theOptionsOfADelayHopfRunThatDoNotExistAreRefused`, `omegaLyapunovAndRootsKeepTheirPlace` and `aDelayHopfBranchSurvivesASessionRoundTrip`; item 7 is the unchanged ODE suites in the two full runs. One difference from the ODE Hopf curve: on a delay curve `GH` is labeled only with `Lyapunov = true` (default false, SPEC section 8s), while an ODE curve always tests it.
- Not done: the coefficients at ZH and HH points and delay cycles through the front end (later slices, each needs a SPEC amendment first). Same ODE0054 and `PhasePlane.m` line 612 notes as slice 1.

P34 slice 3, the zero-Hopf and Hopf-Hopf coefficients on the delay Hopf curve (SPEC section 8s, slice 3; route A, no C ABI change, `abi_version` stays 1, no new dependency).
- Rust (cf99b18 and 3a1673e SPEC; 10b9d07 engine, FFI and tests): opt-in JSON key `coefficients` (bool, default false, delay `"H"` only; an error on an ODE `"H"` run, on every other type and for a non-bool value). After the run, `dde::zero_hopf` is called at each `ZH` special with omega = sqrt(kappa), and `dde::hopf_hopf` at each `HH` special (they exist only with `roots`) with the larger of sqrt(kappa) and `omega2` first. `BT` gets none and `GH` already has `l2`. The values go into the special's `coefficients` object as name and value pairs after `omega2`; a missing value is left out. A refused special keeps its label and gets no values, and the branch gets one warning, `coefficients: the <label> special at index <index> has none: <error text>`. The four Rust checks passed before 10b9d07, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` ended `EXIT=0` with 2086 passed, 0 failed.
- MATLAB (d020819): option `Coefficients` (logical scalar, default false) of `phasecont.continuation`, Type "H" of a delay model only; it is refused for an ODE model, for another `Type` and for a non-logical value. The values arrive as `Special.Data.values`, a struct that is empty when the option is off. `rows2branch.m` needed no change, because slice 2 already maps the `coefficients` key. No `Branch` property and no session field was added, and a branch with coefficients survives a save and load.
- Tests: the Rust tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_hopf_coef.rs` (7) and `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs` (5 new) were run with the engine tests red first; the pc-ffi tests were written after the code. The MATLAB tests were red first: `runtests({'DelayBranchTest','Rows2BranchTest'})` gave 62 tests, 54 passed, 8 failed, 6 incomplete (the 8 new `DelayBranchTest` tests; the new `Rows2BranchTest` case passed at once, because that file needed no change), and 62 passed, 0 failed after the edits. `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"` gave 573 tests, 568 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption, 252.9 s testing time.
- Acceptance map (SPEC section 8s, slice 3). Item 1 `zh_values_equal_the_direct_call_and_the_closed_form`; item 2 `hh_values_equal_the_direct_call_and_the_oracle` and `without_roots_there_is_no_hh_special`; item 3 `the_larger_frequency_comes_first`; item 4 `a_refused_special_gets_the_warning_text`; item 5 `a_zh_run_with_coefficients_carries_the_engine_values`, `an_hh_run_with_coefficients_carries_the_engine_values_and_omega2`, `rows_do_not_change_when_coefficients_is_false_or_absent`, `bad_coefficients_keys_are_errors_that_name_the_key` and `the_coefficients_key_is_an_error_on_every_other_run_type` (all in `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs`); item 6 `zeroHopfCoefficientsArriveAtTheZhSpecial`, `theZhSpecialHasNoValuesWithoutTheOption`, `hopfHopfCoefficientsArriveWithOmega2`, `coefficientsWithoutRootsGiveTheZhValuesOnly`, `coefficientsDoNotMoveTheHopfHopfRows`, `aBranchWithCoefficientsSurvivesASessionRoundTrip`, `badCoefficientsValuesAreRefused` and `coefficientsKeepTheirPlace` (`tests/DelayBranchTest.m`); item 7 `delayHopfSpecialsCarryTheirCoefficients` (`tests/Rows2BranchTest.m`); item 8 is the unchanged suites in the two full runs. Two more Rust tests, `bt_special_gets_no_coefficients` and `only_zh_and_hh_are_computed`, check the labels that get no values.
- Gaps, stated plainly. (1) In a real run the located special is Newton-refined onto the equilibrium, so a start that the engine refuses cannot be built from a perturbed start. The refusal path (the `Err` branch of `run_hopf` that pushes the warning) is run only through the hand-made special of item 4, at the level of the function that the run calls, and not through a whole run. (2) Closed 2026-11-09: `hh_values_on_the_published_model_match_the_numpy_values_and_the_paper` (`crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_hopf_coef.rs`) runs the model of Bosschaert, Janssens and Kuznetsov (arXiv 1903.08276 v1, section 8.3; the model of SPEC section 8j test 1). The 11 values (g2100, g1011, g1110, g0021 re and im, theta, delta, their product) agree with an independent numpy computation to 1e-10 relative and with the four digits printed in the paper to half a unit of the last digit, on both evaluators; the sign is -1. The old values test is still a regression pin and is kept. The case table of the Hopf-Hopf classification is a separate owner item (Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5, rule 9). (3) Closed 2026-11-09: `aRefusedZhSpecialGivesTheWarningText` and `theSameRunWithoutTheResonanceHasValuesAndNoWarning` in `tests/DelayBranchTest.m` run the `ZhOsc` model through the front end (a decoupled oscillator at 2 omega, then at 3 omega) and check the full warning text, the empty `Data.values` of the refused special, and that points, specials and `Codim2` equal those of the run without `Coefficients`. (4) Closed 2026-11-09: `crates/pc-engine/tests/hopf_curve_pin.rs` hashes every `f64` of every point and special of three ODE Hopf curves (Brusselator, Bautin with its GH point, double Hopf with its HH point), plus stop reason and warnings. Checked by building each commit in a separate `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`: the engine before slice 3 (3a1673e) and the slice 3 commit (10b9d07) give the same hash for all three curves, so item 8 holds. The Brusselator hash moved once afterwards, at bcbcea6 (column scaling in the shared `start_tangent` in `curve2.rs`): 2 of the 520 numbers of the point list change by at most 4.44e-16 and 42 tangent entries by at most 3.33e-16. The golden is the post-bcbcea6 value. A worktree and the main tree share `target/` and the test binary name, so a bisection needs its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. The hashes were made on an Intel Core Ultra 7 255H; another CPU whose SIMD dispatch rounds differently can fail them, and the failure message prints the new hash.
- ODE0054 text (SPEC section 8s, item on `Model.Diagnostics`; commits a2db18f SPEC, then the code): the warning and every copy of its text now name `phasecont.continuation` (equilibria and Hopf curves) as well as the `pc-cli` commands, and end with "simulation and the ODE-only entries do not support it". Sites: pc-ode checker, diagnostic table, builtins comment, checker test, corpus golden, CLI golden, language reference (two places), xpp-probes README. New test `ode0054_names_the_matlab_entry_in_the_table_and_the_doc` pins the table entry and the language reference row. Red run: `cargo test -p pc-ode` gave 3 failed (`delay_with_one_argument`, `message_text_per_code`, the new test); after the edits `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` gave 2087 passed, 0 failed, EXIT=0. Refresh the text again when delay cycles run through `phasecont.continuation`.
P34 slice 4, periodic orbits of a delay equation from a Hopf point (SPEC section 8s, slice 4; route A, no C ABI change, `abi_version` stays 1, no new dependency).
- SPEC: 95aaf1e, with corrections f29b7ad (the ABI-gate wording). Rust: aaff6f5. ODE0054 text refresh: b99ce5d. MATLAB: b57bb2d.
- Rust: `"type":"LC"` on a delay model is `Kind::DdeLc {ntst, ncol, count}` with new JSON keys `omega`, `eps` (both required) and `floquet` (`{"nhist","count"}`, optional). The engine order is `check_cycle_floquet`, `hopf_cycle`, `params[free]` set to alpha of the corrected cycle, `continue_cycle_floquet`. The first point is the corrected Hopf orbit with h 0 and 0 Newton iterations. The parser refusal now reads "EP", "H" or "LC".
- MATLAB: `phasecont.continuation(m, start, Type="LC", Free="tau", NTST=20, NCOL=4, Omega=w, Eps=0.05, Floquet=struct(NHist=12, Count=4))` from an `H` point of a delay branch or from a numeric state with `Params` and `Omega`. New options `Eps` and `Floquet` (delay LC only). `Roots`, `Lyapunov`, `Coefficients` and `Var` are refused for a delay LC run with a message that names the option. The branch has the ODE cycle layout (`U`, `Period`, `Mesh` uniform), `Eigenvalues` holds the leading `Count` multipliers, `Stable` is false everywhere (not computed), and `RootCount` is empty. A session round trip keeps it.
- Numbers (SPEC section 8s, slice 4): the OSC run has 25 points, `Alpha(1)` 1.3276067784641747 (1e-9), `Alpha(end)` pi/2, |T - 2 pi| at most 5e-11, row length 86. The LPC model run has 99 points, one LPC special at MATLAB `Index` 68, |alpha| at most 2e-11 there, |T - 2 pi| at most 2e-10, |(r^2 - 1)^2 - alpha| at most 5e-8, a real leading multiplier above 1 and 4 multiplier columns.
- Tests: `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs` (the cycle tests, run red before the engine code), `tests/Rows2BranchTest.m` (8 `delayCycle*` tests) and `tests/DelayBranchTest.m` (18 new tests). The MATLAB side has no red run: the Rust side was red first, and the MATLAB tests were written with the code. An independent read of the diff (three read-only reviewers) found no code bug and a list of test gaps, which these tests close: the multiplier columns and the trivial-multiplier error against the engine rows, the amplitude relation r = sqrt(1 -/+ sqrt(alpha)) at both ends of the LPC run, the leading multiplier (1.018, 0.005), the Omega floor (0 and 1e-9) for a cycle run, the pinned error texts, the ODE refusals, a Rows2Branch case with three distinct specials and one with an empty count-2 branch. Targeted run: `scripts/matlab-win "addpath(fullfile(pwd,'toolbox')); res = runtests({'tests/Rows2BranchTest.m','tests/DelayBranchTest.m'}); ..."` gave 86 tests, 86 passed, 0 failed, 0 incomplete. The full suite before the added tests (`scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"`) gave 592 tests, 587 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered by assumption; the added tests are in the two files of the targeted run.
- ODE0054 text: now "(equilibria, Hopf curves and periodic orbits from a Hopf point)" at the same eight sites as in slice 3.
- Gaps, stated plainly. (1) Closed 2026-11-09 (6a95c99): the H-point start test compares `Alpha(1)` with the oscillator number at AbsTol 2e-9. The Hopf point of the branch is within 2.2e-14 of the exact tau and omega, and `Alpha(1)` differs by 5.6e-10; the period and amplitude of the last point are held to the SPEC bounds (5e-11, 1e-12). (2) `PD` and `NS` are not reached from a Hopf start by any model with a closed form. They are covered by the hand-made rows of `Rows2BranchTest` and by the engine tests of SPEC section 8o. (3) `trivial_error` is in the row and is not exposed by `Branch`. (3a) A numeric start without `Params` takes the parameters the model declares, and `Params` overrides the `P` of an H point; both are inherited from the ODE and slice 2 runs and are left unchanged. (3b) Closed 2026-11-09 (8e55fed): `grid` of a delay cycle is `[NTST NCOL c]`, `rows2branch` checks the row width against it, and a width that does not match is `phasecont:model:badRows`. A delay cycle session saved by the unreleased slice 4 build before this commit (2-element grid) is refused on load; nothing was released with that layout. (4) A real run with a stored orbit as start, mesh adaptation and switching at a branch point of a delay cycle are not in this slice.
- Not done: delay cycles from a stored orbit, mesh adaptation of delay cycles through the front end and branch switching at a delay BPC (route A, a later slice if the owner wants it).
- `PhasePlane.m` treated an empty `Eigenvalues` as stable. Fixed 2026-11-09 (8478f4c): a point without usable eigenvalues is drawn as a black cross (Tag `pc-equilibrium-unknown`), test `aPointWithoutEigenvaluesIsNotDrawnAsStable`. No delay point could reach the app at the time, so the issue was latent.

P34 slice 5, BPC, mesh adaptation and branch switching of delay cycles (SPEC section 8s, slice 5; route A, no C ABI change, `abi_version` stays 1, no new dependency; the owner approved public API changes for P34 on 2026-11-08 and delegated the choices).
- Rust: `Kind::DdeLc` gained `adapt`. New JSON keys on a delay LC: `bpc` (bool, false when absent), `switch` (`"BPC<k>"`) and `switch_direction` (1 or -1, required with `switch`). A switch needs `bpc: true` and refuses `options.mesh_adapt` above 0. A missing `mesh_adapt` means 0 for a delay cycle, so a slice 4 problem gives the same rows as before. With adaptation a row ends with the N + 1 mesh values. A failed run returns code 6 from `run_poll` with the message in `last_error`, and branch 0 stays readable through `run_copy`.
- MATLAB: `Detect = "BPC"` (the only value a delay cycle takes), `MeshAdapt = k`, `Switch = "BPC1"` and a new option `SwitchDirection` (1 or -1). `SwitchDirection` is a public option added inside the delegated P34 scope. A switch returns a 1-by-2 array, the run and the branch that leaves the BPC. `Roots`, `Lyapunov`, `Coefficients` and `Var` stay refused. `rows2branch` reads a grid of `[NTST NCOL c]` or `[NTST NCOL c adapt]`.
- Numbers (OSCZ, SPEC section 8s slice 5, no Floquet): 126 points, one BPC at MATLAB `Index` 98 (engine index 97), tau = 1.45 there to 1e-11; with `MeshAdapt = 3` 123 points and the BPC at `Index` 96 (engine index 95); the switched branch has 74 points in both directions, z is constant per orbit with z^2 = tau - 1.45 to 1e-12, and |z| ends at 0.3475576596694392. The z multiplier is exp((tau - m0) T) on the main branch and exp(-2 (tau - m0) T) on the switched one, both to 1e-9 relative with 6 multipliers and `NHist = 20`.
- Tests: `crates/pc-ffi/tests/delay.rs` (Rust, run red first), `tests/Rows2BranchTest.m` (4 new cases for the adapt column) and `tests/DelayBranchTest.m` (7 new tests, 2 amended). The MATLAB side has no separate red run.
- Not done: a delay cycle from a stored orbit, and a switch from any label other than `BPC`. `Detect` takes `BPC` only, because `LPC` is always labelled and `PD` and `NS` are labelled with `Floquet`.
- Inclination-flip adjoint sign fix and published oracle, 2026-11-09 (SPEC section 8b-3, acceptance 7). Cases B and C of Giraldo, Krauskopf and Osinga (2022) have published flip values (alpha = 0.4664012 at (a, b, c, beta, gamma) = (0.22, 1, -2, 1, 2), arXiv:2207.13846; 0.3694818 at (0.7, 1, -2, 1, 2), arXiv:2207.13854). Case B at T = 30 gave no IFS label. Cause: `orient_adjoint` took its sign from the end vectors, but phi has unit norm over all nodes, so its end values are only 1e-6 to 1e-9 of the norm at T = 20 to 40. At T = 30 the end at t = 0 ran from 1.6e-9 to 4.6e-7 with erratic sign between solves, so phi was flipped between neighboring points and IFS and IFU flipped with it. Fix: the sign comes from the inner product of the whole vector (all n(Nm+1) node values) with the previous accepted vector, interpolated onto the new mesh when the mesh adapts. Now both cases give one IFS label within 3e-8 (B) and 7e-8 (C) of the published values (they have seven digits) at T = 20, 30 and 40, with larger bracket ends of 3.2e-2 to 4.2e-2 (B) and 7.5e-3 to 1.0e-2 (C). That is the signal side of the floor on a published model, almost six orders above 1e-8, for IFS only. Tests: `the_published_flips_of_cases_b_and_c_are_found` and `the_sign_reference_follows_the_mesh_when_it_moves` (`hom_inclination.rs`), four unit tests in `hom_adjoint.rs`. Limit (iv) of acceptance 6 is settled: the later oracle zeros at alpha 2.285 and 2.639 are true inclination flips (an independent shooting script outside the repository confirmed them). Still open: no published IFU value exists, so the IFU signal side is untested, and the 1e-4 target and the 1e-8 floor stay our own choices.

Review pass of 2026-11-09 (the owner said "go ahead and fix whatever is left and the owner review items, research fixes and implement"; Rust and SPEC only, no C ABI change, no new dependency, no public MATLAB signature change).
- Lyapunov notice (c99cf22): on a delay Hopf curve `GH` and `l1` exist only with `Lyapunov = true` (SPEC section 8s), and a run without it says so. The default of `Lyapunov` stays an owner question, since a default of true changes results and costs time.
- Pole guard (aaaa4d8, 9f40c35): `POLE_TOL` stays at 1e-2 in `locate.rs`. A sweep of 1001 synthetic brackets found that the guard dropped 97 zeros, each with the "could not locate ZZ between points k and k+1" warning. A local-growth test in place of the guard would keep 24 of them and leave 73 undecided, and it adds four more constants with no source, so it was not adopted. Three new tests pin the guard against scale and noise (`a_pole_is_dropped_at_any_scale_and_under_noise`, `a_zero_with_a_decaying_numerator_is_not_taken_for_a_pole`, `a_zero_is_found_under_noise_at_any_scale`). SPEC sections 8 and 8k said a dropped pole gives no warning. That was wrong: only a zero that `codim2_label` rejects is silent. Both places now say so.
- Relative omega floor (cb2840c): the absolute `OMEGA_MIN` of 1e-8 made the Lyapunov coefficient fail for the Wright equation in rescaled time at alpha of 1e-9 or less (omega 1.6e-9), although the Hopf point and l1 do not change under t = alpha t'. `lyapunov`, `lyapunov2`, `zero_hopf` and `hopf_hopf` now require omega above `OMEGA_MIN` times the sum of the Frobenius norms of the matrices of the linearization. `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_lyap_scaling.rs` runs alpha from 1e-12 to 1e12 (l1 within a relative 1e-6 of -0.21413092244177392) and checks the error text at the floor. The other thresholds (`NULL_RESIDUAL_MAX`, `RESONANCE_MIN`, `PAIRING_MIN`) are ratios and passed over the same range, and `RESONANCE_MIN` is already a ratio, so the near-defective Delta test was skipped. The start of a Hopf curve followed in bcbcea6 (block below).
- Hopf-Hopf table (SPEC section 8j-2): four later papers that print the table or use its case structure show no disagreement (Niu and Jiang arXiv 1402.0287, Shen et al. arXiv 2002.09642, Izuhara and Kobayashi arXiv 2311.07045, Broer et al. arXiv 2001.08916). All of them take it from Guckenheimer and Holmes, so the table is still unconfirmed under rule 9. The table is unchanged. Owner action: read Section 7.5 of Guckenheimer and Holmes. The order of the oscillators (a swap exchanges II with III and V with VIII only) and the formulas for S1, S2 and l1 stay unconfirmed; Kuznetsov's errata list (https://webspace.science.uu.nl/~kouzn101/BOOK_ERR3.pdf) is the place to check the formulas.
- Inclination-flip adjoint (89f7d24): the orientation rule now uses the whole vector, not its ends. No published value exists for IFU, so the bracket floor `IF_BRACKET_FLOOR` of 1e-8 and the target of 1e-4 are still my choices.
- Whole-run refusal path closed on 2026-11-09: a ZH model with a decoupled oscillator at 2 omega (`ZH_OSC` in `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_hopf_coef.rs`) makes `Delta(2 i omega)` singular at the located ZH special, so `run_hopf` returns Ok, the special has no coefficients and the branch carries one `coefficients: the ZH special at index N has none: ...` warning. A run without the option has the same points, specials and stop reason, and the same model with the oscillator at 3 has the coefficients and no warning. The test runs on both evaluators. The three gaps that were still open here (the HH value test as a regression pin, the unchecked MATLAB warning text, the unpinned ODE `H` curve) are closed, see gaps (2) to (4) of slice 3.
- Book check of the ODE Hopf curve, 2026-11-09 (`crates/pc-engine/tests/hopf_curve_closed_form.rs`, model `crates/pc-engine/tests/models/lengyel_epstein.ode`, our own file written from the equations). Strogatz, Nonlinear Dynamics and Chaos, 3rd ed., section 8.3 (eq. 4, 5, 7 and Examples 8.3.2 and 8.3.3; pp. 281 to 286): x' = a - x - 4xy/(1+x^2), y' = b x (1 - y/(1+x^2)) has the equilibrium (a/5, 1 + (a/5)^2) and the Hopf curve b = 3a/5 - 25/a with omega^2 = (15a^2 - 625)/(a^2 + 25) for a > sqrt(125/3). `continue_hopf_curve` follows the curve from a = 10 (b = 3.5, omega^2 = 7) in both directions: point, b and kappa agree with the closed form to 1e-8 (observed 1e-15 towards smaller a), and the first Lyapunov coefficient at the start is -0.09/sqrt(7) within 1e-9, supercritical as the book says for a = 10. The book calls the Hopf supercritical without a range, but the curve has a GH point where l1 changes sign. I computed it separately with exact order 3 Taylor jets and the planar formula of Guckenheimer and Holmes eq. 3.4.11 (ad-hoc Python, not in the repo): a = 18.49507523094392, engine 18.495075230943886, and the engine l1 agrees with the independent value to 9.2e-15 absolute at 302 points. The test asserts one `GH` special at that value (1e-9) and the l1 sign on both sides. Strogatz has no Hopf-Hopf material, so the Hopf-Hopf case table (Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5) stays an owner item under rule 9.

Decisions of 2026-11-09 on the open owner items (the owner said "make the best decisons" and "go ahead with it"; each was decided on my judgment and none changes a result, the C ABI or the public MATLAB API):
- Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5: I cannot read the book, so the Hopf-Hopf table stays unconfirmed under rule 9 and the "unconfirmed" marks stay. Settled 2026-11-10 as not a release blocker (see the errata bullet below).
- Hopf-Hopf table, second check 2026-11-09 (the request named "Golubitsky and Schaeffer section 7.5"; Section 7.5 is Guckenheimer and Holmes, and Golubitsky and Schaeffer volume I has no Hopf-Hopf section). Found: Scholarpedia "Hopf-Hopf bifurcation" (Guckenheimer and Kuznetsov) confirms the omega1 > omega2 order of SPEC section 8j, the definitions of theta and delta and the simple/difficult split, and prints no table. Table I of Du et al. (arXiv 1804.03775v7), read in full, agrees with all twelve quadruples of the code tests (e0 recomputed for each), and an extraction of Buono and Belair (2003) gives the same sign rows. No code changed. Still not done: the primary text (Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5, or Kuznetsov 3rd ed. section 8.6) is unread, so the table is confirmed against secondary sources only and the "unconfirmed" marks stay under rule 9. Owner decision: accept the secondary confirmation, or supply a copy of the section.
- Hopf-Hopf table, Kuznetsov errata check 2026-11-10: the Erratum for the 3rd edition (26 September 2025, BOOK_ERR3.pdf) lists corrected text with the changed parts in red. I read the pages as images. It touches the Hopf-Hopf pages 360 to 366 and none of it is computed here: p. 360 line -13 now reads "otherwise, exchange the subscripts in (8.112)" with "reverse time and" struck out (the ordering step for the amplitude system, and the page SPEC section 8j cites as the paper's reference [43]); Fig. 8.25 portrait III region label 12; the second-order terms of the curves C and Y on pp. 363 and 364; the genericity conditions (HH.7) and (HH.8); and the sign formula for l1 with the factor -theta on p. 363. It lists nothing on the definitions of theta and delta, the case names, or the order omega1 > omega2. The code and SPEC 8j-2 use none of the curves, the figure, (HH.7), (HH.8) or that l1 formula, and the label comes from the four real parts through the rescaling in SPEC 8j-2, which does reverse time when Re g2100 < 0. Whether the corrected p. 360 sentence changes that rescaling's reading of the table is not answered by the errata, so the "unconfirmed" marks stay and nothing in SPEC or code changed. The errata PDF gives no old text, so a diff against the printed 3rd edition is not possible from it. Owner decision unchanged: accept the secondary confirmation with this caveat, or supply Guckenheimer and Holmes section 7.5 or Kuznetsov 3rd ed. section 8.6 as corrected. On 2026-11-10 the owner handed this decision over, and it is settled as follows: the table ships as unconfirmed, the `dde-hh` help text and the `HhCase` doc comment now say so in words, and a check against the book itself stays a follow-up that does not block a release.
- `Lyapunov` default stays false (opt-in, with the notice). A default of true changes the rows of a run and costs time, so it stays an owner question.
- Inclination flips: `IF_BRACKET_FLOOR` of 1e-8 and the 1e-4 target stay as documented own choices, since no published IFU value exists.
- 1.0 release, the File Exchange link and any C ABI change wait for an explicit go-ahead. Route A stays and `abi_version` stays 1.

Time scale of the delay Hopf curve (bcbcea6; SPEC sections 8e and 8f, tests 11 and 8). Before: the start of a Hopf curve and the H classification on a delay equilibrium branch used an absolute omega floor of 1e-8, and a start of 1e-3 or 1e3 times the unit scale was refused as rank deficient. Now: (1) `validate` and `hh_at` in `hopf.rs` and the new `Ctx::omega_floor` in `branch.rs` use 1e-8 times the size of the matrices of the linearization, as the Lyapunov floor does; the FFI parser checks only that omega is finite and above 0, and the CLI and MATLAB help say so. (2) `start_tangent` in `curve2.rs` divides each Jacobian column by its norm for the rank test and the null vector, then scales the vector back. `crates/pc-engine/tests/dde_hopf_scaling.rs` runs Hutchinson's Hopf curve for alpha from 1e-3 to 1e3 (r tau = pi/2, kappa = r^2 and omega = r within 1e-7), and `a_hopf_point_at_a_tiny_time_scale_is_still_an_h` (`dde_branch.rs`) finds the H at alpha = 1e-9 (red without the new floor). Limits: the step and tolerance options of a run are absolute, and the arclength includes kappa = omega^2, so a user who rescales time must rescale `h0`, `hmin`, `hmax`, `tol_x`, `tol_f`, `tol_refine` and `bounds` too; at alpha = 1e-4 with that scaling the Newton corrector fails (residual 1e-4 after 20 iterations) and no range below 1e-3 is claimed. At alpha = 1e-9 the root finder leaves candidates unconverged and says so in a warning. Checks on bcbcea6: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2119 passed, 0 failed) and `cargo deny check` pass; `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"` after restaging the DLL gives 601 total, 596 passed, 0 failed, 5 incomplete (the same 5 filtered by assumption as before).

P36 (branch `perf-ensemble`, from `main` at 6e3b7b9, HEAD 19568cd when this was written, 27 commits, local only, not pushed; the owner said no push of this branch). A speed and memory pass on the ensemble engine. No C ABI, MATLAB API, job JSON or `Backend="auto"` change. The bit-exact tier stays (no fast-math, FP_CONTRACT OFF, `--fmad=false`), and so do the rule 7 panic barriers and the cancel reads. Every item kept the bits of the item before it (the bench prints a bits check on every row) and passed `pc-cli gpu selftest` on CUDA 0, OpenCL 0 (RTX 5060) and OpenCL 1 (Arc 140T), single and double. Times below are the minimum of 5 interleaved rounds of `pc-cli ensemble --bench` on Windows, in ms.

- CPU. C1 (per-member cancel, so a cancel returns within one member instead of one 65536-step launch), C2 (`CpuTape`, 0 ulp against `Tape` in a proptest), C3a to C3f (loop hoists, one scratch per rayon task, `into_f64_vec` without a copy for double), C4 (JIT for double runs, no member threshold, 0 ulp against `CpuTape`, `F` compiled once per `Arc<ModelHandle>`; a panic during that compile fails `ens_start` with an error, per rule 7). C3e was dropped.
- GPU host. G2 (skip the `work` download when every member is `Ok`) and G3 (fill and zero buffers on the device, reuse the ctl buffer).
- GPU kernel. G1 (private scratch: the trial state and stage vectors are thread-local, `work` shrinks to what a launch hands on, `PC_WL` is `n + 1` for RK4 and `2n + 3` for DP5). H1 (RK4 segment constants once per segment), H2 (one-pass state check, bound capped at the largest finite value), H3 (DP5 `facold^beta` only on an accepted step), `restrict` (0e2f71b: `PC_RESTRICT` on the 7 buffer pointers of the 4 entries and both `pc_member` copies).
- Results on the RTX 5060, single, 100k members, before G1 to after: vdp-rk4 69.7 to 7.7, l96-8 234.8 to 16.4, l96-40 1182.8 to 49.0. DP5 double did not gain from G1 (FP64 runs at 1/64 rate on this card). After G1 the baselines are vdp-rk4 about 55 to 62 double and 7 to 9 single at 100k, vdp-dp5 CUDA double 100k about 160, l96-40 CUDA double 10k about 118, Arc vdp-dp5 double 100k about 370.
- H1: 0.72 to 0.85 on vdp-rk4 and l96-8 OpenCL single rows, 0.94 to 0.97 on double rows, CUDA single within noise. H2: flat to slightly better (l96-40 single 0.86 CUDA, 0.70 OpenCL RTX). H3: flat, since vdp-dp5 has few rejects.
- `restrict`, h3 against rs, vdp-rk4 100k: CUDA single 9.9 to 7.9 (0.80), Arc single 16.8 to 13.5 (0.80), OpenCL RTX single 0.93, double rows 0.98 to 1.01. vdp-dp5 100k double 0.98 to 1.01. l96-8 100k: CUDA single 19.3 to 15.2 (0.79) in the first run and 15.8 to 14.5 (0.91) in a 7-round rerun, Arc single 0.92 and 1.06, double rows 0.99 to 1.01. The l96-8 OpenCL RTX single row read 1.40 once and 1.01 over 7 rounds, so it was noise. l96-40 10k: all rows 0.92 to 1.02. CPU rows moved 0.64 to 1.28 on unchanged code, which is the CPU bench noise. A four-way interleaved run of C4 to H3 showed no regression beyond noise.
- Regression kept on purpose: Arc 140T OpenCL l96-40 single, 330 to 745 ms at 10k and 3480 to 8052 ms at 100k after G1, still about 2 times faster than the CPU at 10k. Two fixes were tried and dropped (a smaller RK4 footprint, 1.3 times worse; forced `always_inline` on `PC_FN`, no change). The owner chose to keep G1 as it is.
- Dropped after measurement, do not revisit: G4 (DP5 index list for compaction, 1.06 times on CUDA at 100k and 1M, below the 1.1 threshold), a block size of 128 or 256 (within 3 percent of 64), C3e, g1b, g1c.
- Skipped on purpose: progress counters in the ctl block (about 1 percent of a launch) and a warm start of the launch ramp (small gain, TDR risk on a laptop GPU).
- Metal: the kernel text changes are type-checked on `aarch64-apple-darwin` only and have never run on a Mac. The kernel buffer layout is unchanged, so MSL needs only the `PC_RESTRICT` define, which is empty there.
- Slice 3, continuation and IVP. A `perf` profile of `morris_lecar.ode` runs (Linux build with symbols) ranked the candidates, and four of them were built, each with the same output hash as the base. 1b5072e reuses the cycle assembly at an unchanged x and mesh, 12e739d finds the plain JIT entries by index instead of by hash, 07e08f0 skips the second interval condensation of the monodromy at one x, and 21503d6 moves the finished branch into the run state instead of cloning it (the worker frees the streamed points after it releases the lock, and copies only for a run that switches; SPEC, Background runs). Minimum of alternating runs, in ms, base then the first three commits: `ml_lc` 154.2 to 127.6, `ml_lc_big` 224.1 to 185.7, so the ML cycle runs are about 17 percent faster than at the P36 base. The branch move, in a separate same-session A/B against 07e08f0: `ml_lc` 134.8 to 132.2 and `ml_lc_big` 190.1 to 187.2 (both noise), `ml_ivp_long` (340024 points, tspan 0 to 200000) 360.9 to 333.6 (7.6 percent). Peak memory: `ml_ivp_long` 222 to 211 MB, `ml_lc_big` 23.25 to 20.9 MB.
- Slice 3, measured and not done. Batched IVP points under the lock: the profile shows the cost is the per-point clone and its allocations, not the uncontended lock, and batching would delay the live points. Column-restricted `JacP`: no workload shows it, and `ml_ep` is too short to profile. The libm `expm1` and `tanh` calls (about 17 to 22 percent of the ML profile) and the faer LU and matmul calls are bit-changing, so they stay as they are.
- MATLAB side, 2026-11-12, on the branch DLL and MEX restaged after 21503d6 (before the CUDA error note below, which changes no numbers): 43 of 43 ensemble tests pass, the GPU self-test passes 6 of 6 (OpenCL 0 and 1, CUDA 0, single and double), and the full suite is 646 total, 640 passed, 0 failed, 6 incomplete (baseline 596 of 601).
- Large n on CUDA, found in the review pass. G1 moved the stage vectors from global memory into private arrays, which CUDA keeps in local memory with a cap of 512 KB per thread (7 n reals for RK4, 10 n for DP5). On the RTX 5060 a DP5 double job fails at n = 6000 (n = 5500 passes), an RK4 double job at n = 10000 (n = 8000 passes), and single precision at n = 10000 passes for both. The kernels before G1 ran these sizes. The failure is a clean `gpuLaunch` error, and since 19568cd its text names the size of the arrays, the number of states, the cap and the cpu backend (`cuda::scratch_note`, replaced by `cuda::global_note` in 5af2f01; SPEC, GPU section). No job is refused and there is no fallback kernel, since either would change what a backend accepts. The OpenCL limit on the Arc 140T (256 KB of scratch) is the same before and after G1: n = 5500 fails to build in both builds and n = 3500 passes in both. The output bits of every job that completes are identical across the base, G1 and the final build. The global-scratch kernel below removes the CUDA limit.
- Review pass, 2026-11-12 (three reviewers on sonnet: CPU ensemble and JIT, GPU host and kernel, continuation). Continuation: no finding. CPU: no numerics, cancel or unsafe defect. Findings left as they are: `native_rhs` returns the error and fails the run only when the compile panics on the model thread (rule 7, set in C4); an ordinary compile failure, a delay model and an oversized frame already run on the tape (the reviewer would fall back to the tape for a panic too; decided below), the model mutex is held across the model-thread call that compiles `F` (a second run on the same model waits for the compile, once per model handle), `the_handle_compiles_once_and_a_recompile_leaves_the_code_alone` cannot fail as written, and the DP5 case of `a_non_finite_state_is_non_finite_under_any_bound` fails at the start and never reaches the fused state check in a step. GPU: the large-n finding above, and nothing else.
- Decisions, 2026-11-12. The owner approved the local fast-forward of `perf-ensemble` into `main` (nothing is pushed) and the global-scratch CUDA kernel above a size threshold. For the C4 compile-failure rule the owner asked for a decision after review: keep the rule-7 error. A panic in the compile on the model thread reaches `ens_start` as an error, because a tape fallback would hide a pc-jit bug. Every other compile failure already runs on the tape with the same bits. The cost: the slot stays empty after the error, so each later double-precision CPU run on that handle compiles again and fails again; single precision and GPU runs are not affected.
