- Global-scratch CUDA kernel, branch `cuda-global-scratch` off `main` (SPEC `efd5eff`, code `5af2f01`, not merged). The kernel has a second layout, `Global`, with `y`, `y1`, `yt` and the stage vectors in the work buffer behind the persistent words. The operations and their order are the same as in `Private`, so the bits are the same. CUDA picks `Global` when the private arrays of one member reach 256 KiB (`GLOBAL_SCRATCH_FROM`): DP5 double from n = 3277, RK4 double from n = 4682, DP5 single from n = 6554, RK4 single from n = 9363. OpenCL and Metal got the variant in step 4 (the bullet after the next one). On the RTX 5060 (CLI, 16 members, t1 = 0.1), checked by comparing the output bits: DP5 double at n = 6000 and n = 6200 is identical to the pre-G1 CUDA build (0 of 96000 and 0 of 99200 values differ), RK4 double at n = 10000 is identical to the CPU (0 of 160000), and the jobs that G1 already ran are identical to the G1 output: DP5 double at n = 3500, 4500 and 5500, RK4 double at n = 8000, and single at n = 10000 for both integrators. The one difference to the CPU in the n = 6000 job is a single value, 1 ulp, in member 7; the pre-G1 CUDA build has the same value, so it is not new, and I have not looked into the cause. A cold driver compile at n = 6200 took 190 s with `Global` and 193 s in the pre-G1 build; a second run of the same job took 3 s from the driver cache. OpenCL on the RTX 5060 with this build: DP5 double at n = 5500 is identical to the G1 output (0 of 88000 values differ, all `ok`), and RK4 double at n = 10000 fails with `CL_OUT_OF_RESOURCES (-5)` after 3106 s; the G1 run of the same job left an empty output too, and the OpenCL kernel text is unchanged, so this is the OpenCL private-memory limit and not new. `pc-cli gpu selftest` with the 5af2f01 CLI exits 0 with `pass` and `bit_exact` true on CUDA device 0, OpenCL device 0 (RTX 5060) and OpenCL device 1 (Arc 140T), single and double (6 runs). The device test `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/cuda_global.rs` passed on the RTX 5060 (283 s, mostly cold compiles; `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda cargo test -p pc-ensemble --test cuda_global`): the self-test under a forced `Global` layout in single and double, then RK4 at n = 5000 and Dormand-Prince at n = 3500, both above the 256 KiB rule, each run under the size rule and under a forced `Private` layout with the same state bits, status and step counts, then Dormand-Prince at n = 6000. Fixed-step RK4 also equals the CPU bit for bit. The first version of that test compared Dormand-Prince bits with the CPU and failed by a few ulps in many values, because the step size takes powers and the CUDA `pow` is not promised to match the CPU's (the same cause as the 1-ulp difference above, which I had not explained); the bit-exact tier of the self-test is fixed-step RK4 only. The test now compares Dormand-Prince against the CPU to 1e-7. MATLAB run with the DLL built from `1550510` (the MEX is unchanged, since the gateway and the C ABI are the same): `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` passes in single and double on CUDA device 0, OpenCL device 0 and OpenCL device 1 (6 of 6), and `EnsembleTest` plus `EnsembleHelperTest` ran 43 tests with 43 passed, 0 failed and 0 incomplete. Not measured yet: the full MATLAB suite on this branch. Member batching (step 1 of the memory work, SPEC §13 "Member batches", commits `1cbffa0`, `05e7453`, `fbd5c2a`, `31490d5`): the GPU backends cut a job into batches of members so the buffers of one batch fit a budget, and the batches run one after the other on one context and one compiled kernel. CUDA takes 60 percent of the free device memory from `mem_get_info`, OpenCL and Metal take 50 percent of the reported device memory and also keep each buffer under `max_alloc`; the fractions are not tuned. The planner (`batch::plan_batches`) is pure and has unit tests, and `run_batched` joins the batches in member order, reads the cancel flag before each batch and stops at the first error. A job that fits runs as one batch. `tests/batch.rs` (CPU as the runner, 9 tests) shows the result of batches of 1, 4, 7, 11, 22, 23 and 100 members has the bits of one run, in both precisions, for RK4 and Dormand-Prince, with per-member parameters, a mix of statuses and a cancel. On the RTX 5060 `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda cargo test -p pc-ensemble --test cuda_batch` passed (1 test, 10.7 s): 70 members of van der Pol (single and double, RK4 and Dormand-Prince, per-member parameters) and of Lorenz-96 at n = 40 (global and forced private layout) whole and in batches of 1, 7 and 64 give the same bits, and a Lorenz-96 job at n = 5000 with 23425 members, 400008 bytes each (9.4 GB against 8.5 GB of card memory), runs with every member `ok`, and its first and last 25 members equal the CPU bit for bit. Not done: a device run of the OpenCL and Metal batch path (the Windows CLI was not rebuilt for this change), a retry after an out-of-memory error.
- Layout rule tuned by timing (step 3 of the memory work, SPEC §13 `cuda::layout_for`, 2026-11-12). The 256 KiB threshold alone let the `Private` layout ask the driver for about 10 GB of local memory on a card with less, because CUDA reserves local memory for every resident thread of the device (26 * 1536 = 39936 on the RTX 5060) whatever the member count, and keeps it for the life of the context. `layout_for` now takes a `Capacity` (resident threads, free memory, the reserve the context already holds) and picks `Global` when the arrays take 256 KiB, when Dormand-Prince arrays hold 3300 reals or more, or when the `Private` reserve is more than 3/4 of the free memory. Measured with `tests/cuda_layout_time.rs`: RK4 `Private` was about 1.4 times faster at 0.70 to 0.75 of the free memory and `Global` 2 to 3 times faster from 1.0 up, at about 7.5, 5.0 and 2.8 GB free; Dormand-Prince `Private` was faster up to 3200 reals (1.08 to 1.65 times) and `Global` from n = 335 (1.29 to 1.75 times); the bits were the same in every case. The held reserve is added back into the free memory so one job keeps one layout across calls, and the batch budget is 60% of the free memory less the `Private` reserve. 17 CUDA unit tests pass with the new rule, and `cuda_global` and `cuda_batch` pass on the RTX 5060 (`PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda cargo test --release -p pc-ensemble --test cuda_global --test cuda_batch`: 2 passed, 13 s). Not done: the held estimate is about 88% of the measured drop at n = 1200, a retry after an out-of-memory error, and the Windows CLI and DLL rebuild.
- OpenCL and Metal Global layout (step 4 of the memory work, SPEC §13, 2026-11-13). The kernel text has `PC_SCR`, the qualifier of the scratch pointers: `PC_PRIV` in the prelude, `PC_GLOBAL` under `Layout::Global`, because OpenCL C 1.2 and MSL have no generic address space. `kernel_source_in` takes a layout for every dialect, `opencl::force_layout` and `metal::force_layout` force one, and both hosts pick it with `gpu::layout_by_size` (256 KiB of step arrays, size only, no free-memory query). Timed with `tests/opencl_layout_time.rs` (ignored): on the RTX 5060 through OpenCL `Private` fails with `CL_OUT_OF_RESOURCES` between 437 and 468 KiB and `Global` ran to 546 KiB; below the failure `Private` is up to 1.33 times faster in single precision and level to mixed in double, the bits equal in every case. On the Arc 140T `Private` is faster wherever both compile, and both layouts fail to compile from 4600 states in double and 9000 in single (292320 and 292288 bytes of scratch against 262144 at n = 4600). The cause is the compiler, not the layout: the straight-line right-hand side keeps every `x[k]` load live and the Intel compiler spills them (317888 bytes at n = 5000 for a ring and for Lorenz-96, in either layout), so the 256 KiB rule never picks the slower layout there. The cap stays: a launch that runs out of resources has no safe retry, and a cap near 440 KiB would sit within 7% of the failure. Checked: `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=opencl ./opencl_global.exe` (Windows build of `tests/opencl_global.rs`) passed on both devices (238 s): the self-test under a forced `Global` in single and double, Lorenz-96 at n = 40 with RK4 and Dormand-Prince in both precisions, `Private` against `Global` with the same bits, and `Global` in batches of 1 and 7 with the same bits, a 2000-state ring under a forced `Global` equal to the CPU, and a 5000-state ring that the size rule runs in `Global`: on the RTX 5060 equal to the CPU, on the Arc 140T failing the same way in both layouts (the test accepts that only when `Private` hits the same scratch error). `cuda_global` and `cuda_batch` pass again after the text change (`PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda cargo test --release -p pc-ensemble --test cuda_global --test cuda_batch`, 2 passed). Metal is type-checked only (`cargo clippy -Zbuild-std ... --target aarch64-apple-darwin`) and its text goes through the macOS CI check. Step 5 (Windows and MATLAB, 2026-11-13, tree at 50d1a44): `scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64` and `cargo build --release --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-cli` (CLI copied to `phasecont-stage/cli`) both exit 0. The MEX was not rebuilt, since the gateway last changed in 06d7582, before the staged `pckern.mexw64` of 2026-11-08. `pc-cli gpu selftest` exits 0 for CUDA and OpenCL on the RTX 5060 and OpenCL on the Arc 140T, single and double (six runs). `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` through MATLAB has `Pass` true for the same six cases. `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 646 total, 640 passed, 0 failed, 6 incomplete (all filtered by assumption, the same counts as 2026-11-10), 212 s of testing. Not done: fewer live values in the kernel's right-hand side to move the Arc limit (a separate change, results would not change), Metal on a device.
- Open: (1) the two weak tests named in the review pass; (2) owner call on the global-scratch kernel: the memory cost in the band between 256 and about 450 KiB, where `Global` needs about 6 times the device memory per member for DP5, which batching now bounds by the free memory (a job of any size runs, in more batches); also that the run downloads the whole work buffer for members that did not finish `ok`; (3) the Metal kernel text is type-checked only.

### XPPAUT 8.2.2 triage fixes (2026-11-18, merged into `main` and pushed at 56509d4)

The owner answered "Yes to all" to the 11 triage items of the XPPAUT 8.2.2 check and asked for an advisor review of how to build them, with a check that the code is optimized and uses bit operations where it pays. Three branches were built and merged into `xpp82-integrate`, which starts at the check-derivs tip. That branch is on `main` and pushed (56509d4, CI green). The second round of fixes is in the next section.

- `xpp82-check-derivs` (c5576d8, item 6a and 6b): an entry too large to build is a SKIP row (95a6c3e), the finite-difference rows leave out sample points where the model value is not finite and count them, and a row with no usable point fails (c018f4b, f8ce848). The corpus test keeps SKIP rows out of its counts (c5576d8). Item 6c, a finite-difference step that scales with the parameter, is not built: no source gives a formula that would fix `frcp`, so it waits for the owner (rule 9).
- `xpp82-zero-tangent` (merged as a27c095, item 1): the DAG ops `ZMul(t, p)` and `ZDiv`, which give +0 when the tangent t is zero at run time and the plain product or quotient otherwise. They are in the evaluator, the derivative rules, the multilinear recurrence, the Cranelift lowering, the CPU tape and the C, CUDA, OpenCL and MSL emitters. SPEC section 2 states the rule. The JIT lowers the guard to a compare and a select, which x64 emits as a compare and a jump. An integer mask lowering (de7ef45) was built for the bit-operation request and reverted (a13bccd) because it was slower on real Jacobians: `wcfun` Dir[U,U,U,U] took 89 to 101 ns per call with no guard, 100 to 104 ns with the select and 142 to 158 ns with the mask. The mask won only on a microbenchmark with random zeros.
- `xpp82-parser` (merged as 40644ff, items 2 and 3): any first word that starts with `so` is `solv` (still ODE0030) and any first word that starts with `n` is `number`, a backslash plus blanks continues a line, `arret` and `out_put` are event actions on a `global` line, an aux may share its name with a fixed quantity, and `name[n]` with a whole number is `name<n>`. Each ruling has an XPPAUT 8.2.2 probe in `references/xpp-probes/`, and `run.sh` gave 414 PASS lines.
- Race fix (c835df4, merged as f676d50): two tests in `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs` wrote the same scratch file `sqclamp.json` in parallel, and one read it half written ("EOF while parsing"). The race came in with b2f2d26 on the zero-tangent branch, not with the parser. The cause was read from the error text and the code, not reproduced first. After the fix the `cont` tests ran 30 times with no failure.

Checks on f676d50: `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2495 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok. Measured against the base f60085f:

- Release `pc-cli` over the 217 `.ode` files of XPPAUT 8.2.2 (kept outside the repo, GPL): 14 files go from a parse error to a clean parse and none the other way. `bs`, the four `lecar` copies, `itoy` and `toy_ok` also pass `ir` and `check-derivs` (`itoy` and `toy_ok` with 279 SKIP rows each). The other seven stop at refusals the triage kept (DAE, simulation only, `sum`). `tstauto/bob` now passes `check-derivs` (377 rows were FAIL on the base), and its derivative tables shrink from 409 to 399 nodes. The one sweep failure left is `tstauto/frcp` (item 6c).
- Jacobian bits, JIT and DAG, over the same models: 5156040 values, 419 change. 148 go from NaN to a finite value (all in `bob`) and 271 are zeros that change sign (`coaster2D`, `jcoaster`, `jcoaster1`). JIT and DAG agree to 0 ulp on both builds.
- `pc-cli ensemble --bench` (64 and 1024 members, CPU and CUDA): the 28 hashes match the base.
- MATLAB (DLL restaged from f676d50 on 2026-11-18 03:42): `buildtool test` 896 total, 887 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete, all 9 filtered by assumption.
- Rule 3: no test file in the branches matches an XPPAUT example file, exactly or after normalizing blanks and case.

Owner items from this work:

1. Zero tangents. (a) The +0 is a choice that follows the fold rule of `Mul`; no reference confirms it, and the slower Dual fallback can give -0 where the DAG gives +0. (b) A product term is still a plain `Mul`, so a clamped factor times a partial that is not finite still gives NaN, for example `exp(-max(1-x,2)*(1/max(a-x-10,0)))`. A guard on every product term costs a compare and a select on each. (c) The guard slows `wcfun` Dir per call (numbers above); accept it or ask for a cheaper lowering. (d) The `powneg` row A (FD) failed before this work and still fails. (e) The body of commit c306964 says the lowering has no branch, which is wrong on x64; the code comment is correct and the history is kept.
2. Parser. (a) `nout` is read by XPPAUT 8.2.2 and still gets warning ODE0024. (b) XPPAUT drops text after a backslash and continues the line, while PhaseCont refuses it. (c) No ruling for an aux that repeats a parameter, a state or another aux, which XPPAUT accepts. (d) Prefixes of the other keywords (`set`, `options`, `special`, `export`, `volt`, `hom_bcs`), `arret` as the name of a quantity, and the `name[n]` targets `map`, `set`, `wiener`, `markov`, `global`, `options` and `dy[j]/dt` were not probed. (e) XPPAUT continues a `#` comment line that ends in a backslash, and PhaseCont does not, as before. (f) Raising the size limit for derivative entries would let `itoy` and `toy_ok` build their 279 SKIP entries.
3. check-derivs. (a) Item 6c, the step for `frcp`. (b) `ode/vlsi` A (FD) fails with ratio 4.5 and `ode/crazy` A with ratio 165, both as on the base and probably the same step problem. (c) `ode/evelyn` leaves out 9 of its 20 points, and a row where both values are NaN prints "worst inf". (d) The codegen corpus test never meets a SKIP row from a real model.

Where these items stand after round 2 (next section): 1(a) every evaluator now stores +0, so the DAG and the fallback agree on the sign; 1(b) product terms and call terms whose other factor may be non-finite are guarded; 1(c) still open; 1(d) `powneg` passes 60 of 60 seeds through the Richardson rows; 1(e) unchanged. 2(a), 2(b), 2(e) are built, and 2(c) is built for a parameter only; 2(d) was probed and the differences are parser questions 4 to 6 below; 2(f) is the node-limit options below. 3(a) `frcp` passes 30 of 30 seeds, so item 6c may close; 3(b) `vlsi` passes 30 of 30 and `crazy` still fails 4 of 30 (seeds 12, 13, 20, 30); 3(c) `evelyn` uses 20 of 33 drawn points on every row and no longer prints "worst inf"; 3(d) unchanged.

### XPPAUT 8.2.2 fix round 2 (2026-11-18 to 2026-11-19, branch `xpp82-integrate2`)

Round 2 builds the owner answers to the items above ("1 fix all of one, 2 fix parser consulting advisor, 3 ask advisor"). Three branches start at 56509d4 and are merged into `xpp82-integrate2`. A review pass checked each branch and its fix round (`review2.md`, outside the repo). On 2026-11-19 the owner approved the merge: local `main` is fast-forwarded to 0d7595d, and `xpp82-integrate2` is pushed as a branch for CI (run 44615292520). `origin/main` is still 56509d4. In that run the `rust (windows-latest)` job failed in `a_zero_tangent_never_gives_minus_zero_in_c` (`crates/pc-codegen/tests/aot.rs`), which asserted more than 100 compared values and so assumed two C compilers; the Windows runner has gcc alone. The test now asserts 96 values per available compiler. Every other job passed.

- `xpp82-zt2` (69ef004): every stored zero derivative is +0 in the DAG, the interpreter, the JIT and the AOT C. A product term whose other factor may be non-finite uses `ZMul`, and the chain term of a call (`sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `atan`, `sinh`, `cosh`, `tanh`, `exp`, `abs`, `erf`, `erfc`, `PowI` with n >= 0, the table slope and the finite-slope branch of `pow`) is guarded when its argument may be non-finite, for example `exp(-(1/max(a-x-10,0))^2)`. SPEC section 3 states the rule. `cargo test` 2510 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored. The vdp-rk4 bench hashes are unchanged, and over the corpus only `morris_lecar` gains guards (`ZMul` 6 to 15 nodes over JacU and JacP).
- `xpp82-checkderivs` (69a82ba): the finite-difference rows compare the Richardson value `(4 D(h/2) - D(h))/3` with an allowance for rounding from the `eval_err` bounds (SPEC section 3, item 2). A draw is left out only when the fallback is not finite there and the DAG gives the same value; a draw where the two differ is compared and fails. Each row prints the points and values it compared, and a row that compared none fails. `cargo test` 2524 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored. Reruns of `powneg` (60 seeds), `frcp`, `vlsi`, `crazy` and `evelyn` (30 seeds each) give byte-identical output to the first fix round.
- `xpp82-parser2` (a9ffd8d): `nout` and `njmpx` are read as `njmp` (the last of the three wins), text after a backslash is dropped with warning ODE0075, a `#` comment that ends in a backslash swallows the next line with warning ODE0076 (line 1 of the main file is exempt), the language server has two quick fixes for each, the writer cuts a comment before its first backslash, and an aux may repeat a parameter. SPEC section 8ae. `cargo test` 2533 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored; `references/xpp-probes/run.sh` with XPPAUT 8.2.2 gave 286 probes and 572 PASS.
- Integration (`xpp82-integrate2`, with the review fixes R5 and the `eval_entry` read in `check.rs`): `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo deny check` pass, and `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` ran 148 suites with 2577 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored. The extra ignored test is the R1 test below. On the integration branch the vdp-rk4 bench hashes are unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1, `scripts/check-bench-bits`), and `references/xpp-probes/run.sh` with the debug `pc-cli` gave 572 PASS and 0 FAIL. The full MATLAB suite (`buildtool test`, with `pckern.dll` restaged from this branch) ran 897 tests with 888 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete, against 896, 887, 0 and 9 on 56509d4; the new test is `ModelTest/backslashWarningsAreIssued`, and it passed.

Open from round 2:

- R1, "Multi-slot derivatives on a pole" (owner question, rule 9). On a pole the DAG gives NaN where the interpreter gives +0 in 76116 of 399360 multi-slot directional derivative values (`crates/pc-ir/tests/zmul_multi.rs`, the on-pole test, ignored). The cause is the plain block product in `crates/pc-ir/src/multi.rs`. A zero-factor guard there cut the count to 3691, all of the opposite kind (DAG +0, interpreter NaN, third order), so neither side follows one rule. Which side is right, and is 69ef004 kept as it is until then?
- R2: in a chain of `#` comments that end in a backslash, the Remove fix of the second ODE0076 makes the next line live. The first fix comments out the whole chain, so the case needs two steps.
- R3: an `exp`, `sinh` or `cosh` that overflows at a finite argument (above about 709) is not guarded, since `may_be_nonfinite` does not track overflow.
- R4 (measured 2026-11-19, decided below): JIT time per call, 56509d4 against `xpp82-integrate2`, release build. Each number is the mean of three interleaved runs, and each run takes the minimum over 21 repeats of 200000 calls. `morris_lecar`: JacU 85.0 to 87.1 ns, JacP 121.8 to 119.6 ns, Dir[U,U,U] 142.6 to 147.8 ns, Dir[U,U,U,U] 237.1 to 250.4 ns (+5.6 percent). `wcfun`: JacU 38.3 to 39.6 ns (+3.4 percent), JacP 43.1 to 45.7 ns (+5.9 percent, the largest rise), Dir[U,U,U] and Dir[U,U,U,U] within 1 percent. Five of the eight entries got slower, by 2.4 to 5.9 percent, and `morris_lecar` JacP got 1.8 percent faster. Cause (found 2026-11-19 from the size of the machine code Cranelift emits, since `perf` counters do not work in WSL): the JIT adds `+ 0.0` to every derivative output before it stores it (the rule that a zero derivative is +0), which lowers to one `vaddsd` per output with its constant in the constant pool. Built without that add, `wcfun` JacU and JacP come out at exactly the base size, and the JacU bytes are identical to base. The rest of the `morris_lecar` growth comes from the `ZMul` guards that correctness needs. Hoisting the constant changes nothing, because Cranelift already shares it. Code size in bytes (56509d4, `xpp82-integrate2` without the `+ 0.0`, `xpp82-integrate2`):

  | Model | JacU | JacP | Dir[U,U,U] | Dir[U,U,U,U] |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `morris_lecar` | 1280, 1424, 1504 | 2416, 2688, 3008 | 5136, 6224, 6288 | 13632, 16368, 16336 |
  | `wcfun` | 768, 768, 864 | 944, 944, 1120 | 4144, 4128, 4176 | 12256, 12240, 12208 |

  Ten later interleaved reruns of the same probe on a busier machine spread more from run to run (for example `wcfun` JacP on 56509d4 from 28.8 to 49.6 ns) than the rises above, so this probe does not resolve the percentages; the code sizes are the firmer evidence. Decided 2026-11-19 with the advisor (the owner may reverse it): the cost cannot be avoided without dropping the +0 rule, so it is accepted. The probe is scratch and is not committed.
- N1: other prefix spellings such as `totalx`, `dtx`, `method` and `transx` keep their own key in `option_pairs` (`crates/pc-ffi/src/parse.rs`), as before; only `nout` and `njmpx` fold into `njmp`.
- The AOT cache in `tempdir/phasecont-aot` has no size cap.
- Node limit (parser item 2(f)): `MAX_NODES` is checked against the one DAG that all entries share. Measured options: Dir order 4 with about 2M nodes (0 SKIP, 54.7 s), or order 3 with the current 1M (0 SKIP, 42 s); a per-entry limit is the structural fix and is not built.
- `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md` still names XPPAUT 8.0 in one place.
- Parser questions 1 to 8 of the parser2 report, among them an aux that repeats a state or another aux (ODE0041 kept), other keyword prefixes, `global` targets and `no_interp`.
- check-derivs questions: accept `crazy` as a limit of finite differences or tie the step to the model scale; close item 6c; keep or tighten the `|D(h) - D(h/2)|/3` term; check the Dahlquist and Bjorck section number (3.4.6) against the print edition.
- Book scan (`/home/nub04/bookscan/out/RANKED.md`, outside the repo): the BT homoclinic curve beta1 = -(6/25) beta2^2 from Kuznetsov 4th ed. p. 359 would close SPEC 8b test 4; psi15 and psi16 on p. 650 are a second source for the homoclinic tests; the Hopf-Hopf sign table is still unconfirmed; the zero-Hopf theta differs by a factor of 2 (-2.0316 against -4.0633).

Decisions of 2026-11-19 on the open items above. The owner asked for them to be made with the advisor ("make the decisions with the advisor"), and the owner may reverse any of them.

- R1: keep 69ef004, where a zero factor gives +0 at every order. Sort the 3691 remaining values by where the interpreter's NaN starts: if it comes from 0 times a non-finite value, fix the interpreter; if it comes from inf minus inf, fix the guard in the DAG. Done means the on-pole test runs (not ignored) with 0 mismatches, the vdp-rk4 bench hashes stay the same, and the R4 probe is rerun. SPEC section 3 states the rule. If neither case explains a value, stop and give the owner the smallest expression that fails.
- R2: keep as it is.
- R3: first check `1/(1+exp(u))` at u = 800 on 56509d4, then state the limit in SPEC section 3.
- R4: accepted, see above.
- N1: no folding of other prefixes. Check that a `totalx=` line gets the `ignoredOption` warning.
- Parser questions: keep ODE0041 (Q1); a `"` active comment that ends in a backslash swallows the next line and warns ODE0076, as a `#` comment does (Q2); keep the error (Q3); keep full words (Q4); not now (Q5, Q6); no change (Q7); no (Q8). The rules that broke ties: a loud error never turns silent; a silent difference from XPPAUT gets a warning; no quirk known only from XPPAUT's GPL code is copied; nothing is built without a model that needs it.
- check-derivs: accept `crazy` as a documented limit of finite differences, keep the `/3` term, and close item 6c only if its row passes.
- Node limit: check whether pc-engine uses a four-slot Dir. If not, keep order 3 with 1M nodes. If it does, use order 4 with 2M nodes, record peak memory, and list a per-entry limit as the structural fix.
- These stay with the owner (rule 9): the zero-Hopf theta (to be compared with SPEC against the book page first; if the shipped coefficients are wrong, work stops and the owner hears first), Kuznetsov p. 359 with its sign and convention, the Hopf-Hopf sign table, the Dahlquist and Bjorck section number, and the missing Ermentrout book.
- R1 outcome (2026-11-19): stopped, the owner decides. Neither case in the rule explains all values. The 3691 count was taken with a test-only change in `multi.rs` (a zero guard on each block component in Faa di Bruno), not on HEAD. Three changes were tried one at a time:
  - A, both factors of a `Mul` term guarded when either may be non-finite: the 1313 values where the DAG gives NaN go to 0, and the values where the interpreter gives NaN go from 2378 to 2547.
  - B', a strong-zero product in the interpreter for a zero tangent component (`Scalar::zmul` and `zdiv` used in `Dual::term` and the `Div` tangent): 2378 goes to 1607, 1313 goes to 1342.
  - A and B' together: 1747 values remain, all with the DAG at +0 and the interpreter at NaN.

  The smallest one is `x'=-x+exp(-(2+x*x)/max(a-x-10,0))` with a = 1, Dir [U,U,U] at x = (0,0) with directions (1,0), (2,0) and (-1.5,0), output 0. Here `x*x` at x = 0 has zero first tangents, and `max` clamps to 0, so the quotient has infinite second-order tangents. The DAG multiplies the tangent components together first (Faa di Bruno), so a zero component cancels the infinite one before the partial `exp(-inf) = 0` is applied, and the result is +0. The interpreter nests one dual per direction, so it first forms a derivative of the partial that is exactly 0 and then multiplies it by an infinite tangent, and the result is NaN. The two orders are the same polynomial and differ only in IEEE arithmetic. Only a rule where a zero partial also cancels an infinite tangent closes the gap, and that rule changes on-pole Jacobian values at first order too (tried as B2: the tanh shapes went from 316 to 3216 differing values). That is a new convention, so it waits for the owner (rule 9). The on-pole test stays ignored. Patches, trace and dumps are in `~/xpp82-reports/r1-2026-11-19/`; nothing was committed.
- Parser Q2 outcome (2026-11-19): done. The lexer treats a backslash in an active comment (a line whose first token is `"`) as in a `#` comment: the next line gives no tokens, the swallowing chains, a blank or comment line gives no warning, and ODE0076 comes with the same two quick fixes. Text after the backslash gets no ODE0075, since it is part of the comment. XPPAUT 8.2.2 also exempts an active comment on line 1 of the main file (probe `backslash-active-comment-line1`, v@3=1), where PhaseCont used to join line 2 and fail with ODE0044 and ODE0039; it now reads line 2 as XPPAUT does. Four new probes (`-chain`, `-blank`, `-line1`, `-line1-comment`); `run.sh` with XPPAUT 8.2.2 gave 290 probes and 580 PASS. SPEC 8ae item 2, language.md and CHANGELOG updated.
- R3 outcome (2026-11-19): done, the limit is older than round 2. A scratch probe evaluated F, $A$ and the order 2 directional derivative of `1/(1+exp(u))`, `exp(-u)/(1+exp(-u))`, `tanh(u)`, `1/cosh(u)` and `sinh(u)/cosh(u)` at u = 700, 709, 710, 800 and -800 with the DAG and the fallback, on 56509d4 and on 565bd0d. Every NaN is the same on both commits (for example `1/(1+exp(u))` at u = 710 and 800 gives F = 0 and NaN for $A$ and Dir); the only differences are fallback zeros that 56509d4 gave as -0 and round 2 gives as +0. SPEC section 3 now states the limit ("Overflow at a finite argument"). No code change.
- Fallback quotient defect (found during R3, owner item, not fixed). The nested dual quotient in the fallback (`Dual::div`, `crates/pc-ir/src/interp.rs`) forms the tangent term from `q / o.re`. In nested duals the factor $1/b^k$ underflows to 0 while the tangent of $b$ is huge, so a cancelling term drops out and the result is finite but wrong by order 1. The DAG and the JIT match the closed forms within about 1 ulp at orders 2 to 5 for u from 100 to 700 (at order 1 both the DAG and the fallback underflow to 0 from u = 400, where the true value is about 1e-174). Fallback values against the exact value 0 for `sinh(u)/cosh(u)`:

  | Order | First wrong at | Example |
  |---|---|---|
  | 2 | u = 400 | -1 at u = 400 and 700 |
  | 3 | u = 300 | 1 at u = 300, -1 at u = 400 |
  | 4 | u = 240 | -1 at u = 240, 2 at u = 400 |
  | 5 | u = 150 | 1 at u = 150, 9 at u = 400 |

  For `1/(1+exp(u))`, whose order k derivative is $(-1)^k e^{-u}$ to within rounding, the fallback gives 0 at order 2 from u = 300 and the wrong sign at order 3 at u = 240 and 300; order 4 is off by a relative 1.5e-9 at u = 145 and wrong from u = 150, and order 5 has the wrong sign from u = 140. The default evaluator is the JIT (`Evaluator` empty or `"jit"`, or `phasecont.config("Codegen")`), so normal runs are not affected. The defect reaches `Evaluator="fallback"`, session replay with `--fallback`, and the derivative check, where the fallback is the oracle and can report a false mismatch. A fix in `Dual::div` would change fallback bits that `zero_sign.rs`, `zmul.rs`, the P38 guards and replay identity compare, so it waits for the owner.
- Node limit outcome (2026-11-19): done for `check_model`. pc-engine uses no four-slot Dir, but the advisor kept order 5 and asked for the limit to apply per entry. `check_model_with` now takes a DAG checkpoint before each directional mix and rolls back to it after the comparison (new crate-private `IrModel::rollback`, also used when an entry passes the limit), so each mix is built on the same base DAG and `MAX_NODES` (1M) applies to one entry at a time. `MAX_NODES`, `lower` and the JIT are unchanged. Test `the_node_limit_applies_to_each_directional_mix_on_its_own` sets the limit to the base plus the largest single mix through order 3; without the rollback more than 20 Dir rows are skipped, with it none. `pc-cli check-derivs` on the corpus models itoy and toy_ok (release build, 600 s cap, MATLAB running at the same time): 399 checks, 0 SKIP, 0 FAIL on both, 57 s wall and 153 to 156 MB peak each. Before: 279 SKIP in 9.4 s and 132 MB (toy_ok). Most of the added time is the Dir[U^5] JIT compile, which was a SKIP before and took 38.5 s alone in the node-limit measurement. No order 5 row disagreed with the fallback on these two models, so the fallback quotient defect above did not show; there is still no finite-difference row past order 3 to cross-check orders 4 and 5.
- Zero-Hopf theta outcome (2026-11-19): the shipped coefficients are not shown wrong, so work goes on; the owner signs off on which book reading is accepted (rule 9). Kuznetsov 4th ed. disagrees with itself by a factor of 2. Its normal form (8.64) and (8.65) uses monomial coefficients (G200 = g200/2). Lemma 8.10 (8.73) gives B = G200 and D = H110 - i w0 G300/G200, and the scaling to (8.78) gives theta + i vartheta = D/B, so theta = Re H110/G200. Theorem 8.6 (p. 372) prints theta(0) = Re h110/g200 = Re H110/(2 G200), and Ex. 8.4 (p. 417, Lorenz-84) uses that form: -126065/62051 = -2.0316. `dde-zh` (SPEC 8i) computes theta = Re g110/Re g200 with monomial g, which is the (8.78) form. Run on Lorenz-84 at the book's ZH point (a = 0.25, b = 4, the book's F and G, a zero-weight `c*delay(z,tau)` term so the DDE entry accepts the ODE): theta = -4.063270535527226, s = +1, g200 = -0.30885, equilibrium residual 4.4e-16. That is twice the book's printed value and equal to the (8.78) form; the BJK paper's printed -139.0315 (SPEC 8i, HR point 1) also matches our form. The factor does not change the sign of theta, and the ZH cases depend only on s and that sign. PhaseCont does not classify ZH cases, and the ODE `normal.rs::zero_hopf_at` outputs b, c, d and e but no theta. No code change.
- N1 outcome (2026-11-19): no folding of prefixed `@` keys, as decided. The new MATLAB test `TrajectoryTest/aPrefixedOptionIsNamedInTheWarning` loads `@ totalx=5, dtx=0.1` (XPPAUT reads them as `total` and `dt`) and checks that `simulate` gives `phasecont:simulate:ignoredOption` and names `totalx` and `dtx` as the file spells them. Full MATLAB suite on 50f63bb plus the test: 898 total, 889 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (all filtered by assumption).
- Round D outcome (2026-11-19): docs only, no code change. The evidence (our own models, XPPAUT output and logs) is in `~/xpp82-reports/item10-2026-11-19/`.
  - Item 10, delay history. Two models of our own, `x'=-delay(x,0.5)` with `@ delay=1, total=1`, one with the constant history `x(0)=1` and one with `x(0)=1-t`. By the method of steps the exact values are x(1) = 1/8 (constant history: x = 1 - t on [0, 1/2]) and x(1) = 1/24 (history 1 - t: x = 1 - 3t/2 + t^2/2 on [0, 1/2], x(1/2) = 3/8). XPPAUT results:

    | Version | History | x(1), dt 0.1 | x(1), dt 0.01 | Error, dt 0.1 | Error, dt 0.01 |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | 8.2.2 | 1 - t | 0.041267 | 0.041663 | 4.0e-4 | 4.1e-6 |
    | 8.2.2 | 1 | 0.124205 | 0.124992 | 8.0e-4 | 8.3e-6 |
    | 8.0 | 1 - t | 0.0300 | 0.040425 | 1.2e-2 | 1.2e-3 |
    | 8.0 | 1 | 0.1 | 0.1225 | 2.5e-2 | 2.5e-3 |

    Both versions converge to the exact values, 8.2.2 at about second order in dt and 8.0 at about first, so 8.0 is less accurate, not wrong. PhaseCont does not simulate a delay equation: the constant-history model fails in `lower` with `error[ir:delay]`, and the `1-t` model fails in the parser with ODE0023 (plus the ODE0054 warning). A future delay simulator should be tested against 1/8 and 1/24.
  - Item 5, history functions and `set`. A history function needs a time simulation of a delay equation, which no phase covers, so it is blocked until the owner schedules one. Where `set(a,b,c)` was first seen is not recorded. Probes on our own models (`set-probe/a` and `set-probe/b`): 8.2.2 refuses `aux s2=set(0,1,17)` with "Illegal use of set - variables only" and writes no output; with `aux s=set(x,0.5,2)` and `x'=-x` the aux column is 2 at every output time and x follows exp(-t). PhaseCont refuses both with ODE0039 (`function set is not declared`). What b does and whether `set` has side effects is not measured; that needs more 8.2.2 probes on our own models before any SPEC text.

Pointers added to SPEC section 13: phase reduction (no reference chosen), two-parameter LPC, PD and NS curves of delay cycles, and period-doubling branch switching on delay cycles. No phase numbers and no acceptance text. Today `phasecont.continuation` refuses any delay `Type` other than EP, H and LC with `phasecont:model:badOption` (`toolbox/+phasecont/continuation.m:285`).
  - 5.41 row recheck. No repo file holds the row, so the split is recorded here by manual page. A periodic orbit started from a Hopf point (XPPAUT 5.41 manual p. 69) is full: `Type="LC"` from an H point. A periodic orbit started from a fresh initial condition (p. 68) is partial: the engine takes the LC `"start": "orbit"` (SPEC section 10), and since round E item 8 (2026-11-19) an LC run in MATLAB also starts from a Trajectory or a struct with t, x and p, with the period from `Period` or from the latest close return of the orbit to its last point (SPEC section 11, "Limit cycles from a simulation"; `tests/CycleOrbitTest.m`). The row stays partial until the owner signs off the detection rule (Open questions, "Detected period of an LC start").
- Round E outcome (2026-11-19). All three items are built and tested on branch `xpp82-integrate2`, local only.
  - Item 8, an LC run from a simulated orbit: 3a5d575 (SPEC section 11, "Limit cycles from a simulation"). The detected period is an open owner decision (Open questions, "Detected period of an LC start").
  - argK formals: cd95353, b33cd6c, merged in 53ea921, documented in a495d47 (SPEC section 2). XPPAUT 8.2.2 binds a declared `argK` by name, and we do the same. It also stops on a function body that uses an `argK` the function does not declare; we keep the manual's positional reading there instead (argument K, or ODE0050 beyond the parameter count). That difference is recorded in SPEC section 2. Settled 2026-11-19: the owner chose to keep the positional reading, and each such use now gives warning ODE0078, which says XPPAUT 8.2.2 stops there.

Rulings: a repeated name keeps its first place and later repeats are dropped with ODE0077; a parameter, a fixed quantity or an unknown name is dropped with ODE0077; a list left empty gives no list; `Output` is a Dependent property and is an empty table (one row per time) without a list; the run stores only the aux columns the list names; a non-finite aux value is stored as computed; a Trajectory whose store has no aux columns (a session from before round E) raises `phasecont:simulate:noAuxColumns` when the list names an aux quantity; continuation ignores the list and warns `phasecont:continuation:onlyIgnored`; ODE0033 is retired and its number stays reserved; the writer writes one `only` line per statement as read (settled 2026-11-19: the owner asked the advisor, who kept it, since the output reads back to the same list and warnings; no code change). The problem JSON key `"aux": true` is sent only when the list names an aux quantity.
