Checked: with the profile's target directory the run printed "is clean" and "staged" (before the fix the same run stopped on three `cranelift-codegen/.../out/*.rs` paths), and a stage of `/proc/nope` printed the failure line and exited 1.
- Round F (2026-11-19): the owner asked the advisor to settle decisions F-0 to F-6 in SPEC section 8af, and the advisor chose option (a) in each (the owner may reverse any). The section now states them as rules, with these conditions: F-0, the codec is in Rust behind one appended ABI entry, and a `pckern` older than the toolbox raises `phasecont:engine:load` instead of calling past the function table; F-1, an LC start from a thinned Trajectory re-runs without `thin`, warns `phasecont:continuation:thinRerun` before the re-run, uses the same Timeout and Ctrl-C path, and refuses with `badStart` without `Record.Problem`; F-2, a drawn line is thinned only above 4 times the axes width in pixels; F-3, compression is on by default with `Compress=false`, and the CHANGELOG says that the 1.0.0-preview reader stops on a version 2 file with "unsupported session version 2; this build reads version 1" (checked at tag `v1.0.0-preview` in `sessionCodec.m` and `session.rs`); F-4, ODE0047 becomes a warning; F-5, a recursive model skips the AOT path and runs on the JIT, and the session record names the evaluator that ran; F-6, the GPU stack has its own allocation and batching shrinks to fit, models without recursion keep their kernel source and hashes, and a frame too large for one member raises `gpuLaunch`. The section also states the aux columns: the flat store row is `[t, x(n), aux(naux)]` with stride n + 1 + naux under `"aux": true`, and the Thin rule counts every stored column, aux included. Build order P40 to P44.
- P40 done (2026-11-19): IVP and ITER runs store their rows in one flat buffer of `[t, x, aux]` per row (`pc-engine/src/rows.rs`), which doubles from one row and so holds at most twice the rows it has. `run_copy` reads the rows straight from that buffer, and the rows and their bits equal those of the base commit e58b9d4 (`pc-ffi/tests/flat_rows.rs`, with and without aux, and a cancelled run keeps a prefix). The CLI prints IVP and ITER points without the `h` key, since the flat row has no step size; the owner may reverse this. The C ABI and the MATLAB API do not change.
- P41 done (2026-11-19): `simulate(..., Thin = tol)` and the IVP key `"thin"` (SPEC section 8af-2). The engine thins the flat store in place at every end of the run (`Rows::thin` in `pc-engine/src/rows.rs`, a Ramer-Douglas-Peucker search on an explicit stack with a mask of one byte a row), and poll reports 0 rows until the end. `Trajectory.Thin` holds the tolerance; a session reads it back from the stored problem text. An LC start from a thinned Trajectory re-runs without the key (F-1). Tests: 7 unit tests in `rows.rs`, 3 ABI tests in `pc-ffi/tests/thin.rs` and 8 in `tests/ThinTest.m`. Rows kept, measured through MATLAB: Hopf normal form over [0 40], 567 rows, 141 at 1e-2, 499 at 1e-3, all 567 at 1e-6; Lorenz over [0 20] at RelTol 1e-9, 3140 rows, 338, 1105 and 3140; the stiff scalar model x' = -1000(x - cos t) over [0 6], 4673 rows, 25, 77 and 1715. The search costs O(n^2) row checks in the worst case, when each split keeps a row next to an end of its segment; the unit test thins 10^6 rows of a chirp.
- P42 done (2026-11-20): drawn-line thinning (SPEC section 8af-3) in `toolbox/+phasecont/+internal/drawThin.m`, called by `Trajectory.plot` and `Branch.plot`. One MarkedClean listener on the axes, kept in the line's application data, redraws the line when the limits, the scales, the pixel size or the view change; the 0.35-pixel cell rule is vectorized, so no engine call is needed (F-0). A map's dots and a 3-D view are drawn exact. `UserData.PhaseContRows` holds the drawn rows. A `uiaxes` in a `uigridlayout` takes its new pixel size only after a pause and a `drawnow` following a figure resize, so the listener catches up on the next redraw. Tests: 10 in `tests/DrawThinTest.m` (no MEX). Full MATLAB suite on 2026-11-20: 940 total, 931 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. A 700 by 500 pixel figure (axes 542.5 by 407.5 pixels) draws 2714 of 10^6 rows of y = t^2. Owner items, not built: exported vector figures and `print` carry the thinned vertices on screen at export time; the cell is in MATLAB logical pixels, so under display scaling it is larger in physical pixels; there is no option to turn the thinning off, since one would change the public API.
- P43 done (2026-11-20): session compression (SPEC section 8af-4). The codec is in Rust (`pc-ffi/src/xor1.rs`) behind one appended ABI entry, `codec`, and MATLAB reaches it through `pckern("codec", ...)` (F-0). A column is coded against the prediction 2·v(i-1) - v(i-2) under the NaN rule, decoding is strict, a block is written as xor1 only when that is shorter than f64le, and a file is version 2 only when some block is xor1. Readers in Rust and MATLAB accept versions 1 and 2; `Compress = false` writes version 1 (F-3). Sizes, measured through MATLAB on the files of the 2026-11-18 measurement: the ML diagram 1,614,485 to 1,336,539 bytes (0.828, 1.21 times), the 5127-row trajectory 165,807 to 140,528 bytes (0.848, 1.18 times), both inside the 1.15 to 1.8 times of the SPEC "Ratio" line. Fixtures: `tests/+fixtures/xor1_block.json` and `ml_small_v2.pcsession.json`, which is the Rust `to_text()` of `ml_small.pcsession.json` (8 xor1 blocks, 5 f64le, 7503 against 8483 bytes); a Rust test and `SessionFormatTest/theVersion2FixtureReadsAsTheVersion1Fixture` check that both read to the same session. The gateway check for a `pckern` older than the toolbox is tested with the test-only `pckern("debug_struct_size", n)`, which shortens the table the gateway trusts; no real older DLL was loaded. The 1.0.0-preview refusal was run on `ml_small_v2.pcsession.json`: the preview `pc-cli session replay`, built from `git archive v1.0.0-preview`, exits 2 with "pc-cli: unsupported session version 2; this build reads version 1", and the preview `sessionCodec.readFile` raises `phasecont:session:badFile` with "unsupported session version 2; this build reads version 1". Checks on 2026-11-20: cargo 2677 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored, with fmt, clippy and deny clean; bench hashes unchanged; full MATLAB suite 945 total, 936 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (started before the version 2 fixture test was added), then SessionFormatTest 26 of 26 with it.
- P44 in progress (2026-11-20), stage 1 (parser, IR, interpreter and DAG evaluator). Baseline first: `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/recursion_baseline.rs` (22718f3, 87d9792) pins, for every test model at 692088a, the node count and op hash after each entry, the emitted C, the tape length and two GPU kernel texts; JIT code and frame sizes sit in a second golden file checked only on a host with the same ISA fingerprint. The advisor settled the open design points, and SPEC section 8af-5 now states them as rules for the owner to confirm or reverse: (a) a recursive body reads states, parameters, `t` and delay slots as globals, and a call differentiates through a unit that returns its value and derivatives from one frame (changed 2026-11-20, see below); (b) a lazy `If` op, distinct from `Select`, with a memo per frame; (c) an `if` whose arm reaches a call lowers to `If` in the main scope too, and every other `if` stays `Select`. Also: one DAG arena with an `Arg(k)` leaf instead of a DAG per function (a change to the SPEC's first wording), call arguments in a hash-consed side table, a recursive call in a table formula refused, the cap error as its own engine error mapped to `numeric` (not `NotFinite`, which continuation retries as a failed step), and the JIT, tape, C and GPU emitters refusing a `Call` until their stages land. Call counts of JacU and second derivatives at n = 100 and n = 1000 go here when measured.
- P44 derivative cost (2026-11-20). The first design, partial units built from the symbolic derivative of a recursive body, makes about n² calls per JacU column and n³ per second derivative at depth n, since each partial calls the lower orders again. The owner asked for a fast tool, so before any recursion code was written the advisor replaced it: a tangent unit (JacU, JacP) or jet unit (Dir rows) returns the value and every derivative component of a call from one frame, as the interpreter's dual numbers do, and the tangent body takes each inner primal call as output 0 of the tangent call. Cost is n + 1 calls per column at depth n, the same as the value. A new op `Out(call, j)` reads output j ≥ 1. A cache of call results was rejected because the GPU kernel has no hash table. Two costs remain and are not planned: branching recursion such as `fib(n)` grows like φ^n calls, derivatives or not, and an entry that needs both a call's value and its derivative at top level (the JacU of `x' = x*pw(x, n)`) runs both calls, about 2(n + 1). SPEC section 8af-5 states the rule and the call-count tests.
- P44 progress (2026-11-20). Done and committed locally: the DAG arena with `Arg`, `Call`, `If` and `Out`, checkpoint and restore, and the frame machine with the depth cap (84628f5, 1101566); restore now undoes every derived entry set after the checkpoint, including an overwrite, which the first version left in place (fe2e33f, found by a side review); tangent units in `diff::d` (9f29284), with tests in `crates/pc-ir/tests/recursion_diff.rs` that check n + 1 calls at n = 100 and n = 1000, a bitwise match against the dual recursion for `pw` and for an `exp` body, an active set that alternates down the recursion (two units), two calls in one body and a call inside a call argument (both at the primal call count), a call that only feeds a condition (no tangent unit), and a cap hit during the build followed by restore. Measured: JacU of `pw(x, n)` makes n + 1 calls at n = 100 and n = 1000. Fixed 2026-11-20 (51144a5, ced0ebf): a static activity analysis (a least fixpoint over the call closure, run at build time, so no generated code tests a tangent for 0 and GPU warps take no extra branch) marks a counter such as n inactive, so its derivative is 0 with no calls; a JacU or JacP entry that reaches a function without a body is now an `Unsupported` IR error naming the function, rolled back so the same request after `set_body` works (this settles the open question of a silent NaN); a Dir entry of a model with a call is refused until the jet units exist, instead of a NaN form. Fixed 2026-11-20: a body is set once (a second `set_body` returns false), and a derivative through a function with several outputs, an `Out` node or a formal outside its body is an `Unsupported` error with a named reason instead of a NaN (tests in `crates/pc-ir/tests/missing_body_entry.rs`). Still open: the GPU emitters have no `Call` yet, so warp divergence from recursion depth is not measured (Nsight Compute and Systems are installed, see `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`). Next: jet units in `multi.rs`, then the interpreter, the engine error, the lowering and the ODE0047 flip.
- P44 jet units (2026-11-20, 778404c). A Dir entry of a model whose right-hand side reaches a `Call` now builds through jet units in `multi.rs` instead of being refused. A jet unit of f takes the arguments of f plus the nonzero components of each argument and returns all 2^k components of the call from one frame (SPEC section 8af-5). Units are keyed by f, the order, whether f reads a covered variable and the slot kinds, and the component mask of each formal, so a request builds only the components it needs. The Dir builder rebuilds the top level in node order, so the Dir row of `x' = x*pw(x, n)` makes n + 1 calls, not 2(n + 1). The advisor chose these three points on 2026-11-20 and the owner may reverse them. Tests in `crates/pc-ir/tests/recursion_jet.rs` (n = 100 and n = 1000 in the call counts, `Full` slots in the bitwise and refusal cases): closed forms of `pw` along `[U]`, `[U, U]` and `[U, U, U]` with n + 1 calls, a body that reads a state, a call in a condition and under `exp` and `pow`, zero masks that make no call and register no unit, a bitwise match against the body inlined eight levels deep for orders 1 to 5 and mixed slots, and refusals through a missing body and through a unit with several outputs. A Dir entry through a unit with several outputs or without a body stays an `Unsupported` error, as it was at 8cf86a0 (then for any call), and now names the function, as JacU and JacP do since 79e72e8. A ninth test, added after review, checks an active set that alternates down the recursion (two jet units per request, n + 1 calls). MATLAB cannot reach the jet units yet, since no lowering emits `Call`, so the MATLAB run below only checks that nothing else regressed, and the bench does not exercise Dir. Also changed (57eee2a), not yet run on Windows (needs a push): for the two Windows test failures of CI run 44782912130 on 8cf86a0, the golden labels in `recursion_baseline.rs` now use `/` on every host, and `msl_syntax.rs` passes `-D_ALLOW_COMPILER_AND_STL_VERSION_MISMATCH` so the MSVC STL accepts the older clang. Every other job of that run passed, including matlab on macOS. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2746 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored, before the ninth test; `recursion_jet` then gives 9 passed in its file, with the alternating test) and `cargo deny check` ok; the golden (`recursion_baseline`) passes and the vdp-rk4 bench hashes are unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1); the full MATLAB suite (`buildtool test`, 2026-11-20, after restaging pckern) ran 946 tests: 937 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. Not pushed. Next: the interpreter, the engine error, the lowering (every body set before any derivative) and the ODE0047 flip.
- P44 stage 1 end to end (2026-11-20, 042e414 to c59e93a and the lowering commit after them). `ModelInfo` lists the recursive functions of a model (042e414). A new engine error, `CallLimit`, reports a call past the cap and maps to the `numeric` ABI code, so no new `PC_ERR` code was added (817b295). The JIT and AOT evaluators refuse a model with a recursive call at build with `EngineError::Model` naming the function, and only the fallback evaluator runs it (c530156). The interpreter runs a recursive model on an explicit task and value stack, so depth does not use the native stack; frames of recursive functions count toward the 1,000,000 cap, and stack growth goes through `try_reserve`, so running out of memory is the cap error; models without recursion keep the old walker bit for bit (c59e93a). The lowering now emits `Call`, `If` and `Arg` for a recursive function, sets every body before any derivative is built, and refuses a recursive call in a table formula with `BadTable`; ODE0047 is a warning whose text says the function is evaluated with at most 1,000,000 active calls. Tests: `crates/pc-ir/tests/recursion_lower.rs` (13 tests from `.ode` source: `fact` exact to 22!, `sum` at 999,999 levels and one past the cap, `pw` with F, JacU and Dir[U,U] within 1 ulp of the closed forms for n = 0 to 30, mutual recursion, a body that reads a state, n + 1 calls for JacU and Dir[U,U] at n = 100 and 1000, nested `if`, a call shared by two arms, an untaken arm with no call, the table refusal, a delay inside a body, and a formal used as an exponent under Dir), `recursive_model_runs_only_on_the_fallback` in `crates/pc-engine/tests/evaluators.rs`, `crates/pc-codegen/tests/recursive.rs` (C emitter refuses), the ODE0047 test in `crates/pc-ode/src/check/tests.rs`, and `ModelTest/recursiveFunctionWarns`. Owner items, not built: (1) a wrapper function whose argument is a recursive call evaluates that argument eagerly, as every non-recursive call does; (2) the tape and GPU paths report `unsupportedModel` for a recursive model where SPEC section 8af-5 says `model`; (3) a cap hit during `simulate` shows as a stop reason plus a warning, not as an error; (4) `rhsGaps.m` turns `phasecont:engine:numeric` into a gap, so a cap hit there is not reported; (5) the normal-form code catches evaluator errors and goes on (`hopfcurve.rs` lines 288 to 318, `lpcurve.rs` 88 to 105, `mapcurve.rs` near line 566), so a cap hit there gives NaN coefficients instead of an error; (6) `Fallback::try_eval` fills its output with NaN before it returns an error; (7) the Dir rows of the DAG evaluator and the interpreter can differ by up to 2 ulp, as before this work. The GPU emitters still have no `Call`, so warp divergence from recursion depth is not measured. Checks on 2026-11-20: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2776 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; the golden (`recursion_baseline`) passes unchanged, since the recursive engine model is built from inline source and not added to `pc-engine/tests/models`; vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1); full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern, 947 total, 938 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. A review found that the formal-exponent case of `machine_matches_the_dag_on_recursive_models` used the exponent 17, which the interpreter sends to `pow` either way, so it could not catch a loss of the fix that clears the constant flag of a recursive formal; it now uses 7, and with the fix removed the test fails. Acceptance rows of section 8af-5 against tests: `fact`, `sum` and the cap, `pw` with JacU and the second derivative, `ev`/`od`, the body that reads a state, the n + 1 call counts, `h`, `g` and the untaken arm are the tests of the same names in `recursion_lower.rs`; the baseline row is `recursion_baseline.rs`; the F-4 row is `ModelTest/recursiveFunctionWarns`. The AOT-note row is stage 3 and the GPU rows are stage 4. Owner items (2) and (3) break SPEC rule bullets of 8af-5 (Errors and Depth cap), not acceptance rows; for (2) the error-code list of SPEC section 10 maps "the model uses something a kernel cannot lower" to `unsupportedModel`, so the two sections disagree and the owner picks one. A model built with no `Evaluator` uses `phasecont.config("Codegen")`, which is "jit" by default, so a recursive model raises the ODE0047 warning and then `phasecont:engine:model`; the refusal text now says that only the fallback evaluator runs recursive functions, and `recursiveFunctionWarns` checks it. Picking the fallback on its own for a recursive model would change public behavior, so it is an owner item (8). The same acceptance rows also pass on the interpreter that the fallback evaluator runs: `interpreter_gives_pw_and_its_derivatives_to_1_ulp` and `interpreter_reaches_the_cap_and_one_more_fails` in `crates/pc-engine/tests/evaluators.rs`. Items (4), (5) and (6) also break the Errors bullet of 8af-5, which says a cap hit is reported as an error and never turned into NaN; so the rule bullets not met are (2), (3), (4), (5) and (6). Item (5) needs no owner decision, since the SPEC already rules on it, but it is not fixed in stage 1 and stays unmet.
- P44 item (5) fixed (2026-11-20). A recursion cap error (`CallLimit`, which out-of-memory in the interpreter also maps to) at a site that used to turn an evaluator error into NaN, a missing value, a warning, a failed locate trial or an unrefined point now ends the branch with the stop reason `Error(CallLimit)`, keeps the points computed before it, and writes no NaN. This is the observable that a cap hit inside a step already gave. Every other error keeps its old path through one helper, `keep_call_limit` in `error.rs`, so branches of models without recursion do not change. Sites: the bounds landing in `palc.rs` (the bare point, whose tests would be NaN, is dropped), the locate trial and `refine_zero` in `locate.rs`, `refine_specials`, the map and cycle coefficient passes, the codim 2 tests and annotations of the Hopf, fold, map PD and NS, LPC, PD, NS and homoclinic curves, and the delay Hopf passes (`l1_at`, `l2_at`, `hh_at`, `omega2_at`, `special_coefficients`) and `omega_floor`. `Problem::accept` now returns a `Result`, and a cap error from it ends the run. A pass after the last step ends through `end_with_error` in `branch.rs`: a normal end (`MaxSteps`, `Bounds` and the rest) becomes `Error`, so the cap hit is never hidden, while a cancelled run stays cancelled and an earlier error stays the stop, with the new error kept as a warning. Tests: `crates/pc-engine/tests/call_limit.rs` (51 tests, a cap test paired with an other-error test for each path, through the fault-injection double `tests/common/inject.rs`) and the three `end_with_error` unit tests in `branch.rs`. With `is_call_limit` forced to false, 26 of the 51 fail. Limits: a cap hit at or before the first point of a run (for a homoclinic curve, also in the start's Newton solve) still returns `Err`, since there is no branch yet; `continue_equilibrium` in `dde/branch.rs` has no partial branch and returns the cap error as `Err`, as its sibling calls already did (a test pins this); the `accept` sites of the LPC, PD and NS curves use the helper but make no model call (the assembly is cached by the bits of x), so no injection test reaches them. `finish_stop` in `run.rs` still maps a stop of `Error` to `RunState::Done`, so a continuation run that ends on a cap hit reports `Done` with stop reason "Error". That is owner item (3) widened from `simulate` to continuation runs; the one-arm change (`Ok(StopReason::Error(e)) => RunState::Failed(e)`) is ready when the owner rules on it. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2830 passed, 0 failed, 14 ignored), `cargo deny check` ok, the golden `recursion_baseline` passes, vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1). Full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern: 947 total, 938 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. The rule bullets of 8af-5 not met are now (2), (3), (4) and (6).
- P44 stage 2, recursion on the JIT (2026-11-21). `CompiledModel` compiles the entries of a recursive model with `compile_with_calls`. The public `compile`, `compile_f32`, `compile_native` and `bench_native` still refuse a `Call`, so the ensemble tape and the native ensemble path do not change until stage 4. A `Call` lowers to a call of the helper `pc_jit_call`, which runs the unit on the frame machine (`pc_ir::machine::run_unit`, with thread-local buffers, a sparse reset of the global store and the same 1,000,000 cap) and reports a failure through a thread-local error that `CompiledModel::call` reads after the entry returns. The helper catches a panic and turns it into `Malformed`. An entry with a live `If` lowers to branches, so the untaken arm makes no call. Entries without calls keep their code byte for byte, and the golden passes unchanged. Each lazy entry compile keeps its own `Arc` clone of the `Dag` (tens of KB for the test models); one shared snapshot is not possible while entries compile lazily and the DAG grows. `finish()` refuses a recursive model only for AOT, so owner item (8) is gone: the default evaluator runs a recursive model. Owner item (3) is built for the cap error and for model errors: `finish_stop` ends a run whose branch stopped on `Error(CallLimit)` or `Error(Model)` as `Failed`, as SPEC 8af-5 (Depth cap) says. MATLAB then raises `phasecont:engine:numeric` for the cap and `phasecont:engine:model` for a model error, and does not return the points before the stop; `pc-cli` keeps them in its output and exits 1 (it exited 0 before on a cap hit). A NaN, a Newton failure or a bad problem still ends `Done` with Stop "Error", which TrajectoryTest, MapIterateTest, CobwebTest and MapPhasePlaneTest pin. The model build in pc-ffi now fails on a `Model` error in its warm evaluation and still ignores a NaN or a cap hit at the declared start. On a helper error the entry runs to its end with a NaN in its own slot and `call` returns the error, so `out` is not valid then; that is the shape of open item (6). The unmet rule bullets of 8af-5 are now (2), (4) and (6). Timing in release, ns per call, JIT against the DAG evaluator: `pw` F at n = 30 5227 against 5181, at n = 1000 153408 against 168031; `sum` F at n = 10000 1420878 against 1952146. Both run the same frame machine, so the JIT gains only in the code around the call. Tests: `crates/pc-jit/tests/recursion.rs` (the 8af-5 oracle corpus on the JIT), the flipped JIT arm and the DDE and cap tests in `crates/pc-engine/tests/evaluators.rs`, the `finish_stop` unit test in `run.rs`, `a_recursion_cap_hit_exits_1_and_keeps_the_branch_points` in `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs`, and `ModelTest/recursiveFunctionWarns` and the new `ModelTest/recursionCapRaisesNumeric` (JIT and fallback, `rhs` and `simulate`). Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2864 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; the golden `recursion_baseline` passes unchanged; vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1). Full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern: 947 total, 938 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete; it did not include `recursionCapRaisesNumeric`, which was added after, and `runtests('ModelTest')` then gave 30 passed, 0 failed.
- P44 stage 3, AOT (2026-11-21, 8a44a16). A recursive model built with `Evaluator = "aot"`, or with `Codegen` set to `"aot"`, runs on the JIT without starting the C compiler, and the warning `phasecont:aot:recursive` names the functions once per Model object (F-5, D1, D2). `model_info` has a new key `"recursive"`, the reached recursive functions once each in first-use order, so MATLAB decides before it calls `mex` (D3, D4); a declared function that no entry calls makes no unit and is not listed. The note is raised after the parse warnings, so `lastwarn` after the build reads it. A session records the evaluator that ran, so a replay names the JIT. The C emitter's `Recursive` text and the engine's AOT refusal now say that the JIT runs the model instead of "not yet". Tests: `info_lists_the_reached_recursive_functions` in `crates/pc-ffi/tests/model.rs`, the refusal text in `crates/pc-codegen/tests/recursive.rs`, and `AotTest/recursiveModelRunsOnTheJitWithOneNote` (exactly one note per new Model, a hook that fails if a compile starts, `rhs` and `simulate` equal to the JIT, and the `Codegen` route) and `AotTest/recursiveSessionRecordsAndReplaysOnTheJit`. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2865 passed, 0 failed, 15 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; `recursion_baseline` passes unchanged; vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1). Full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern (`scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/nuken/phasecont-stage/bin/win64`, `buildtool mex`, then `buildtool test` with the LaTeX PATH line): 950 total, 941 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete.
- P44 stage 4a, recursion on the CPU ensemble tape (2026-11-21). `Tape::build` now takes `Arg`, `Call`, `If` and `Out` and builds one unit per reached recursive function (root range, arity, outputs). The tape executor runs a call on a frame stack held in the per-worker `Slab`, grown with `try_reserve`, and a global call node runs at most once per right-hand side. Both `CpuTape<f64>` and `CpuTape<f32>` run it; there is no f32 JIT call path. A member whose right-hand side goes past 1,000,000 frames (owner decision E1) ends with the new status `recursionDepth`, code 6, at its last accepted time, with NaN states after it; the other members go on with the same bits as a run of the call-free model. The failure is a flag in the `Slab` that the integrator tests after each evaluation and before `all_finite` and `reject_state`, so the NaN in the failed scratch outputs never reaches a stored state. The ensemble `native` gate in pc-ffi now takes the compiled `F` only for a call-free tape, so a recursive model runs on the tape. Code 6 reaches `ens_poll` counts (`recursion_depth`), `ens_copy`, the `pc-cli` names and help, `phasecont.ensemble` (Status "recursionDepth" with the `memberStopped` warning) and `EnsembleResult`. Until stage 4b, `ens_start` refuses a recursive model on the CUDA, OpenCL and Metal backends with `Model` (`phasecont:engine:model`), before it probes for a device, so the ID is the same on a machine without that GPU. The test reads the DAG, not the tape, so a recursive model the tape rejects for another reason (for example `erf`) gets the same code; its message names that construct instead of pointing to the cpu backend, since `Backend = "cpu"` refuses that model too when the run is polled ("uses erf, which the ensemble engine cannot run"). Two pc-ffi tests cover both. The constructs the tape lacks are the same for every ensemble model, with or without calls: `erf`, `erfc`, `lgamma`, `besselj`, `bessely`, `polygamma`, tables, table slopes and directional-derivative inputs. Stage 4b emits the GPU kernels from the same tape, so a recursive model that uses one of them runs no ensemble on any backend (owner item: add these to the tape, or leave them as a known gap). `Backend = "auto"`, the default, tries CUDA, OpenCL, Metal and then the CPU, so on a machine with a GPU it gets the same error and a recursive model needs `Backend = "cpu"` until stage 4b; without a GPU it runs on the CPU (not covered by a test). The three GPU hosts keep their own refusal (`unsupportedModel`) behind it for callers that skip `ens_start`, such as `pc-cli ensemble`, and the kernel emitter refuses `Call` by name. For the GPU paths this meets stage 1 owner item (2) as SPEC 8af-5 states it; the error-code list of SPEC section 10 still maps a kernel refusal to `unsupportedModel`, which now covers only those inner paths. Tests: `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/recursion_cpu.rs` (a member past the cap beside two that finish, RK4 and DP5, double and single, bit-equal to the call-free model; a member that reaches the cap mid-run keeps t = 0.2 on RK4, and on DP5 in both precisions stops before x = 2/3 with no rejected step and no NaN in a stored sample; a map member; code 6; an ignored timing test), the tape unit tests and the call proptest in `tape.rs` (its regression seed is in `crates/pc-ensemble/proptest-regressions/tape.txt`), `a_recursive_model_runs_and_counts_the_member_past_the_cap` and `a_gpu_backend_refuses_a_recursive_model_with_the_model_code` in `crates/pc-ffi/tests/ensemble.rs`, `a_member_past_the_frame_cap_is_named_recursion_depth` in `crates/pc-cli/tests/ensemble.rs`, and `EnsembleTest/recursionDepthStopsOneMemberWithAWarning` and `EnsembleTest/aGpuBackendRefusesARecursiveModel`. The DP5 test and the GPU refusal test each fail when their code path is switched off. A cancel now reaches a member inside one long recursive right-hand side: the tape executor reads the cancel flag every 65,536 calls (`CANCEL_POLL`) and fails the evaluation, and `run_cpu` checks the flag after each step of the parallel loop, so the run returns `Cancelled` and never a result whose members read `recursionDepth`. Tests: `a_set_cancel_flag_stops_a_long_evaluation` in `tape.rs` and `a_cancel_inside_a_long_recursive_right_hand_side_returns_cancelled` (fib(60), 1 and 4 members, flag set after 100 ms from a second thread) in `recursion_cpu.rs`; with the poll removed the unit test fails and the run test hangs, and with the check after the loop removed the run test fails. Memory: the frame stack lives in the `Slab` of each rayon worker for one run and is dropped at its end, so the peak is the number of workers times the deepest stack of the run (one frame holds the formals, the live values and the resume point of its unit). `try_reserve` turns a refused allocation into `recursionDepth`, but on Linux with overcommit an allocation that the kernel grants and later cannot back is not seen. The stage 2 thread-local machine of `run_unit` drops its buffers after a run past 65,536 frames, 262,144 tasks or 1,048,576 values (`too_big` in `pc-ir/src/machine.rs`), so it does not pin memory either. Owner item: the DAG evaluator and the stage 2 JIT helper do not read a cancel flag inside one evaluation, so a `Timeout` or a cancel of a front-end run (simulate, continuation) on a deeply recursive model takes effect only after the current evaluation ends; the 1,000,000-frame cap bounds its depth but not its call count (fib(60) runs for hours). Passing a flag into them is not built. Timing in release, ns per right-hand side, tape f64, tape f32 and the DAG evaluator: `pw` at n = 30 571, 538 and 5192, at n = 1000 18141, 17461 and 164658; `sum` at n = 10000 184531, 188913 and 1950194, at n = 100000 1909859, 1891277 and 21737353. The tape is about 9 to 11 times faster than the frame machine that the JIT helper of stage 2 also runs, so the JIT helper could run the tape instead (not built). Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2889 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; `recursion_baseline` passes unchanged; vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1), at 0.258 s single and 0.234 s double for 100,000 members. Full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern (`scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/nuken/phasecont-stage/bin/win64`, `buildtool mex`, then `buildtool test` with the LaTeX PATH line): 952 total, 943 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete.
- P44 stage 4b part 1, the C-dialect trampoline (2026-11-21). For a tape with units, `kernel_source_in` now emits the call tape as one switch loop per right-hand side (`emit_calls_rhs` in `tape.rs`), with frames on a per-member stack in `PC_GLOBAL`, so no dialect needs recursion (OpenCL 1.2 and Metal have none) and CUDA does not use its small native stack. A frame holds `1 + flen + nflags` reals (resume slot, formals and live values, and one flag slot per call that both arms of an `if` share); in single precision the slots are 4 bytes. The stack of one member is 4,096 frames of the largest unit (G1), `PC_STK` in the kernel text and `kernel::stack_len` on the host. The kernel takes the stack as a trailing argument (`stk + m * PC_STK` per member, Metal `[[buffer(12)]]`), every call of `pc_rhs`, `pc_hinit` and `pc_dp5_step` passes `stk` and a failure word `cerr`, and a failure ends the member with `PC_RECURSION_DEPTH` (6): RK4 and maps at the step, DP5 after the non-finite check and before the step-size test, so it is never a rejected step. A global call that re-enters itself ends with `recursionDepth` too, where the CPU tape recurses to its cap. Call-free tapes keep their kernel text byte for byte, so `recursion_baseline` and `kernel_baseline` pass without a bless. The shim harness passes a stack buffer (NaN-filled, kept across launches) when `stack_len > 0`. Tests: `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/recursion_shim.rs` (11 tests, the kernel compiled as C against the CPU tape at the 4,096-frame cap, same bits, statuses and counts: `fib`, `ev`/`od` with different arities, a body that reads a state, an argument-free call inside a body, a call shared by both arms, a global call that re-enters itself, exactly 4,096 frames, a DP5 and an RK4 member that reach the cap mid-run, a map member, the stack size, and every dialect, integrator, precision and layout passing the stack); with `PC_REQUIRE_CC=1` none is skipped. SPEC 8af-5 has a paragraph "As built in stage 4b". The CUDA, OpenCL and Metal hosts still refuse a recursive model (`ens_start` with `Model`) until each is wired. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2903 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1). MATLAB not rerun, since nothing MATLAB reaches changed. Next: the CUDA host, then OpenCL, Metal and the Nsight check.
- P44 stage 4b, recursion on the CUDA backend (2026-11-21). The CUDA host no longer refuses a model with a `Call`: it allocates one stack buffer of `kernel::stack_len` reals per member of a batch and passes it after the parameter stride. Batching counts the stack as its own cost, outside the 256 KiB scratch rule, and shrinks the batch until the stacks fit device memory; when the stack of one member does not fit, the run stops with `gpuLaunch` naming the function, the frame size and the device memory. `Backend::call_frames` gives the cap of each backend (CPU 1,000,000, CUDA 4,096, none for OpenCL and Metal), and the FFI gate and the self-test read it. `pc-cli gpu selftest` has a new tier `recursion` on backends that run calls (k = 1, 2, 7, 64, 1,000 and 4,095 against x0·e^-1, plus a member at the backend's cap that must end with `recursionDepth`); `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` keeps its four checks. OpenCL and Metal still refuse a recursive model with `phasecont:engine:model`. Tests: `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/recursion_cuda.rs` on the RTX 5060 (the seven shim cases, a member that reaches the cap mid-run, a map member and batches of two, in both precisions and both layouts, bit for bit against the CPU tape at 4,096 frames for RK4 and maps, and to 1e-3 single and 1e-6 double for DP5, whose CUDA `pow` differs), selftest unit tests for the new tier (a runner that does not stop at the cap fails it, OpenCL and Metal have no such tier), pc-ffi tests (CUDA takes a recursive model, OpenCL and Metal refuse it), and `tests/EnsembleTest.m` `cudaStopsAMemberPastTheKernelStack` (k = 4,095 and 4,096 in single precision). Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2910 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; on the RTX 5060 `recursion_cuda`, `cuda_global` and `device_selftest` pass, and `pc-cli gpu selftest --backend cuda` passes in both precisions (recursion tier worst error 6.68e-8 single, 1.72e-11 double, `bit_exact` true); vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1); full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern and `buildtool mex`, 953 total, 944 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (the Metal skips). Next: OpenCL (4c), Metal (4d), the Nsight check (4e).
- P44 stage 4c, recursion on the OpenCL backend (2026-11-21). The OpenCL host no longer refuses a model with a `Call`: it allocates one stack buffer of `kernel::stack_len` reals per member of a batch, clears it on the device and passes it as kernel argument 12, after the parameter stride. `check_alloc` holds the stack buffer to the device's largest allocation and its memory like the other buffers, and batching counts it as in 4b. `Backend::call_frames` now gives 4,096 for OpenCL; Metal still has none and refuses a recursive model with `phasecont:engine:model`. Tests: `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/recursion_opencl.rs` runs the 4b cases (seven models, a member that reaches the cap mid-run, a map member, batches of two, both layouts and both precisions, bit for bit against the CPU tape for RK4 and maps, to 1e-3 single and 1e-6 double for DP5) on every OpenCL device; the pc-ffi test now takes a recursive model on CUDA and OpenCL and refuses it on Metal; `tests/EnsembleTest.m` `aGpuStopsAMemberPastTheKernelStack` runs on CUDA and OpenCL. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2911 passed, 0 failed, 16 ignored) and `cargo deny check` ok; the Windows build of `recursion_opencl` passes on the RTX 5060 (OpenCL 3.0 CUDA, driver 621.24) and the Arc 140T (OpenCL 3.0 NEO, driver 32.0.101.9150) in 64.23 s with `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=opencl`; with the GPU visible `recursion_cuda` passes and pc-ffi `ensemble` gives 26 passed; `pc-cli gpu selftest` passes for cuda 0, opencl 0 and opencl 1 in both precisions (Arc single recursion tier worst error 6.68e-8 against 1e-5); vdp-rk4 bench hashes unchanged (single 78cf287b0a170177, double 8013014065fe54f1); full MATLAB suite after restaging pckern and `buildtool mex`, 954 total, 945 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (the Metal skips and the skips by assumption). Next: Metal (4d), the Nsight check (4e).
- Order of work: CI and docs, R4, R1, parser Q2, the zero-Hopf check, round D and round E (both done 2026-11-19, above), then round F (SPEC section 8af, rows P40 to P44). Each item gets its SPEC text and tests first. Commits stay local until the owner approves the next push.

## Roadmap (2026-11-13)

This section comes from a read-only audit of 2026-11-13 (launch gaps, hardware coverage, optimization headroom and parity with XPPAUT) and from the owner's answers that day. Launch here means a release of the work after v1.0.0-preview to GitHub Releases, and later File Exchange and the Add-On Explorer.

- Known gaps without a phase (added 2026-11-19, round D): delay time simulation with history functions, `set(a,b,c)`, phase reduction, two-parameter LPC, PD and NS curves of delay cycles, and PD switching on delay cycles. The list and its state are in SPEC section 13; the first two are not scheduled (owner's call).

### Owner answers of 2026-11-13

- Map models (M1, see below): full map support. That means iteration, fixed points from F(x) - x = 0, multipliers, and the fold, period-doubling and Neimark-Sacker bifurcations of fixed points. It is a new problem class and gets its own phase with a plan. The plan also says what the launch does with maps if that phase is not done by then.
- `@` options in `simulate` (M3): warn and name them. `simulate` warns once with `phasecont:simulate:ignoredOption` and lists every `@` option it does not apply. Results do not change. The README says which `@` options PhaseCont reads.

### M1: map models run as ODEs (confirmed, in v1.0.0-preview too)

`crates/pc-ir/src/lower.rs` lowers `x(t+1)=...` like `x'=...`. Only the ensemble reads `IrModel.kind` (`crates/pc-ensemble/src/job.rs`), and it refuses a map with `phasecont:engine:unsupportedModel`; `simulate`, `equilibrium` and `continuation` run the map as an ODE. A MATLAB check on 2026-11-13 (logistic map, r = 3, x(0) = 0.5, `simulate` on [0 5]) returned 51 points at non-integer times (0, 0.0093, 0.051, ...) with x rising smoothly from 0.5 to 1.0, which is the ODE x' = r x (1 - x), not the iteration 0.5, 0.75, 0.5625, .... `equilibrium` from 0.5 stopped with `phasecont:engine:numeric` (singular matrix), because it solves F(x) = 0 instead of F(x) = x. The same lowering is in the v1.0.0 tag, so the release notes of the next version must say that map models gave wrong results before. `language.md` marks the map statement "full" and SPEC section 2 says "parse, simulate"; both change with the map phase.

### Map support phase (plan approved 2026-11-14)

Branch `map-support` from `launch-prep` (a8d5590), unpushed. On 2026-11-15 `launch-prep` was fast-forwarded to `map-support` locally, with no push; `git branch -f launch-prep a8d5590` undoes it. The plan was approved with its ten decisions as recommended. Each slice adds its SPEC section and acceptance tests before any code.

0. Refuse maps until they work. `run_start` and `model_equilibrium` refuse a map with `phasecont:engine:unsupportedModel`, `pc-cli cont` refuses it too, `model_info` gains a `"map"` key, the phase-plane app refuses a map with `phasecont:app:mapModel`, and `@ meth=discrete` makes `x'=f` a map, as in XPPAUT. `rhs`, `jacobian` and the C emitter stay allowed. This slice ships in the next release whether or not the later slices are done (owner decision 1 of the plan).
1. Iterate a map: `Model.IsMap`, `simulate(m, x0, [n0 n1])` with integer iterates, an internal `"ITER"` problem type. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 1 state below.
2. Fixed points from G = F - x, multipliers in `Eigenvalues`, LP, BP, PD and NS on a one-parameter branch with 4 test columns, and the LP, PD and NS coefficients from Govaerts, Khoshsiar Ghaziani, Kuznetsov and Meijer (2007), Table 2.3. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 2 state below.
3. Two-parameter curves of fixed points: LP, PD and NS curves of a map. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 3 state below.
4. Codim-2 labels on those curves. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 4 state below.
5. Period-k points. The owner answered decision 10 for this slice on 2026-11-15: an `Iterate = k` option on `equilibrium` and `continuation` with a read-only `Branch.Iterate`, both `Switch = "PD1"` on a map EP run and a start from a stored PD special (Iterate doubles), and LP, PD and NS curves of period-k points. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 5 state below.
6. A map mode in the phase-plane app. The owner approved slices 3 to 8 on 2026-11-15 ("ok" to the fourth owner item) and answered the cobweb question of this slice the same day with "Both": a cobweb view in the app and `phasecont.cobweb`. Built on 2026-11-15, see the slice 6 state below.
7. An ensemble map method. Built on 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16, see the slice 7 state below.
8. The remaining docs. Built on 2026-11-16, see the slice 8 state below.

Slice 0 state (2026-11-14, SPEC section 8t, commits c83e749 to the session replay test on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Built. `run_start` refuses a map for every problem type before it parses the problem, `model_equilibrium` refuses it next to its `ode_only` check, and both return `UnsupportedModel` (code 13, `phasecont:engine:unsupportedModel`) with the text in `pckern::error::MAP_MODEL`. `model_info` has the key `"map"`. `pc-cli cont` refuses a map after the model loads. `PhasePlane` refuses a map with `phasecont:app:mapModel`, also from a session file. `Model.rhs`, `jacobian`, `emit_c`, `import` and `latex_render` still work on a map.
- `@ meth`. The value is the last unspaced `@` entry whose key starts with `meth`, ignoring case. A value that starts with `d` makes an ODE file a map with warning ODE0074; an empty value or an XPPAUT integrator letter changes nothing; any other first letter keeps the ODE with ODE0074. Ten XPPAUT 8.0 probes back this (`references/xpp-probes/meth-*.ode`, `map-meth-euler.ode`, `opt-prefix-total.ode`). `XPPAUT=/usr/local/sbin/xppaut references/xpp-probes/run.sh` gave 174 probes, 348 PASS and "all checks passed" (177 probes and 354 PASS after the three `opt-prefix` probes below, and 181 probes and 362 PASS after the four `map-t*` and `map-dt` probes of b82f4f5, which measure what `t`, `t0` and `dt` mean inside an XPPAUT map). The probe README (`references/xpp-probes/README.md`) is the record of these measurements, not `docs/spikes.md`.
- Ruling: `pc-cli cont` exits with 1 on a map, not with the bad-problem code 2 that the plan named, because `CONT_HELP` already gives 1 for a refused model. SPEC 8t says 1.
- Ruling: `simulate` now gives its `ignoredOption` warning after the run returns instead of before it starts, so a `meth=discrete` file gets the refusal without a warning that its `meth` option is ignored. A run that fails gets no warning. The CHANGELOG says so.
- `ensemble` now refuses an ODE file with `@ meth=discrete`, which it integrated before.
- Rust checks: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2289 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored (2288 before the session replay test), `cargo deny check` ok.
- MATLAB: after `scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64` restaged the DLL, `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 664 total, 655 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete, and 665 total, 656 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete on 2026-11-14 after the replay test. The 10 `MapRefusalTest` cases pass. The incompletes are the 6 of the baseline plus the 3 Metal cases that launch-prep added (44fa560), which find no Metal device on Windows. Against the 646 of main 50d1a44 that makes 18 more: the 9 map cases, the 3 Metal cases, and 6 methods that launch-prep added (5 in `TrajectoryTest.m`, 1 in `RunBoundedTest.m`, from `git diff 50d1a44 a8d5590 -- tests`).
- Session replay. `crates/pc-cli/tests/session_map.rs` writes a session with an equilibrium record and a trajectory record on the logistic map, once as `x(t+1)=...` and once with `@ meth=discrete`, and checks that `pc-cli session replay` exits with 1 and fails both runs with the map refusal. MATLAB `phasecont.session.replay` goes through the same `run_start` and `model_equilibrium` calls, and `MapRefusalTest.replayOfAMapSessionFailsEveryRun` checks that it fails both runs with `phasecont:session:runFailed` and the refusal text, for `x(t+1)=...` and for `@ meth=discrete`.
- `crates/pc-ode/docs/language.md`: the Map row now says what SPEC section 2 says. Slice 8 rewrote the Map row again and changed no other line of that file.
- Review: a code review of the slice found no correctness bug. Its one fix, the ODE row of the SPEC section 2 table, is 0c18123. `phasePlaneRefusesAMapFromASessionFile` now checks that the app deletes the model it loaded from the session: handle ids count up in steps of 2, so every id between the caller's model and a model made after the refusal must give `phasecont:engine:badHandle`.

Items of the map phase that were open after slice 0, settled on 2026-11-14 (the owner said to go ahead with the recommendations):

1. `meth=ode45` and any other value whose first letter XPPAUT does not list as an integrator keep the file an ODE with ODE0074, while XPPAUT 8.0 iterates it as a map (probe `meth-ode45`). Decided: keep the ODE. SPEC 8t says so. The owner signed off this item and items 2 and 3 on 2026-11-15 ("ok" to the third owner item).
2. Option prefixes. XPPAUT 8.0 takes a key that starts with a full option name as that option, and the longer name wins. Probes on 2026-11-14 showed this for `dt`, `dtmin`, `t0`, `trans`, `njmp`, `total`, `bound`, `toler` and `meth`; `tot` sets nothing. `options::PREFIX_NAMES` holds those nine names, and `is_known_option` gives no ODE0024 for a key that starts with one of them. Every other name stays exact until a probe shows the rule for it. New probes `opt-prefix-dt`, `opt-prefix-t0` and `opt-prefix-short`; `opt-prefix-total` now matches.
3. The language server calls a state of a map, also of a `meth=discrete` file, a map variable in hover, completion and the document symbols (853ea00).

Slice 1 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8u, commits 0fb0ed9 to 5227d8e plus the MATLAB commits after them on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. `crates/pc-engine/src/iterate.rs` computes x(k+1) = F(x(k), p, t = k) and writes rows [k, x(k)]. The internal problem type `"ITER"` takes `tspan` [n0 n1] with integers n0 < n1 and |n| at most 2^53, and at most 1000000 iterates per run. A value that is not finite stops the run with `Error` and keeps the rows. `run_start` runs ITER only on a map, refuses an IVP on a map, and refuses ITER on an ODE. `pc-cli cont` and `pc-cli session` run ITER problems. Checks: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` (2314 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored) and `cargo deny check` all pass at 5227d8e.
- MATLAB. `Model.IsMap`. `simulate(m, x0, [n0 n1])` on a map checks TSPAN first (`phasecont:model:badOption`), then refuses `RelTol`, `AbsTol`, `MaxSteps` and `Hmax` (`phasecont:simulate:mapOption`). The `ignoredOption` warning on a map leaves out a `meth` value that starts with `d`. `Trajectory.plot` draws the iterates of a map as dots against n, and a session keeps the `Map` flag, since `Session.rebuild` sets it from the record type `"ITER"`. Replay reruns an ITER record. Other runs on a map are still refused, and 1.0.0 IVP records of a map still fail replay.
- Tests. `tests/MapIterateTest.m` has 11 cases (the loops keep the association of the expression tree, so the logistic and Henon iterates equal the hand loop bit for bit). `MapRefusalTest` now checks that only `simulate` runs on a map. `scripts/matlab-win "addpath(fullfile(pwd,'toolbox')); r = runtests(fullfile(pwd,'tests',{'MapIterateTest.m','MapRefusalTest.m'})); ..."` gave 20 total, 20 passed, 0 failed. The full suite, `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"`, gave 675 total, 666 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete.
- Review. A review pass found that the bound on an iterate number used `k.abs()`, which overflows on i64::MIN, so the token -9223372036854775808 got past the 2^53 and 1000000 bounds in a release build. The check is now a range test, and `parse_problem` refuses that token (SPEC section 8u, test 2). The same pass brought the map row of `crates/pc-ode/docs/language.md` and the run kinds of `status_json` in the SPEC up to date.
- Docs. README (the map paragraph with an example, and the `@ meth` note) and CHANGELOG (Added, Changed, Compatibility and Known limits) say that `simulate` iterates a map.

Slice 2 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8v, commits 5755cc0 to 2eba56e on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. `MapAsRoot` (`crates/pc-engine/src/mapfix.rs`) gives the EP code G = F - x and G_x = F_x - I, so Newton, continuation, location, refinement and the LP and BP tests run on G unchanged. The eigenvalues of a map EP point are the multipliers, the eigenvalues of F_x. A map EP row has stride 3n + 7 and the four tests [LP BP PD NS], with ψ_PD = ∏(μ_i + 1) and ψ_NS = ∏_{i<j}(μ_i μ_j - 1). An NS zero is labeled only when the pair nearest to the unit circle is complex. The LP, PD and NS coefficients follow Govaerts, Khoshsiar Ghaziani, Kuznetsov and Meijer (2007), Table 2.3, and an NS point also has θ in `values.theta`. `run_start` runs EP and ITER on a map and refuses every other type with `UnsupportedModel`. `model_equilibrium` solves G = 0. `pc-cli cont` and `pc-cli session` run map EP problems and records. Checks at ee1b8be: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2335 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok.
- MATLAB. `equilibrium` on a map returns the fixed point and its multipliers. `continuation` with Type `"EP"` on a map returns a Branch with 4 test columns, `Stable` from the moduli of the multipliers and `Codim2` N-by-0. `Detect` on a map defaults to `["LP" "BP" "PD" "NS"]`, and any other entry raises `phasecont:model:badOption`. `"PD"` and `"NS"` on an ODE raise the same id. The help texts of `equilibrium`, `continuation` and `Branch` say what the fields hold for a map.
- Owner decision of 2026-11-15 ("add stable"), which reverses the first ruling of this slice (no property, only a sentence in SPEC 8v): `phasecont.Point` has a read-only Dependent property `Stable`. It is computed from `Eigenvalues` when it is read and is not stored, so the session format does not change. Ruling on its rules, revertible: for a Point with Label `"EP"`, a Model that is not a delay model and at least one eigenvalue it is `all(abs(ev) < 1)` on a map and `all(real(ev) < 0)` on an ODE, the rules of `Branch.Stable`; every other Point has `Stable` empty (`false(0, 1)`), since at LP, BP, H, PD or NS an eigenvalue lies on the stability boundary. SPEC 8v tests 10 and 11 pin it (`MapFixedPointTest`, `EquilibriumTest`). `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` on 2026-11-15 gave 689 tests, 680 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete (filtered by assumption), 276.1 s.
- Three choices beyond the approved decisions, each in its own commit so the owner can revert it alone, all three accepted by the owner on 2026-11-15: the key `theta` in `Data.values` of an NS point (in the engine commits), the refusal of `Detect = "H"` on a map (a1520e4), and the rule that loads a map EP branch saved by 1.0.0 (width 3n + 6) with NaN in its PD and NS columns (7fda486). Without 7fda486 such a branch fails to load, since `rows2branch` accepts no other width.

An equilibrium record now runs, finds the fixed point instead of the stored root of F and fails the comparison with `Pass = false` and no warning. An EP branch that asks for the H test fails with `runFailed`.
- Tests. `tests/MapFixedPointTest.m` has 10 cases. The logistic PD gives an r error of 0 and b = 9, the delayed logistic NS gives an r error of 4.4e-16, a θ error of 2.2e-16, d = -1 and coefficient2 = -1.73205080756888. `MapRefusalTest` now checks that `simulate`, `equilibrium` and EP `continuation` run on a map and that the engine refuses an LP curve with `phasecont:engine:unsupportedModel` and the refusal text. The full suite, `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"`, gave 685 total, 676 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete on 2026-11-15. Before a1520e4 and 7fda486, `MapFixedPointTest` and `MapRefusalTest` gave 17 total, 17 passed, 0 failed on the tree of cd194c6. The README example gives PD at r = 3 with x = 0.66666666666666663 and b = 9.

- Review. A review pass with four reviewers found no defect in the engine or the boundary code. Its one confirmed finding: `verifyCurvesAreRefused` in `MapRefusalTest` accepted any `phasecont:` id, and with one `Free` name the logistic cases stopped at the toolbox check of the Free count, so they would pass with no engine refusal. The logistic models of those tests now have a second parameter, and the test asserts the id and the text (eaaddc8). The 1:3 resonance guard of the NS coefficient had no test. `map_ns` with θ = 2π/3 now pins it, and the test fails when the 1:3 clause is removed (95943e4). The 1:1 and 1:2 clauses have no test; an exactly real pair stops earlier at the check that the pair is complex, so they fire only for a pair with a tiny imaginary part. Slice 3 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8w, commits a7b2612 to 53a2d3f on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. `crates/pc-engine/src/mapcurve.rs` has the run kinds `MapLpCurve`, `MapPdCurve` and `MapNsCurve`. The LP and PD curves share one bordered system [A + σI, w; vᵀ, 0] with σ = -1 for LP and +1 for PD, and the NS curve uses M = A² - 2κA + I with 2-by-2 borders, from Govaerts, Khoshsiar Ghaziani, Kuznetsov and Meijer (2007), equations 2.4 to 2.9. The curves run on the compiled map, so their rows hold the multipliers of F. The four rulings that SPEC 8w names: (a) three new run kinds in a new module, not the adapter `MapAsRoot`; (b) no codim-2 labels in this slice, and run kind names `"LP"`, `"PD"` and `"NS"`; (c) MATLAB dispatches on `Model.IsMap`, so on an ODE `"PD"` and `"NS"` stay the cycle curves; (d) the map LP curve uses the PD system with σ = -1 instead of the ODE LP curve on G = F - x, which departs from the plan text, since one code path keeps the multipliers of F in all three row layouts and the ODE LP curve's codim-2 tests (CP, BT, ZH) do not fit a map.
- Owner item: `START_RESIDUAL_MAX` = 10^-6, the bound on the residual of a start before the corrector moves it onto the curve, has no source. Section 8v locates PD and NS points to 10^-10 in alpha when refined and 10^-8 when not, so their residual on the curve system can be above `tol_f`, and the bound must sit above that. Revertible in one constant.
- JSON and CLI. `map_type_runs` in `pc-ffi/src/json/problem.rs` decides which problems run on a map, and `run_start` and `pc-cli cont` both call it. LP and PD rows have stride 3n + 4 and NS rows 3n + 5 (with κ after a1). `pc-cli session` replays the three curve records.
- MATLAB rulings, revertible: `Branch.isCycleType`, `internal.latexTable` and `latex.figureFromBranch` tell a map curve from a cycle curve by its NTST, which is NaN on a curve of fixed points, so they need no Model. `Branch.cycle` on a map PD or NS curve raises `phasecont:model:badBranch` with "cycle needs a Branch with orbits; this "PD" curve of fixed points of a map has none." `continuation` sends Types `"H"`, `"LC"`, `"LPC"` and `"HOM"` on a map to the engine as `{"type":TYPE}` (`refuseOnMap`), so the refusal and its text come from one place, `map_type_runs`. `rows2branch` loads a map LP curve saved by 1.0.0 (width 3n + 7) in the ODE LP layout and a map PD or NS curve saved by 1.0.0 as the cycle curve with a mesh it is.
- Tests. Rust: `crates/pc-engine/tests/map_curve.rs` (SPEC 8w tests 1 to 6), `crates/pc-ffi/tests/map.rs` (tests 6 and 7), `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs` (test 7 for all three curves) and `crates/pc-cli/tests/session_map.rs` (test 8). MATLAB: `tests/MapCurveTest.m` has 9 cases (test 9). `MapCurveTest`, `MapFixedPointTest`, `MapIterateTest` and `MapRefusalTest` gave 31 total, 31 passed, 0 failed. After `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64` restaged the DLL, `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 699 total, 690 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete on 2026-11-15. Checks at d0f93ed: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2353 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok.
- Review. A review pass with three reviewers (engine, boundary, MATLAB) and one verifier confirmed one gap: SPEC 8w test 7 asks that the rows of `run_start` equal those of `pc-cli cont` for all three curves, and only the PD curve had that test. `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs` now checks the LP and NS curves too, each test with its own JSON file, since two tests that wrote the same file in parallel read each other's problem. The verifier rejected three findings, and two of them were fixed anyway since the fix is small. The start residual of the map curves and of the ODE LP curve folded with `f64::max`, which drops NaN, so the `is_nan` guard after it never fired; the fold now keeps a NaN entry (the verifier found that `check_finite` keeps NaN out of the residual in practice, so no result changes). The NS refusal test of `pc-ffi` started off the fixed point, so it did not show that the NS rows alone are off; it now starts on the fixed point x = y = (-1.5 + √(2.25 + 4a))/2 at b = 0.5. The third, that the NS start from an EP branch checks only one coordinate, needs no change: that curve is the line b = 1 (or a = 0 on `map_ns4.ode`), and tests 3 and 4 check the state, κ and the multipliers against the closed forms. Slice 4 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8x, commits 3f257f2 to 328ad8e on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. The three curves of slice 3 now carry the codim-2 tests of Table 2.2 of Govaerts, Khoshsiar Ghaziani, Kuznetsov and Meijer (2007): the LP curve `["LPPD","LPNS","CP","R1"]`, the PD curve `["LPPD","PDNS","GPD","R2"]` and the NS curve `["R1","R2","R3","R4","LPNS","PDNS","NSNS","CH"]`. The tests are in `crates/pc-engine/src/mapcurve.rs`, the products over the rest set reuse `rest_product`, `pair_product` and `remove_nearest` from `cycle/codim2.rs`, and CH reuses the NS coefficient of slice 2 through `map_ns_core` in `normal.rs`. Labels only: a special has a null `coefficient`, and no normal form of a codim-2 point is computed.
- Rulings of SPEC 8x, each revertible:
  - (a) The rest set is the multipliers without the critical ones (1 on the LP curve, -1 on the PD curve, both roots of μ² - 2κμ + 1 on the NS curve), each removed as the multiplier nearest to its value. A fixed point has no trivial multiplier, so none is removed first.
  - (b) The tests use the unit null vectors v̂ and ŵ of the bordered solves. GPD is NaN where its solve fails or its value is not finite.
  - (c) `Problem::codim2_label` does not get κ, so NSNS removes the complex pair whose product is nearest to 1 and labels only if the nearest pair of the rest is complex.
  - (d) CH = d/|c1|, computed at the multiplier nearest to κ + i√(1 - κ²). It is NaN at the 1:1, 1:2 and 1:3 resonances within 10^-6 in θ, where the slice 2 coefficient refuses.
  - (e) The CH test counts a sign change only if the larger end value is at least `CH_BRACKET_FLOOR` = 10^-8.
  - (f) For |κ| ≥ 1, LPNS, PDNS, NSNS and CH are NaN, and R1 to R4 are computed.
- Owner items: (1) the value of `CH_BRACKET_FLOOR` (10^-8, no source; on an area-preserving map d is 0 and CH is rounding noise, and the floor keeps that noise from being bracketed). (2) Whether CH should get a floor on |c1| relative to the size of the terms of c1 instead of the current exact-zero check; no test model needs it.
- Rows. LP and PD rows have stride 3n + 8 with 4 codim-2 columns, and NS rows 3n + 13 with 8. The slice 3 layouts (3n + 4 and 3n + 5) never reached a release. A map LP curve saved by 1.0.0-preview (width 3n + 7) still loads in the ODE LP layout with Codim2Labels `["CP" "BT" "ZH"]`.
- Tests. Rust: `crates/pc-engine/tests/map_curve_codim2.rs` (SPEC 8x tests 2, 3, 5 and 6, eight new models under `crates/pc-engine/tests/models/`), the unit test `nsns_labels_only_a_second_complex_pair` in `mapcurve.rs` (test 1), `crates/pc-engine/tests/map_curve.rs` (test 4 and the amended tests 3 and 7 of 8w), `crates/pc-ffi/tests/map.rs` and `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs` (the new strides and labels). MATLAB: `tests/MapCurveTest.m` checks Codim2 and Codim2Labels on every curve, LPPD = σ(b - 1) on the Hénon LP and PD curves, and the R3 special of the NS curve at a = 5/4.
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test --no-fail-fast` 2370 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok. After `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64` restaged the DLL, `MapCurveTest`, `MapFixedPointTest`, `MapIterateTest` and `MapRefusalTest` gave 42 passed, 0 failed, 0 incomplete, and `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 699 total, 690 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete on 2026-11-15.
- Review. Three reviewers (numerics, row layout, tests) and one verifier. One finding was confirmed and fixed: the doc comment of `stride` in `crates/pc-ffi/src/run.rs` still named the slice 3 widths. Four were rejected: a NaN CH value is allowed, since the spec labels CH only where it is defined; the sign of CP depends on the arbitrary sign of ŵ, and the test counts sign changes, not signs; the sign of GPD is covered by `test3_generalized_flip`; and the place of the R3 bracket in `pc-ffi` is covered by the engine and MATLAB tests.

Slice 5 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8y, commits 4a26fa8 to cfab1bd on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. `IteratedMap` (`crates/pc-engine/src/mapiter.rs`) evaluates F^k on the entry contract of `pc-ir`: `JacU` is the product A_{k-1} ⋯ A_0, `JacP` the recursion M_{j+1} = A_j M_j + F_p, and `Dir` the set partition form of the chain rule for 1 to 5 slots, from Section 4.1 of Govaerts, Khoshsiar Ghaziani, Kuznetsov and Meijer (2007). With k = 1 a run uses the model itself. EP runs and `model_equilibrium` solve F^k(x) = x through `MapAsRoot` over `IteratedMap`, and the curve runs of Sections 8w and 8x take `IteratedMap` directly. `"switch":"PD1"` on a map EP run continues branch 1 at iterate 2k from the PD special with tangent (q, 0)/|q|, and `"start":"pd"` starts branch 0 at iterate 2k from a given PD point of F^k. Both refuse a PD point that is not simple.
- Rulings, each revertible:
  - Branch 1 of a PD switch runs at 2k even when 2k is above 10^6, the bound of the problem key, since the bound is on what a caller sends. SPEC 8y says so. MATLAB leaves a start from a PD Point with 2k above 10^6 to the engine refusal.
  - The two simplicity checks of the PD switch and the `"pd"` start scale both thresholds by max(σ_0, 1), as SPEC 8y says, so A^(k) within 10^-8 max(σ_0, 1) of -I is not called simple. A third check, σ_{n-2} ≤ 10^3 σ_{n-1}, closes the gap that a kernel of dimension 2 or more between the two thresholds would pass (SPEC 8y, `kernel_gap_ok`).
  - SPEC 8y test 6 uses a 3-dimensional model, (x, y, z) → -(1 + a)(x, y, z) (`map_flip3.ode`). The test locates that PD to 10^-5, not 10^-8: every multiplier crosses -1 at once, det(A + I) = (-a)³ has a triple zero, and the located a (about 2·10^-6) is only as close as the cube root of the floor of the test function. Every singular value of A + I is then about 2·10^-6, so the first check refuses the switch. At a = 0 every singular value is 0, and the second check refuses the start. The test checks no coefficient there, since the coefficient of Section 8v assumes a simple PD.
  - `run_start` splits the start refusal so that an LP, PD, NS or `ITER` run of a map gets "start: only an EP run of a map takes this key", and an `ITER` problem with `"start"` gets that refusal, not the ODE text. The `problem.rs` test now expects it.
  - `model_equilibrium` wraps the model in `IteratedMap` on an ODE too, always with k = 1, which evaluates the model itself, so one code path serves both kinds.
  - `pc-cli cont` prints the `"iterate"` of each branch before its points.
  - MATLAB: from an EP Point or a state vector, a curve takes a given `Iterate`. Only a special that is not an EP point raises `phasecont:model:badOption` when `Iterate` differs from the special's. A Point labeled `"PD"` on a map is a PD special, so `continuation(m, P, Type = "EP")` from it doubles the iterate. The problem JSON gets `"iterate"` only when k > 1, so a run without the option sends the same problem as before.
  - `Iterate` is not given only when it is the default, a 0-by-0 double. Any other empty value, such as `zeros(1, 0)`, `''`, `{}` or `string.empty`, raises `phasecont:model:badOption`, so it cannot skip the ODE refusal or run a map at iterate 1 unnoticed.
  - Sessions keep scalar items, so the two Branches of a PD switch are saved as two items of one run, as for a BP switch.
- JSON and CLI. `"iterate"` is parsed by `parse_iterate` in `crates/pc-ffi/src/json/problem.rs` with the six errors of SPEC 8y. Every branch of a map EP or curve run has `"iterate"` in the status JSON, and branch 1 of a PD switch has 2k. `model_equilibrium` reads `"iterate"` from its options. `pc-cli cont` passes the key through.
- MATLAB. `equilibrium` and `continuation` take `Iterate` (`toolbox/+phasecont/+internal/iterateOption.m`), `Branch.Iterate` is read-only and `Point.Iterate` is hidden and read-only. `rows2branch` reads `"iterate"` from the status, and `Session.rebuild` reads the iterate of an equilibrium Point from its stored options.
- Tests. Rust: `crates/pc-engine/tests/map_iterate.rs` (SPEC 8y tests 1 to 6, models `map_logistic2.ode`, `map_logistic3.ode`, `map_henon2.ode` and `map_flip3.ode`), `crates/pc-ffi/tests/map.rs` (test 7) and `crates/pc-cli/tests/cont.rs`. MATLAB: `tests/MapPeriodTest.m` has 6 cases (test 8).
- Checks: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2384 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok. After `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 scripts/build-pckern win64 /mnt/c/Users/<user>/phasecont-stage/bin/win64` restaged the DLL, `MapPeriodTest`, `MapCurveTest`, `MapFixedPointTest`, `MapIterateTest` and `MapRefusalTest` gave 48 total, 48 passed, 0 failed, 0 incomplete, and `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 705 total, 696 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete (the 9 of slice 0, the 6 of the baseline and the 3 Metal cases, filtered by assumption) on 2026-11-15.
- Gap between the two simplicity checks: closed on 2026-11-16 with a third check (factor 10^3, set by the owner's "fix that too"); see the ruling below.
- Review. A review workflow with four reviewers (engine, JSON and C ABI boundary, MATLAB, tests) read the slice 5 diff. The boundary reviewer found nothing. The other three found four low findings, each checked against the code: (1) branch 1 of a PD switch can run above 10^6, kept as the ruling above with a SPEC sentence; (2) the gap between the two simplicity checks, now stated in SPEC 8y and an owner item; (3) an explicit empty `Iterate` passed as not given, fixed in `iterateOption.m` with refusal cases in `MapPeriodTest`; (4) the PD1 refusal of test 6 meets only the first check, which holds, but the start at a = 0 already meets the second, so the fix is a comment in the test and the corrected test 6 ruling above.

Slice 6 state (2026-11-15, SPEC section 8z, commits 82b9a60 to 279d590 on `map-support`, unpushed):

- MATLAB only. The engine, the C ABI and the session format do not change. `PhasePlane` picks the ODE, map or cobweb view from the model in the constructor and in `openSession`. The map view iterates from a click with `simulate(m, x0, [0 N])`, marks fixed points by the modulus of their multipliers and draws the curves F_i(x) = x_i as nullclines. The cobweb view draws the graph of F, the diagonal and the staircase of a map with one variable. `phasecont.cobweb(m, x0, N)` draws the same cobweb in any axes. `phasecont.internal.cobwebSteps`, `cobwebGraph`, `nextColor` and `rhsGaps` are new internal helpers.
- Rulings, each revertible:
  - `Trajectory.plot` takes one color per call from the color order through `nextColor` and puts the color order index one past it, so two plots with `hold on` get two colors. Before, each line of a plot moved the index on.
  - `rhsGaps` evaluates the batch `rhs` and, on `phasecont:engine:numeric`, evaluates each column alone, so a column whose value is not finite becomes NaN. The cobweb graph and the nullcline and field grids of every view use it, the ODE view included. Where the values are finite, the ODE view draws what it drew before.
  - `Branch.m` does not change. The app cobweb draws with `line` and takes its color from `nextColor`, as `phasecont.cobweb` does.
  - A fixed point whose multipliers are empty or not finite gets the cross `pc-equilibrium-unknown` and the status "Fixed point at <where>: the multipliers are not available.".
  - The README and CHANGELOG sentences that still call two-parameter curves and period-k points of maps planned were left for slice 8, which updated them.

Slice 7 state (2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16, SPEC section 8aa, commits f73a09c to 6600f92 on `map-support`, unpushed):

- Engine. `pc_ensemble::job::validate` checks the model kind against the method before any other check: RK4 or Dormand-Prince on a map is `Unsupported`, and `iter` on an ODE is `BadJob`. `iter` needs whole-number times, with magnitude at most 2^24 and at most 2^24 iterates per segment in single precision, and 2^53 and 4294967295 in double precision. `Integrator::Iter` in `kernel.rs` is the RK4 loop with one `pc_rhs` call in place of the four stages, with the same entry points, buffers and `rc` layout, for CUDA, OpenCL and Metal and in both layouts. The CPU backend in `traj.rs` adds `Kind::Iter`. The job JSON takes `{"type":"iter"}`, and the `pckern.h` comment and the `pc-cli` help name it. The C ABI keeps its signatures and `abi_version` stays 1.
- Self-test. A new tier `iterate` runs x(t+1) = 0.5x + t over [0, 10] with the limit 1e-12 in double precision and 1e-5 in single. On the CPU the error is 0 in double precision and about 4.5e-8 in single. The Hénon map with a = 0.2 and b = 0.3 over [0, 50] joins the bit-exact and determinism checks. `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` keeps its four checks.
- MATLAB. `ensemble` on a map iterates from n0 to n1, and TSPAN holds increasing whole numbers. `Dt`, `Rtol`, `Atol`, `Hmax` and `MaxSteps` raise `phasecont:ensemble:mapOption` in that order, after the precision check and before the start states. The signature default of `MaxSteps` is `[]`, which still means 100000 for an ODE. `Job.Method` is `"iter"`, `Job.MaxSteps` is n1 - n0, and `refine` of a map result iterates again on the CPU in double precision. The help of `ensemble` and `EnsembleResult`, the `Model.m` comment, the README and the CHANGELOG describe it.
- Rulings, each revertible:
  - The help of `EnsembleResult` said that a member that stopped early holds its last state after `TReached`. The states there are NaN, for an ODE too, so the help now says NaN.
  - The single-precision bound of 2^24 is the engine's check, so MATLAB passes such a TSPAN on and it returns as `phasecont:engine:badProblem`. The help of `ensemble` and the README state the bound.
- Tests. `crates/pc-ensemble/tests/iterate.rs` (13 cases, SPEC 8aa tests 1 and 2), five kernel text, shim and global layout cases and the CUDA compile case (test 3), four self-test cases (test 4), three `pc-ffi` cases (test 5), two `pc-cli` cases (test 6) and `tests/EnsembleMapTest.m` (14 cases, test 7).
- Checks on 6600f92: `cargo fmt --check` ok, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 cargo test` 2412 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok. `scripts/matlab-win "addpath('tests'); r = runtests({'tests/EnsembleMapTest.m'}); ..."` gave 14 total, 14 passed, 0 failed, 0 incomplete, and `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` with the DLL restaged from 6600f92 (the gateway in `mex/` has not changed on this branch) gave 763 total, 754 passed, 0 failed and 9 incomplete (the 9 of slice 0, the 6 of the baseline and the 3 Metal cases, filtered by assumption) on 2026-11-16.
- Device runs on 2026-11-16. `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 cargo test --release -p pc-ensemble --test cuda_global` passed (1 test, 172.86 s) on the RTX 5060. It forces `Layout::Global` and runs the self-test, so the map jobs ran on the device in the global layout. `pc-cli gpu selftest` (built at 728e5c4; the later commits change only tests) on CUDA device 0 and OpenCL devices 0 and 1 (RTX 5060, RTX 5060, Intel Arc 140T), each in single and double precision, exited with 0, and `pass` and `bit_exact` were true in all six runs. The CUDA single-precision `iterate` error was 4.486796618047092e-8. Metal is syntax-checked only, with no device run.
- Owner items: the error ID `phasecont:ensemble:mapOption`, the signature default `MaxSteps = []` and `Job.MaxSteps` = n1 - n0 for a map, `Job.Method = "iter"`, `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` staying at four checks, the new `iterate` tier and the Hénon job in `pc-cli gpu selftest`, and Metal with no device run.
- Review. A review workflow with three reviewers (engine and kernels, the MATLAB front end, the tests) read the slice 7 diff. The engine and front-end reviewers found nothing. The tests reviewer found three gaps, each checked against the code and fixed: no test failed if the Hénon job left the bit-exact or determinism check, or if the `iterate` tier always passed or gave F the wrong time (be8d127; four mutations of `selftest.rs` each made a new test fail), and the MATLAB tests checked only the `mapOption` ID, not its text, its order or its place before the start states (6600f92; two mutations of `ensemble.m` each made the new test fail).
