Map support owner items, rulings of 2026-11-16 (decided on 2026-11-16 under the owner's delegation; each costs one constant, one name or one test file to undo, and nothing is pushed):

- `START_RESIDUAL_MAX` = 10^-6, `CH_BRACKET_FLOOR` = 10^-8 and the exact-zero check on c1: kept as built. On 2026-11-16 the owner chose to keep all three absolute rather than scale them with the problem, so this item is settled. None has a source. Each is a bare engineering bound, so each is listed under Known limits in the CHANGELOG. If wrong: a start within 10^-6 of a curve is corrected when a tighter bound would have refused it, a CH sign change below 10^-8 goes unlabeled, and a c1 that is rounding noise near zero gives a large CH value.
- Gap between the two simplicity checks (PD switch, `"pd"` start, `switch_bp`): closed on 2026-11-16 at the owner's "fix that too". A third check refuses when n >= 2 and sigma_{n-2} <= 10^3 sigma_{n-1} (`kernel_gap_ok` in `switch.rs`, SPEC 8y). The factor 10^3 has no published source and the owner's instruction is its only authority, so it stays listed as an unsourced constant. It refuses a simple point whose two smallest singular values are within a factor of 10^3 of each other, which is close to degenerate. If wrong: a near-degenerate simple point is refused with "PD is not simple" or "BP is not simple", and the constant is one line to change. Checks on 2026-11-16: `cargo fmt --check` ok, clippy ok, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2413 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok, and after restaging the DLL `buildtool test` gave 768 total, 759 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete.
- Slice 6 API (`phasecont.cobweb`, `Params`, `Parent`, `[h, tr]`, `phasecont:cobweb:badModel`, the new meaning of `phasecont:app:mapModel`, `XIndex` and `YIndex` both 1): accepted as built. The change is additive.
- Slice 7 names: `phasecont:ensemble:mapOption` matches `phasecont:simulate:mapOption`, and `Job.Method = "iter"` matches `"rk4"` and `"dp5"`, so no rename. `MaxSteps = []` means 100000 for an ODE as the help states, and a map refuses `MaxSteps`, so the default never reaches a map; `Job.MaxSteps` is n1 - n0 for a map, and a long span is bounded in both layers (checked 2026-11-16, no change needed): `ensemble.m` refuses n1 - n0 above 2^32 - 1 with `phasecont:ensemble:badOption` (`EnsembleMapTest.badMapSpansAreRefused`, span [0 2^32]), and the engine refuses more than 2^32 - 1 iterates per sample interval in double precision and more than 2^24 in single precision (`crates/pc-ensemble/tests/iterate.rs`, `EnsembleMapTest.aLongSingleSpanIsRefusedByTheEngine`). A run takes time but allocates only its output rows. Accepted. `gpuSelfTest` stays at four checks.
- Slice 8: `MapReadmeTest` stays whole, with the copy-check case, because SPEC 8ab test 1 states it. The sentence on maps in the package Description stays, as decision 9 allows.
- Merge: `map-support` is fast-forwarded into `launch-prep` (a true fast-forward, since `launch-prep` is an ancestor). `main` is not moved. Nothing is pushed.

### Before the launch

Owner:

1. Done on 2026-11-16: `main` was pushed at 76aac62 and then at b19fb4b, which makes `hopf_curve_pin` compare counts only on CI (see the open questions).
2. Decided 2026-11-16: the release is 1.0.0. The 1.0.0 GitHub release of 2026-11-04 (tag `v1.0.0` at b045897) is renamed `v1.0.0-preview` and marked as a pre-release, since nobody is known to have downloaded it, and the CHANGELOG tells preview users to uninstall the preview first, because both report toolbox version 1.0.0. The tag needs the owner's go. On 2026-11-17 the repo text was reworded on branch `release-rename-text` (not pushed): CHANGELOG, README (Upgrading), SPEC, STATUS and the comments in `Session.m`, `rows2branch.m`, `session_map.rs` and the map, session and `rows2branch` tests call the 2026-11-04 release 1.0.0-preview, and the CHANGELOG and README tell preview users to uninstall it first. The version in `buildfile.m`, `PackageTest`, `packageToolbox.m` and the `EngineVersion` fixtures stays 1.0.0. The lines of this file that name the old release say 1.0.0-preview too, except where they name the tag `v1.0.0` itself or the asset `PhaseCont-1.0.0.mltbx`. The rename steps were done on GitHub by 2026-11-17: tag `v1.0.0-preview` points at b045897, the release of that tag is a pre-release titled "PhaseCont 1.0.0-preview" with its two original assets (`PhaseCont-1.0.0.mltbx` and `SHA256SUMS`), and no `v1.0.0` tag exists. `phasecont.fit` ships (API approved 2026-11-16).
3. Decided 2026-11-14 (map plan, decision 1): the launch ships slice 0, which refuses map models, whether or not the later map slices are done. Superseded on 2026-11-16, when map slices 0 to 8 were all done, so the launch ships full map support.
4. Deferred by the owner on 2026-11-16 until after the release: the File Exchange route (a GitHub-linked entry; whether one that ships MEX and DLL files is accepted, and whether it shows in the Add-On Explorer, is not confirmed with MathWorks).
5. Approved 2026-11-16: one package per platform next to the all-platform one. Built on 2026-11-17 on branch `per-platform-mltbx`: `scripts/packageFileName.m` names the files (`PhaseCont-<version>-<arch>.mltbx` and `PhaseCont-<version>.mltbx`), `PHASECONT_PLATFORMS` picks the platforms of the `package` task, `release.yml` builds all four, installs each one-platform package and the all-platform package on its runner and attaches all four in one `gh release create`, and `scripts/checkPackageFileName.m` makes `cleanInstallCheck.m` refuse a package of another platform or version. Checks: cargo fmt, clippy, test (2426 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored) and deny ok; `PackageTest` 42 total, 41 passed, 0 failed, 1 incomplete. The owner gave the go for a `release.yml` dry run of the new jobs on 2026-11-17. That run (44203165232, 6994767) built every library and MEX file and passed `licenses`, then failed in `load-el8` with exit code 141 right after `ldd --version` printed its first line, so `package`, `install` and `release` were skipped and the new jobs never ran. The step runs under `bash -e -o pipefail`, and `ldd --version | head -n 1` fails when `head` exits before `ldd` has written all its output and `ldd` dies of SIGPIPE; the loop reproduced it 46 times in 2000 on Fedora, and `sed -n 1p`, which reads all its input, failed 0 times in 2000. The step and `scripts/check-ldd` now use `sed -n 1p`, and the other pipes into a reader that can quit early (`codesign -dv | grep -qx` in `scripts/check-adhoc-signature`, the `vsce ls` checks in `ci.yml` and the entitlement check in `macos-jit.yml`) now read a captured string. The dry run ran again on the fix (44217657694, 8591162, 2026-11-17) and passed: every job succeeded and `release` was skipped, as it is in a `workflow_dispatch` run. The `package` job built `PhaseCont-1.0.0.mltbx`, `PhaseCont-1.0.0-win64.mltbx`, `PhaseCont-1.0.0-glnxa64.mltbx` and `PhaseCont-1.0.0-maca64.mltbx`, and all six `install` jobs (one-platform and all-platform on each runner) passed. Immutable releases were turned on for the repo on 2026-11-17 (owner go, "ok to go on the rest"). They apply to releases published from then on, so the 1.0.0-preview release stays editable. The `gh release create` step of `release.yml` makes a draft, uploads the assets and then publishes, so the assets are attached before the release becomes immutable. The same day Dependabot moved to monthly grouped updates and `ci.yml` got a `concurrency` block that cancels the older run of a pull request.

Assistant, on branch `launch-prep` unless noted:

1. The M3 warning, with a test. Done on 2026-11-13. `simulate` warns once per `Model` object (a hidden notice flag on the handle, so a loop over one model warns once) and names every `@` option the model sets. A warning that is turned off does not use up the notice, so the next call after it is turned back on still warns. The phase-plane app turns the warning off around its own runs, since its notes already name the ignored options; its list now also holds `bound`, the name in `crates/pc-ode/src/options.rs` (it held only `bounds`, which that list does not have). Session replay calls the run loop directly and does not warn. The README has an "`@` options" section. Tests: five in `tests/TrajectoryTest.m` and one more check in `tests/AppTest.m`; `scripts/matlab-win --run runm3.m` (both files) gave 59 total, 59 passed, 0 failed.
2. The README: the feature list (homoclinic curves, delay equations, cycle normal forms, `MeshAdapt`, ensembles, LaTeX, and `fit` if it ships) and a section on what the `.ode` reader refuses (stochastic, `global`, DAE, Volterra, BVP, `sum`, `shift`, `special`, `export`, file tables, `#include` from MATLAB) (M2). Done on 2026-11-13. The feature list names every codim-2 label, the HOM curves, `MeshAdapt`, delay models, ensembles and LaTeX, and says which options are off unless passed; `fit` was left out then, since it was not on this branch; it was added on 2026-11-16 with the merge of `fit-ensemble`. The new section "What the `.ode` reader refuses" was checked against a MATLAB probe of each construct on 2026-11-13 (all give `phasecont:engine:model` and name the statement), and it also covers the old file format, a file with both ODEs and maps, `#include`, active comments, `set`, delay models outside `continuation` (`phasecont:engine:badArg`) and maps. The probe showed that `shift` and `del_shft` were refused with "wrong number of arguments"; 2774b6a makes lowering name them ("`shift` is not supported"), with a Rust test. The staged Windows DLL predates 2774b6a, so MATLAB shows the new message after the next restage.
3. The package Summary and Description in `scripts/packageToolbox.m`, which still describe v0.3. Done on 2026-11-13. They now name phase planes, continuation of equilibria and cycles, the codim-2 curves, homoclinic orbits, delay models, ensembles and LaTeX. `summaryAndDescriptionMakeOnlyTrueClaims` in `tests/PackageTest.m` pins the new summary, no longer forbids "phase plane" and "limit cycle" (both are true now), and forbids "matcont" and "map" or "maps" next to "maci64" and "intel". `scripts/matlab-win --run runpkg.m` (PackageTest) gave 38 total, 37 passed, 0 failed, 1 incomplete (`posixPathsMustBeAbsoluteWithoutBackslashes`, filtered on Windows by its assumption).
4. A CHANGELOG draft: the map note, the NaN padding of v1.0.0-preview LPC, PD and NS session rows, the rule to close MATLAB before an upgrade, the GPU devices that ran, and the options that default to off (`Lyapunov`, `InclinationFlips`). Done on 2026-11-13: `CHANGELOG.md`, with an "Unreleased" section (version not chosen, the toolbox still reports 1.0.0) and a 1.0.0 entry, now the 1.0.0-preview entry.
5. Metal in the MATLAB ensemble tests: add `"metal"` to the `GpuBackend` parameter of `tests/EnsembleTest.m` and set `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=metal` on the macOS MATLAB leg. Done on 2026-11-13. Metal has no double type, so its double precision case is always filtered, even when `PC_REQUIRE_GPU` names `metal`. The `matlab` job in `ci.yml` sets `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=metal` on macOS only. On Windows, `scripts/matlab-win --run runens.m` (EnsembleTest) gave 41 total, 38 passed, 0 failed, 3 incomplete (the three Metal cases) with `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda,opencl`; with `cuda,opencl,metal` the two Metal single precision cases failed as intended and the double case was filtered. It ran on macOS on b19fb4b (2026-11-16) and passed, with the Metal single precision cases run and the double case filtered. If GitHub moves the macOS runners to hardware without a Metal device, this leg fails; the `gpu-macos` job shows the device on macOS 15 and 26 today.
6. A load check of the glnxa64 binaries in a Rocky Linux 8 container (glibc 2.28) in CI. Done on 2026-11-13. The job first ran in the 2026-11-17 dry run, where its check step failed on its first line (item 5 of the owner list), so `scripts/check-ldd` did not run there. In the rerun on the fix (44217657694, 8591162) it ran in CI for the first time and passed under glibc 2.28: `libpckern.so` resolves every symbol, and `pckern.mexa64` resolves every symbol apart from the allowed MATLAB libraries. `scripts/check-ldd` runs `ldd -r` on each file and fails on a missing library, an undefined symbol, a missing symbol version or a file that is not a dynamic object; `--allow LIB` accepts one missing library and the undefined symbols that come with it, but never a missing version. The new `load-el8` job in `release.yml` runs it in `rockylinux/rockylinux:8` (pinned by digest) on the `bin-glnxa64` artifact: `libpckern.so` in full, and `pckern.mexa64` with the MATLAB libraries allowed. `package` waits for it. It runs in the `workflow_dispatch` dry run too. The script was tested on Fedora (glibc 2.44) with small libraries built for each failure case, and on `target/debug/libpckern.so` (passes).
7. After the push: CI on the release commit, the binaries restaged, the full MATLAB suite on that commit, a `release.yml` dry run, then the tag.

| Path | State | How to test |

On `windows-11-arm` the tests that build emitted C skip, because the runner's gcc is x86_64 MinGW and an aarch64 process cannot load its DLLs | make the job blocking after a week of green runs; a native aarch64 C compiler on that runner would let the AOT C tests run there |

### Optimizations: done, dropped, next

Done (all keep the bits): private scratch on the GPU (RK4 single 100k members on the RTX 5060 69.7 to 7.7 ms), the JIT for double precision on the CPU (513 to 258 ms), skipped readback, device fills, `restrict`, the hoisted segment constants, the one-pass NaN and bounds check, global scratch with the layout rule by timing, member batching, and the CPU-only ensemble that no longer loads the GPU driver stack (first call +312 MB to +20 MB).

Dropped by measurement: DP5 member compaction against warp divergence (1.058x against a 1.1x gate), block sizes 128 and 256 (within 3% of 64), progress counters in the control block, and the launch-ramp warm start.

Gates passed only by owner decision: G1 on DP5 (1.00x against 1.2x) and the Arc Lorenz-96 n = 40 single row (0.43x against a 5% regression limit). One SPEC row is not met: the P36 row of SPEC section 14 asks for `vdp-rk4` at 100000 members on the CPU to run at least 1.3 times faster in both precisions. Double went from 513 to 258 ms (1.99x, from the JIT), but single went from 564 ms to 521 to 565 ms (about 1.0x). The single-precision JIT of 2026-11-16 (plan item C, branch `f32-jit`) meets the row: in 5 interleaved rounds of `pc-cli ensemble --bench` on Windows, single `vdp-rk4` at 100000 members took 516 ms without the JIT and 292 ms with it (1.77x), and 560 against 283 ms in a second set (1.98x), or 1.93x against the 564 ms baseline. Double stayed within noise (262 against 260 ms). The bench hashes did not change (single `78cf287b0a170177`, double `8013014065fe54f1`).

Roofline for RK4 at 100k members: RTX 5060 at 6 to 13% of its FP32 peak in single and 54 to 114% of FP64 in double, Arc 140T at about 15% in single, CPU at 1 to 4% of its vector peak.

Next, ranked by expected gain, all bit-exact:

1. Done 2026-11-16: the single-precision JIT (1.93x, see above).
2. Several members per thread and SIMD across members on the CPU (estimate 3x to 6x, a week or more).
3. The Arc live-value spill in the emitted right-hand side (Lorenz-96 n = 40 back to about 330 ms at 10k, and a higher state cap).
4. Done 2026-11-16: Nsight Compute 2026.4.0 and Nsight Systems 2026.5.2 on the RTX 5060, through `pc-cli` in WSL (CUDA driver 13.5, NVRTC 13.5, not the NVRTC 13.1 inside MATLAB), at 100000 members. The reports are kept outside the repo, in the `nsight` folder next to the staged DLL. Under Nsight Compute the launch ramp sees replay time, so launches stay short and its throughput and occupancy numbers describe short launches. Shares of wall time below come from Nsight Systems.
   - RK4 single (7.4 ms): the four kernels take 1.19 ms. The copy of the whole control block back after every launch (8 u32 per member, 3.2 MB) takes 1.58 ms, uploads 0.46 ms, and the GPU is busy about 5 ms of the 7.4. The missing FP32 time is host work and that copy, not the kernel. Branch efficiency 100%, no divergent branches, 46 registers, 72% achieved occupancy. Line 840 skipped progress counters in the control block at about 1 percent of a launch, but that was measured before private scratch made the kernel 9 times faster, so a small summary word in place of the full copy is worth a measured retry (estimate up to 20% on this row, less on longer jobs).
   - RK4 double (50 ms) and Lorenz-96 n = 40 double: the FP64 pipe is 82% busy, branch efficiency 100%, no divergence. Lorenz-96 double uses 255 registers and spills (2.4 GB of local loads and 2.3 GB of stores in one launch), single uses 168 and does not. On a consumer card FP64 runs at 1/64 rate, so Lorenz-96 n = 40 double on the GPU (1.07 s) is not faster than the CPU (1.02 s). No change is planned for this.
   - DP5 double, all 421 launches under Nsight Compute: branch efficiency 93 to 94% until the tail. Active threads per warp go from 28.6 of 32 early to 24.5 at launch 260, 20.9 at 312 and 11.3 at 364, as members finish at different step counts, then back to 26 to 31 in the last few launches. Compaction (G4) was dropped at 1.06x, so this stays as it is.
5. Several devices at once (estimate 1.5x to 1.6x; adds a device list, so it needs an API decision).

Bit-level audit (2026-11-16): 22 candidates (bit masks, bitsets, packed flags, smaller ids) were checked against the rules above, and none passed. The best one, a finiteness scan that tests the exponent bits of a block of numbers at once (`pc-linalg/src/condense.rs`, also usable in `model.rs` and `dense.rs`), keeps the bits and runs 2.3 times faster on the scan, but the scan is about 3% of a sequential condensation solve. The others run once per model at compile time (about 1 ms or less) or touch code that is not hot. The time goes to the per-point clone in `run.rs`, libm, faer and the ensemble tape dispatch, so the list above stays as it is. Two small cleanups remain for later: that finiteness scan, and `pack_row` in `pc-ffi/src/api.rs`, which clones a tangent it never packs.

Owner decisions of 2026-11-16: the fast-math tier is allowed, opt-in, with a warning ("allow fast math operation but include warning"), and the single-precision half of the P36 1.3x row is to be met ("see what we can change so it will fit"). Both wait for a plan that the owner approves.

Fast-math tier, built 2026-11-17 on `fast-math` (plan B, decisions B1 to B4 accepted; not pushed). Commits 7fca0ca (the `math` key of a job, `"exact"` or `"fma"`, default exact; `fma` is refused off CUDA double), 57fa7cf (the CUDA kernel cache keys on the tier, and NVRTC gets `--fmad=true` only for fma), e12273e (`phasecont.ensemble(..., FastMath=true)`, the `phasecont:ensemble:fastMath` warning on every run, `Label.Math`, and `phasecont:ensemble:fastMathBackend` on CPU, OpenCL, Metal and CUDA single) and 718f900 (the self-test report); 0573a7b and the commit after it test the tier on every entry path: `run_on` refuses fma on CPU, OpenCL, Metal and CUDA single before any device is touched and takes it on CUDA double, the NVRTC options per tier, the PTX of a multiply-add holds `fma.rn.f64` only in the fma tier, the cubin route passes the tier on (a cubin records its options, so its bytes alone do not show fusion), the CLI `math` key (default exact, wrong values exit 2, fma off CUDA double exits 2 with the tier message, fma on CUDA double runs and is labeled fma), and `Backend="auto"` with single precision and FastMath raises `fastMathBackend`. Measured on the RTX 5060 through MATLAB at 100000 members, double: vdp RK4 57.5 to 38.9 ms (1.48x), vdp DP5 157.5 to 107.9 ms (1.46x). The exact-tier bench hashes are unchanged (vdp-rk4 single `78cf287b0a170177`, double `8013014065fe54f1`). `pc-cli gpu selftest --backend cuda --precision double` on the RTX 5060 reports `"fma": {"max_difference": 2.6e-16, "status_differ": 0}` and passes. `refine` never passes FastMath, and `fit` runs on the CPU in double, so neither can reach the tier. Checks: fmt, clippy, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test` 2445 passed, 0 failed, 13 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok; the full MATLAB suite after a restage, 890 total, 881 passed, 0 failed, 9 incomplete. Owner items, none blocking:
  1. With `Backend="auto"` and `FastMath=true`, a run that lands off CUDA double is refused with `fastMathBackend` rather than run in the exact tier. SPEC states this as settled; say if auto should fall back to exact with a warning instead.
  2. The CLI self-test key name `"fma"`, with `max_difference` and `status_differ` (null on every other device).
  3. A compile or launch error in the fma jobs of the self-test counts as a failure (exit 1), though the difference itself is report only.
  4. Whether `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` should return the fma report too. It does not today; adding a field changes the public MATLAB API.
  5. The MATLAB test `fastMathOnCudaWarnsAndIsLabelled` checks both tiers against the closed form at 1e-9 (10 times Rtol, the rule of the refine test); both measured 2.74e-11. There is no bound on fma against exact, since no source gives one.

Owner answers of 2026-11-17 (items 1, 3 and 5 settled by the advisor at the owner's request, item 2 answered again the same day): 1. the refusal stays. 3. A refused or failed fma job is a self-test error (exit 1), and the message names the fma tier (`SelfTestError::Fma`). 4. Approved: `phasecont.gpuSelfTest` returns a `FastMath` field, empty unless the run is on CUDA in double, with `MaxDifference` (the largest `|fma - exact| / (1 + |exact|)` over members whose Status is the same in both runs) and `StatusDiffer`; it has no tolerance and does not count in `Pass`. 5. The 1e-9 stays. It is 10 times Rtol, the project's rule for a closed-form check, not a bound on fma. A literature search found no published bound on fused against unfused results, and closed-form checks in other ODE test suites use factors of about 2 to 15 times the tolerance. 2. The self-test key stays `"fma"` (with `max_difference` and `status_differ`). The owner first answered "no" to a question that named the job key `"math"` by mistake; asked again with the right key, the owner chose to keep `"fma"`.

### Against XPPAUT

Integration speed (own models, wall time, `pc-cli` against XPPAUT `qualrk` and `cvode`): Morris-Lecar T = 30000, 74 ms against 108 and 107 ms; a 40-cell chain (80 states), 71 ms against 485 and 700 ms; a 200-cell chain (400 states), 74 ms against 262 and 3655 ms. XPPAUT starts 2 ms faster. Ensembles, continuation and interactive latency were not compared.

PhaseCont is ahead in continuation (two-parameter curves with codim-2 labels and normal forms, homoclinic curves, delay equations), GPU ensembles, the JIT and AOT paths, and LaTeX output. An XPP user still cannot switch for stochastic, event, DAE, Volterra, BVP and file-table models, other integrators (only Dormand-Prince), Poincare maps, Lyapunov exponents, phase response curves and averaging, the data browser, range integration, and a migration guide.

### After the launch

1. Full map support (M1). Done on 2026-11-16, slices 0 to 8 (see "Map support phase" above).
2. A migration guide from XPPAUT and a getting-started page, with one delay or homoclinic example.
3. More integrators (a stiff method and fixed-step methods, so `@ meth` can be honored), then Poincare sections and Lyapunov exponents.
4. Event models (`global`), then stochastic models.
5. The CPU speed items above.
6. An unload or copy-on-load fix so an upgrade does not fail while MATLAB holds `pckern.dll`.
7. The VS Code Marketplace listing (needs the owner's publisher account).

- Eviction after a launch error ran on a device on 2026-11-08. (Device-fault review on 2026-11-09, next two bullets.) `pc-ensemble` has a Cargo feature `fault-injection` (off by default, no new dependency, SPEC GPU ensemble engine) with `pc_ensemble::fault::arm_launch_fault()`. The next launch then uses a configuration the driver rejects (0 blocks for CUDA, a work group far above the device limit for OpenCL), so the error comes from the driver. `kernel_cache.rs` runs the case as its last step: warm run, armed run returns `GpuLaunch` with `launch` in the text, the device keeps 0 entries, the next run compiles again and gives the same states. Commands: `PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda cargo test -p pc-ensemble --features fault-injection --test kernel_cache the_cuda` ended `1 passed` in WSL on the RTX 5060. OpenCL has no ICD in WSL, so a Windows build ran both backends: `cargo test --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu -p pc-ensemble --features fault-injection --test kernel_cache --no-run`, the `.exe` copied to `%USERPROFILE%\phasecont-stage\test`, then `WSLENV=PC_REQUIRE_GPU PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda,opencl ./kernel_cache.exe` ended `2 passed; 0 failed`. An unarmed first attempt exposed that the cases of one backend share the cache, so the case is a step of `steps` and not a test of its own. What it does not show: an invalid launch configuration leaves no sticky error in the context (the sticky case is in the next bullet), and a lost device and a watchdog reset (`GpuTimeout`) did not run on a device. The `gpu-pocl` job now passes the feature, so pocl runs the OpenCL case (not yet run on GitHub). The Arc 140T was not used, since the test takes device 0 of each backend.
- Device-fault review, 2026-11-09 (read of `gpu.rs`, `cuda.rs`, `opencl.rs`, `kept.rs`, then device runs on the RTX 5060 through CUDA in WSL). Fixed: (1) the OpenCL objects now sit in a `Quiet` wrapper (`gpu.rs`), because dropping the `Shared` fields one by one could abort the process when two releases failed in a row (four CPU tests, and a negative control without `Quiet` aborted); (2) `Kept::forget_if` removes the entry a failed run used and not a newer one (unit test); (3) a sticky driver error now says to restart the process. Measured: after `CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS` from a PTX kernel that writes through null, `CudaContext::new` in the same process fails with the same error, so evict and recreate does not recover from a sticky error. The old claim in SPEC that the eviction clears a sticky error was wrong and is corrected there. The panic hook `arm_panic_fault()` and a cancel-before-run case are steps of `kernel_cache.rs`: `cargo test -p pc-ensemble --features fault-injection --test kernel_cache` ended `2 passed` (CUDA ran on the device, OpenCL skipped for lack of an ICD in WSL, so the OpenCL panic path and the `Quiet` wrapper had not run on a device at that point; they ran on 2026-11-09 in the Windows build: `WSLENV=PC_REQUIRE_GPU PC_REQUIRE_GPU=cuda,opencl ./kernel_cache.exe` ended `2 passed; 0 failed` in 1.32 s on the RTX 5060, with the panic step, the cancel step and the launch-fault step on both backends). `PC_RUN_STICKY=1 cargo test -p pc-ensemble --test sticky -- --ignored` ended `1 passed`. Not done: whether real OpenCL drivers fail a release after a device loss (the `Quiet` fix covers it if they do), Metal (not read), and a GpuCompile error still evicts the entry without a probe.
- DDE-BIFTOOL license, checked on 2026-11-07 through the GitHub API (repository DDE-BIFTOOL/DDE-BIFTOOL, branch `master`, read-only). The repo has no license file at its root. `tools/license.txt` is BSD-2-Clause (K.U. Leuven, Engelborghs, Luzyanina, Samaey, Roose, Verheyden, Sieber, Wage, Pieroux), and `tools/bsd-license.txt` is the same text with only K.U. Leuven named. `tools/ku-license.txt` is a non-commercial, internal-research license. The four source files I read (`dde_coll_jac.m`, `dde_psol_jac_res.m`, `dde_psol_monodromy.m`, `dde_coll_create.m` in `ddebiftool/`) have no license notice in their headers, only an SVN `$Id`, so the only statement is `tools/license.txt`. That makes BSD-2-Clause the best-supported reading for the current `master`, and `ku-license.txt` looks like the notice of the old KU Leuven releases (up to 3.0). I did not confirm that from a release note. A port still records the file name and revision in its header and in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and CLAUDE.md rule 3 say BSD-2-Clause without this caveat. Settled on 2026-11-08 (owner delegated the decisions, "decisions make them as u recomended go ahead"): the caveat is now in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, which also says that no code has been ported from DDE-BIFTOOL. The wording in CLAUDE.md rule 3 is local and was left alone.

## Blocked

## Open questions

- Detected period of an LC start (round E item 8, 2026-11-19, SPEC section 11, "Limit cycles from a simulation"). Without `Period`, the run takes the latest return of the orbit to its last point that lies within max(2·dmin, 1e-3·D). The factor 2 and the floor 1e-3 are my choices with no reference behind them, and they change the result. A probe on a Rössler system I wrote (a = b = 0.2, period doubling near c = 2.83) found:
  1. Settled orbits pick right: T = 5.749 at c = 2.5 and T = 11.545 at c = 3.5, both simulated to t = 600.
  2. An unsettled period-one orbit picks twice the period, the cycle traversed twice: c = 2.70 or 2.75 to t = 120, and c = 2.80 to t = 600, where the one-turn return is 0.6 to 4% of D away. `anUnsettledOrbitNearADoublingTakesTwoTurns` pins this.
  3. A period-two cycle with a split below 1e-3·D would pick the period-one cycle. That needs a parameter very close to the doubling, and the probe did not reach one. At c = 2.86 the split is 5% of D and the pick is right.
  Near a doubling, one orbit cannot tell an unsettled period-one orbit from a period-two cycle with a small split, so no cutoff value removes both wrong picks. What is built: the warning `phasecont:continuation:detectedPeriod` gives d/D, names the latest rejected shorter return ("T may be a multiple of the period") and the closest return, and SPEC states both wrong picks. Owner decisions: (a) sign off the values 2 and 1e-3 or pick others; (b) whether a run with a rejected shorter return should refuse with `phasecont:model:badStart` and ask for `Period`, as `missingTime` does for t. I recommend (b) yes: it costs one extra call in the doubled case and removes the quiet wrong pick of item 2.
  Settled 2026-11-19 for (b): the owner chose to refuse. A run with a rejected shorter return stops with `phasecont:model:badStart` and names both periods for `Period`. This also refuses a settled period-two cycle such as c = 3.5 (T = 11.545), which needs `Period` now. Tests `aDoubledCycleIsRefusedWithBothPeriods` and `anUnsettledOrbitNearADoublingIsRefused` (CycleOrbitTest 15 of 15). Still open: (a), the values 2 and 1e-3.

- Open from the review of the session evaluator fix (2026-11-17, branch `session-evaluator`). Items 1 and 3 are settled; item 2 is a small follow-up.
  1. Settled 2026-11-17 (owner said yes). `Model.loadobj` used to rebuild a saved model with the evaluator the MAT file holds, so a MAT file whose model records `"aot"` started the C compiler when it loaded. It now keeps `"jit"` and `"fallback"` and otherwise builds with `phasecont.config("Codegen")`, the same rule as `sessionCodec.buildModel`. Test: `AotTest/aMatFileThatRecordsAotStartsNoCompiler`.
  2. `sessionCodec.buildModel` compiles the model before it compares the source hash. Both values come from the same file, so this is an integrity check and not a guard, but checking the hash first would skip a build that is about to be refused. Checked 2026-11-17: the hash is computed in Rust by `model_from_ode` (`pc-ffi` `parse.rs`), not in MATLAB, so checking it before the compile needs the expected hash passed into `Model.build` through a new option of `Model.fromSource`, which is a public API change. The gain is small, since the codec never picks `"aot"` from a file, so the build it would skip is a JIT compile of text that was already parsed. Left as is unless the owner wants the option.
  3. Settled 2026-11-17. The CLI read the recorded evaluator without checking it, so a misspelled evaluator replayed on the JIT without a message. `Session::parse` now accepts only `"jit"`, `"fallback"` and `"aot"`, the set the MATLAB reader accepts, and `pc-cli session replay` exits 2 for any other value. Test: `session::tests::reading_refuses_what_it_does_not_know`.

- Open from the analysis tools, slice 1 (2026-11-16, SPEC section 8ac, `Point.classify` and `model_jacobian`, branch `analysis-tools`). Three values are project choices with no reference behind them, and each needs the owner's sign-off:
  1. A star (J a multiple of I) is told from a degenerate node by the band ‖J − (T/2)I‖_F ≤ √Tol·s. The square root is there because the eigenvalue split of a near-double root grows like the square root of the perturbation; the factor itself is mine.
  2. A multiplier of a map is on the unit circle when ||μ| − 1| ≤ Tol. The band is absolute, since |μ| = 1 is already a scale-free test.
  3. In one dimension, a zero eigenvalue is reported as `"zero eigenvalue"` and not as half-stable. Telling a half-stable point apart needs f″ at the point, and `model_jacobian` returns only the first derivative.

- Last bits across machines (2026-11-16). The `hopf_curve_pin` hashes differ between this laptop and the Linux, Windows and macOS runners, with the same rustc 1.99.1 and the same point and special counts. The Brusselator hash differs on each runner, and the double-Hopf hash is the same on all three runners but not the laptop's. The cause is not known (libm, a faer code path chosen by CPU features, or something else). Until it is, "keeps the bits" means the same bits on one machine, and the pin tests compare hashes only off CI.

- Settled on 2026-11-08 for E8b (the owner delegated the decisions: "decisions make them as u recomended go ahead"; recorded in SPEC section 8b-3). Numbers are those of the old list.

  3. Design B, a post hoc linear solve for the adjoint. It is built and tested (df47eb1, `hom_adjoint.rs`): `solve_adjoint`, `orient_adjoint` and `inclination_flips`. No HOM run calls them yet.
  4. HomCont tests 15 and 16 and IEQUIB = 2 stay deferred.
  5. The orientation index is not exposed. Its sign convention in Champneys, Kuznetsov and Sandstede 1996 is unconfirmed.
  6. The 1996 paper stays unread. The formulas marked confirmed in SPEC section 8b-2 rest on `homcont.f90`, the manual and the slides.

- Settled on 2026-11-08, E8b item 2, the guard for IFS and IFU. The owner wrote "do what u recomended build a -c"; I read it as "build (a), fall back to (c)" and the owner did not confirm that reading. Option (a) is built: the bracket floor `IF_BRACKET_FLOOR` = 1e-8 through `Problem::crossing_ok` (6e77854, 96947c1, 737efac, 4fd6f80), and the labels are wired behind the `inclination_flips` key (default false) and the MATLAB option `InclinationFlips` (bf5ea0c). Acceptance 6 of SPEC section 8b-3 passes with a smaller margin than the target: the smallest larger bracket end is 8.695e-5, 1.5 percent under the 1e-4 target, and the SPEC says so. The floor value is mine, not a published one. The text below is the case before the guard was built. Without a guard a HOM run reports labels that mean nothing: in the models of SPEC section 8b, tests 2 and 3, IFS is zero in the limit of large T and sign-indefinite noise at finite T (section 8b-2, check 2). SPEC section 8b-3 fixed a test before measuring: adopt a floor on the cosine between the eigenvector and the end of the adjoint only if noise and signal are two orders of magnitude apart. They are not (measured on 2026-11-08, T = 20, 30 and 40, tables in SPEC section 8b-3):
  - The cosine is 0.316 at the first point of test 2 at T = 40, against 1.000 on the oracle curve. Noise values run from 1e-17 to 0.3.
  - In a generic model the cosine goes through zero at a real zero, so a floor on it would hide real zeros.
  - The ratio of the norm of the adjoint at the orbit end to its stacked norm overlaps too: noise 4e-26 to 4.8e-5, oracle 5e-14 to 1.4e-5.
  - The engine's IFS changes sign 3, 2, 2 times (test 2) and 0, 4, 4 times (test 3) at T = 20, 30, 40, and those flips are spurious.
  - The value of IFS does separate on these three models: the noise is at most 2.2e-12, and the larger end value of each bracket of the oracle zeros is at least 2.4e-4 (about eight orders). A floor on the larger bracket end value would need a new hook on the `Problem` trait that `detect_inner` in `locate.rs` calls. A floor on the test value itself would break the location of the zero. The scale of such a floor depends on T, the leading eigenvalue and the normalization of the adjoint, no reference gives a value, and three models do not fix it for others (rule 9). It is not built.
  - Owner choices: (a) adopt the bracket floor with a stated value and its scale, (b) ship IFS and IFU with the key default off and a warning that labels at large T are unreliable, (c) leave IFS and IFU out. Recommended: (a), with the value of the floor set by the owner after a test on a different model with a real inclination flip, because the bracket floor is the only measured separator and it removes the wrong labels. (b) keeps them: test 2 gets 3 wrong labels at T = 20, so with the key on, acceptance 4 of SPEC section 8b-3 fails by construction, and the key only hides the problem when it is off. (c) is the fallback if no value can be signed. Nothing is wired until then.
  - The oracle (Sandstede, a, b, c, beta = -0.25, 1, -3, 1) has three zeros of IFS between alpha = 0 and 3. The independent numpy solve (phi divided by its stacked norm, sign fixed by continuity in alpha) puts them at 1.8966, 2.2847 and 2.6382 for T = 20 with 1000 intervals, and at 1.8986, 2.2829 and 2.6336 for T = 40. With 400, 800 and 1200 intervals at T = 20 they settle (1.8995, 2.2822, 2.6321; 1.8969, 2.2845, 2.6375; 1.8964, 2.2849, 2.6385). Each lies inside one of the three brackets of the engine run, so they are zeros of IFS for this model and not engine noise. Whether they are inclination flips in the limit of large T was not checked. The acceptance test for the oracle zero uses alpha up to 2.1, which holds only the first.
  - Second parameter set of the oracle model, (a, b, c, beta) = (-0.5, 1.5, -3, 1), lambda_S = -2 (measured 2026-11-08 with a throwaway test, not committed): four sign changes of IFS at each of T = 20, 30 and 40, the numpy sweep (steps of 0.1 in alpha) has four in intervals that overlap the four engine brackets in the same order, and |IFS| over the run is between 7.4e-4 and 7.8 at every T, against a noise of at most 2.2e-12 in the other models. This changes lambda_S but not the model, so it does not fix one floor value for other models.
- Settled on 2026-11-07: a zero at the first point of a run is labeled (d4075f2, owner answer "sure").
- Open from P13: no pole of b, c or d was observed in any E7b model, so the `POLE_TOL` guard in `locate.rs` has not dropped a real pole. A model with a resonance that makes a bordered solve singular would test it. The value 1e-2 has no source in a reference, so it is left as it is. The first-point rule reuses it without a new constant. Update of 2026-11-08: the guard has synthetic unit tests, and the delay Hopf curves of the later steps cross real poles at BT and ZH, which the `dde-hopf` tests cover. What no test has seen is the pole of a cycle curve at a resonance (a model for it would need its own continuation), so that case stays unobserved and the value stays unsourced. Update of 2026-11-09: a sweep of 1001 synthetic brackets and three new pins are in the review pass above, and the value stays at 1e-2 as an owner item.
- Settled on 2026-11-07: `Inf` runs through the JVM wait loop, so the Compile button of `LatexStudio` and `phasecont.latex.build` can be cancelled with Ctrl-C. The interactive Ctrl-C check was done on 2026-11-13 (see below).
- Settled on 2026-11-08, scheduler and Ctrl-C (487a79c): `runLoop.m` collects points in chunks instead of growing the array one row at a time, and `everyExitPathFreesTheRun` (a `pollHook` error as the proxy for Ctrl-C) checks that a normal end, an error and an interrupt each free the run id. The MATLAB suite then passed (513 passed, 0 failed, 5 filtered). A real Ctrl-C cannot be sent through `matlab -batch`, and a MEX call cannot be interrupted, so the interactive check stays open. The run has no Timeout (only the LaTeX build has one), the registry does not grow, memory is linear in the stored points and bounded by `max_steps`, and the poll schedule is 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 19 ms and then 50 ms.
- Ctrl-C spike, 2026-11-09 (time-boxed, scratch scripts, nothing kept in the repo). Windows `matlab.exe -batch` ran a script that called `phasecont.internal.runBounded` with `Timeout = Inf` on `ping -n 60 127.0.0.1`, and a PowerShell helper sent `CTRL_C_EVENT` with `AttachConsole` and `GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent`. Attaching to the `MATLAB.exe` pid failed (error 6, it has no console of its own). Attaching to the `matlab.exe` launcher succeeded and the event was sent (`sent=True`), but 10 s later the script had not reached its `catch`, `MATLAB.exe` was still running and so was `ping`. So a console Ctrl-C does not interrupt a batch-mode MATLAB, and this route cannot test the claim. The probe tree was killed afterwards. Limits that stay: Ctrl-C is not tested by any script, the claim rests on the `pause` loop, and one interactive check is still the owner's. How: in the desktop, run `r = phasecont.internal.runBounded(["ping","-n","60","127.0.0.1"], pwd, Inf)`, press Ctrl-C after a few seconds, and expect the error `Operation terminated by user` and no `PING.EXE` left in Task Manager.
- Ctrl-C result, 2026-11-13 (passed, nothing kept in the repo). A PowerShell helper started a desktop `matlab.exe` of its own, waited until `runBounded(sleeper.exe, tempdir, Inf)` ran, confirmed that the foreground window belonged to that MATLAB, and sent Ctrl+C with `keybd_event`. MATLAB printed `Operation terminated by user` at the `pause` in the wait loop, and `stopTree` ended the program before any other cleanup (`sleeper.exe` was gone). A console Ctrl-C cannot reach a `-nodesktop` MATLAB, which has no console, so the check needs the desktop. The run found a defect: the output file was deleted while the program still held it, which warned and left a `tp*.txt` file. Two `onCleanup` objects in one function run in creation order. `runBounded` now has one handler that stops the process tree, waits up to 5 s and then deletes the file (8682f09, with a test that a normal end, a timeout and a failed start leave no file). The second desktop run, on the fixed code, showed no warning and no leftover file. The helper's Alt tap, used to win foreground rights, opened MATLAB's navigation shortcut dialog in two runs and swallowed the key, so it sends F24 now. One earlier attempt sent a console Ctrl-C to a console that held a `matlab.exe` this session did not start (pid 28552). It was stopped by the guard that refuses when a process on the console is not the helper's own, and it is not known whether that process noticed. The owner's desktop MATLAB (started 2026-11-12 11:50) was never signalled.
- Settled on 2026-11-07 (74ed4ee, owner answer "pad"): `Session.load` reads the old rows of a v1.0.0-preview LPC, PD or NS curve (width 24 for LPC and PD, 29 for NS at n = 2, ntst = 2, ncol = 2) by their stride and fills the new tests with NaN. Replaying such a record reports a structure change, because the stride differs.

PhaseCont may write its own `.vsix` packager later. No code for it exists, and the dev-only exception recorded under P12 stays in place until then.
- Settled, Dependabot alert 1: GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249 (CVE-2026-118764, medium), `minimatch` 10.2.6 under `@vscode/vsce` 4.1.0 in `editors/vscode/package-lock.json`, scope development. The fixed version is 11.0.1 and `vsce` asks for `^10.2.6`, so a plain update does not reach it. The runtime copy of `minimatch` (5.1.9, under `vscode-languageclient`) is outside the vulnerable range (>= 7.0.0, < 11.0.1), and the `.vsix` has no `node_modules`, so no user installs the vulnerable copy. The owner chose the recommended option ("pick what u reomended", 2026-11-08), and the alert was dismissed on GitHub on 2026-11-08 as `tolerable_risk` with that reason in the comment. No `overrides` entry was added, so no dependency changed. Revisit when `vsce` moves to `minimatch` 11. Update 2026-11-17: with `vscode-languageclient` 11 the runtime copy is `minimatch` 10.2.6, inside the range, so the reason above no longer holds for the bundle; see the open owner item of that date under P11.

- Owner: File Exchange route for v0.3, checked on 2026-10-29. Direct upload accepts BSD only. Apache-2.0 is allowed only for a GitHub-linked entry. The R2026b help says a package with MEX or DLL files cannot be submitted to File Exchange, and it does not say whether a GitHub-linked entry is exempt. The repo is private, which blocks the GitHub link route. Ask MathWorks before relying on either route.
- Settled in P8: release builds remap the repo and cargo home paths, and `scripts/check-host-paths` fails on a host path left in a binary.
- After 1.0: the gateway never unloads `pckern.dll`. Uninstalling or upgrading the toolbox in a MATLAB session that has used PhaseCont fails with "Permission denied" and leaves a stale folder under `%APPDATA%\MathWorks\MATLAB Add-Ons` until MATLAB exits. The README tells users to close MATLAB before an upgrade. An unload or copy-on-load design waits for a later release.
- Settled in P8: AOT works on Linux and macOS. `AotTest` passes on the hosted ubuntu and macos runners since `internal.aotBuild` passes empty `LINKEXPORT` and `LINKEXPORTVER`. The AOT test in `pc-codegen` still runs gcc only.
- Settled on 2026-10-28: the in-MATLAB part of the macOS arm64 check in SPEC section 4 moved to P7a. P7a did not run it; it is deferred (see below).
- The P1 questions are settled for P2: division follows IEEE; `mod` is `fmod(x,y)` plus `y` when the remainder is negative, which probe `fn-mod-more` confirmed on eight more cases; `if` evaluates both branches and selects one.
- Open from P7a: `model_from_ode` parses one in-memory string with no file loader, so `#include` lines fail with a diagnostic. The MATLAB layer has to inline included files, or a later task adds a loader callback.
- Carried into the open questions from the P1, P2 and P7a notes:
  - Closed in P7a by 8cb0c84: `pc-ffi` parses on an 8 MiB worker thread and rejects input over 16 MiB. Each Model handle owns an 8 MiB thread that lowers the model and runs either evaluator, so `JitEntries` never moves between threads and needs no `unsafe impl`. The diagnostics JSON keeps the first 1000 diagnostics.
  - The JIT inside MATLAB on Windows:
    - `ModelTest` evaluates a model whose JIT frame passes 4 KiB inside MATLAB (it asserts `jit_max_frame > 4096`), so the inline stack probes run on Windows.
    - `cranelift-jit` registers no Windows unwind information. No panic or exception may unwind through JIT code, which rule 7's `catch_unwind` on the Rust side of every call already requires.
  - The JIT inside MATLAB on macOS arm64 (moved from P2 by the owner on 2026-10-28). The Rust side of the SPEC section 4 check passes on the macOS runner already (see "Done").
- Deferred from P7a, with reasons:
  - (a) The MATLAB jobs on Linux and macOS, and the in-MATLAB macOS JIT check. MathWorks' batch token pilot was closed to new requests (checked 2026-10-29). They first ran in `ci.yml` on 2026-11-04, after the repo went public (see In progress).
  - (b) AOT through `mex`, moved to P8a on 2026-10-28.
  - (c) ASan has not run on GitHub yet; it runs on pull requests, the nightly schedule and manual runs. The licenses step in `pckern-win` passed on its first hosted run on 2026-11-04.
- Known minors from reviews, parked:
  - `special_tangent` threshold (checked 2026-11-10, code unchanged): the claim that refinement noise flips the LP orientation did not reproduce. `lp_start_orientation_does_not_depend_on_the_refinement_tolerance` (`crates/pc-engine/tests/special_start.rs`) refines the `fold` LP at `tol_refine` 1e-6, 1e-8, 1e-10 and 1e-12 on both evaluators, and direction 1 leaves toward increasing x every time, because the alpha component of a refined LP tangent is far below `RANK_TOL` (1e-8) and the start takes the first state component above it. Orientation by the largest component would flip the H semantics (direction 1 means alpha increasing, pinned by `h_start_keeps_the_hopf_equilibrium_branch_going`), and a rule that separates the two needs its own tolerance, so the rule stays. Run: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test -p pc-engine --test special_start`, 6 passed.
  - From the final P7a review (2026-10-29):
    - `Stable` is false for a point whose eigenvalues are NaN. The engine fills NaN only after an internal fault, and carrying the last value forward would be a silent default, so it stays.
    - Fixed: the engine now rejects a `stride` that is not the run's (audit item 7b, 6bce632), and `count == 0` reaches the library (audit item 7a).
    - The `wsl.exe` commands in `buildfile.m` now refuse a path with a double quote, `$`, a backtick, a backslash, `%` or a line break (`scripts/wslArg.m`, `phasecont:build:unsafePath`), since wsl.exe and bash cannot take those escaped reliably. (`scripts/build-pckern` and the Linux and macOS branch of `buildfile.m` follow `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`; the branch has not run on a Linux or macOS MATLAB.)
    - `continuation` rejects a start Point from another Model object with the same source. (Plotting a `Branch` after its Model is deleted works; the variables are labeled u1, u2, ...)
    - `BuildTest/gatewayIsBuiltIntoTheBinFolder` is filtered, not failed, when the gateway is missing, so that `buildtool test` runs without a build. `buildtool package` depends on `mex`, and `packageToolbox` refuses a stage without the MEX file.
  - AOT cache: a cache hit trusts `isfile` alone. A library that fails to load now gets its folder removed and is built once more before the `phasecont:aot:load` warning (`AotTest/damagedCachedLibraryIsRebuilt`). A staged rename was left out: a failed compile leaves no library, and renaming a folder that antivirus is scanning can fail on Windows. If the folder cannot be removed (another MATLAB holds the library), the warning comes at once.
  - AOT cache folder: on Linux the cache is now `/tmp/phasecont-aot-<uid>`, created with `mkdir -m 700`, and `aotCacheRoot` refuses a folder that another user owns or that the group or others can write (`phasecont:aot:cacheFolder`, so the model falls back to the JIT). The old shared `/tmp/phasecont-aot` is no longer read. Windows and macOS keep `tempdir/phasecont-aot` because their `tempdir` is per user. The pure check has tests (`AotCacheRootTest`). The Linux branch of `aotCacheRoot` has not run in MATLAB, since only Windows MATLAB is installed here. I ran its `mkdir -m 700` and `stat -c '%u %a'` calls in WSL and got `1000 700`. AotTest still builds in the real cache.
  - Licenses, settled 2026-11-10: each override folder has a `VERSION` file that lists the crate versions its text was taken from, one per line. `pc-cli licenses` uses the override only when the locked version is listed, and fails and names both versions when it is not, or when `VERSION` is missing or empty. The check runs only for a crate that ships no license file of its own. The 24 folders are pinned to the versions in `Cargo.lock` (an unpinned `equator` 0.6.0 and `equator-macro` 0.6.0 ship their own files). Tests: `crates/pc-cli/tests/licenses.rs` (four new cases). A `Cargo.lock` bump of an overridden crate now stops the licenses step until someone checks the new release text and adds its version.
  - Package: the stage root's `matlabPath` entry is an empty string, as `packageToolbox` writes it, and `PackageTest` pins that. (`PackageTest` now reads the metadata through the XML parser, so it does not depend on attribute spelling.)
  - Clean install: the `notAddon` negative path is untested, since it needs a real install. The add-on folder setting read prints a note when it fails.
  - Clean install: the `.mltbx` is uploaded only after the check passes, and a failure in the licenses step stops the `matlab-windows` job.
  - AOT load, settled 2026-11-10 (owner approved the interface items): `pc-codegen` exports `pc_aot_tag`, an FNV-1a 64 hash of the C text before that function (`emit_c_tagged`), and `AotEval::open` compares it with the tag of the model before it calls `pc_aot_init`. A stale, misplaced or wrong-path library now gives `PC_ERR_MODEL` that names the tag. It checks the pairing and is not a security control, since the loader runs a library's initializers before any check. The C signatures and `abi_version` are unchanged.
  - `emit_c` and tables, 2026-11-10: `pc_engine::check_tables` is the "table reads a parameter" check that `finish()` makes. The `emit_c` handler in `pc-ffi` calls it too, so a model with such a table is refused before any C file is written, whichever evaluator is named (`ff670a7`). Without it, `"evaluator":"none"` skipped the check and `aotBuild` ran `mex` twice before the load failed.
  - Checks on `ff670a7` (commits `2232d59`, `f85b857`, `f8ef078`, `aa5f80a`, `ff670a7`): `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES="" cargo test --no-fail-fast` 2157 passed, 0 failed, 11 ignored, `cargo deny check` ok. DLL restaged with `scripts/build-pckern win64`, then `scripts/matlab-win "buildtool test"`: 646 tests, 629 passed, 0 failed, 17 incomplete, all "Filtered by assumption" (LaTeX build tests, three cycle oracle tests, Linux-only path tests). `AotTest` ran 13 of 13 and `ModelTest` 27 of 27 with none skipped. An earlier run of the same suite on the DLL before `ff670a7` had 640 passed and 6 incomplete, so the 11 extra filtered cases are the LaTeX build tests (diagnostic "latexmk is not installed" in the Windows MATLAB session, which had it on an earlier run). The LaTeX build tests did not run in this session. A targeted `runtests` call outside `buildtool` skipped the 10 compiler tests, because `mex.getCompilerConfigurations("C","Installed")` returns empty in a bare `-batch` session here and `mex -setup C` there reports no supported compiler, while the selected one is still stored. A direct `Model(file, Evaluator="aot")` call in that session loaded a cached library (folder dated hours earlier, so a cache hit, not a fresh `mex` run) with no warning, the tag check passed and its RHS matched the JIT exactly. Use `buildtool test` for the AOT cases.
  - Full MATLAB suite, 2026-11-10, on the DLL staged for `ff670a7` (the commits since then change `pc-cli licenses`, its tests, one engine test and docs only): `scripts/matlab-win "setenv('PATH',[getenv('PATH') ';%USERPROFILE%\bin']); buildtool test"` gave 646 total, 640 passed, 0 failed, 6 incomplete (filtered by assumption), and the LaTeX build tests ran. The PATH line is needed because `scripts/matlab-win` does not pass the user bin folder, where `latexmk.cmd` (a wrapper that calls WSL) lives. The cause is not fixed in the repo.
  - Memory of the CLI, 2026-11-10 (peak resident set of `pc-cli`, one run each, no profiler installed): `--version` 3.7 MB, `parse` 4.5 MB, an equilibrium continuation of van der Pol 12.0 MB with the JIT and 9.5 MB with the fallback evaluator, `gpu list` 61 MB (57 MB with CUDA off), `ensemble --bench` 208 MB at 1000 members and 256 MB at 100000. A CPU ensemble job through JSON takes 63 MB at 1000 members, 138 MB at 100000 and 853 MB at 1000000 (job file 21 MB, output 120 MB, about 760 bytes per member through `serde_json`). So the engine core is small; the costs that are large are the GPU driver contexts (50 to 200 MB of host memory whichever engine uses them) and the JSON round trip of a very large ensemble. Memory inside MATLAB, 2026-11-10 (R2026b on Windows, `MemUsedMATLAB` from `memory`, DLL staged for `ff670a7`, Morris-Lecar model, one machine, a few runs, so read the numbers to the nearest 10 MB): MATLAB itself holds about 2.1 GB before the toolbox is called. Loading a model adds 33 MB, the first `simulate` 6 MB, an equilibrium continuation 37 MB, a limit-cycle continuation from its Hopf point 6 MB more. A CPU ensemble holds 0.8 MB at 1000 members, 5.5 MB at 10000 and 12 to 14 MB at 100000 while its result exists, and after `clear` the working set stays about 6, 12 and 15 MB above its level before the sweep, read after the 1000, 10000 and 100000 runs in turn (cumulative over the sweep, and the figure levels off), so the per-member cost is small. The first ensemble call in a session adds 230 to 315 MB once; the second adds 2 MB. Cause, confirmed on 2026-11-10 in two fresh sessions that called `phasecont.gpuDevices()` first: that call adds 327 to 328 MB, and the first CPU ensemble after it adds 2 to 4 MB. The path in the code: the job start in `crates/pc-ffi/src/ensemble.rs` (line 177) calls `find_device` for every job, `Backend::Cpu` included, and `find_device` calls `list_devices`, which probes CUDA, OpenCL and Metal, so a CPU-only job loads the GPU driver stack. The same call sits in `crates/pc-cli/src/ensemble.rs`. The CLI figures above are peak resident set, a different measure from `MemUsedMATLAB`: `gpu list`, which runs the same probe, peaks at 61 MB in the CLI, while the first `gpuDevices` call adds 328 MB inside MATLAB. Fixed in a22744d after the owner said yes on 2026-11-10: `find_device(Backend::Cpu, i)` returns the CPU device without calling `list_devices` and keeps the error `no cpu device <i>: the backend lists 1` for an index other than 0. No C signature, MATLAB signature, default or job format changed. Unit tests in `device.rs` count the probes (a CPU lookup makes none, a CUDA lookup makes one). Before and after, two fresh MATLAB sessions each, first call with `Backend = "cpu"`: +312 and +316 MB on the old DLL, +20 and +8 MB on the new one; the second call adds 0.3 to 1.6 MB. The CLI CPU job now peaks at 6.4 to 6.9 MB at 1000 members (was 63) and 97 MB at 100000 (was 138), two runs each; `gpu list` still peaks at 61 MB. The default `Backend = "auto"` still probes, and on this machine it picks CUDA on the RTX 5060: its first call adds 582 and 595 MB in two fresh sessions (CUDA context, NVRTC and the OpenCL probe), the second 0 MB. That is the cost of the GPU path itself, and the default stays `"auto"` unless the owner changes it. Full MATLAB suite on the DLL built from this tree: 646 total, 629 passed, 0 failed, 17 incomplete, filtered by assumption. The 11 more than the run above are the LaTeX build tests, skipped because that run did not add `%USERPROFILE%\bin` to PATH; `LatexTest` and `LatexStudioTest` then ran with the PATH line and passed 60 of 60.
  - VS Code Marketplace, 2026-11-10: not published and not publishable from here, since it needs the owner's publisher account and a token. The extension is `editors/vscode` (`name` `phasecont-ode`, `version` 0.1.0, `publisher` `nub04`, `engines.vscode` `^1.146.0`). Per platform, after a green CI on the pushed commit: build `pc-lsp` for the target, then `npm run package -- --target <linux-x64|win32-x64|darwin-arm64|darwin-x64>` in `editors/vscode`, then `vsce publish --packagePath <file>.vsix` with the owner's token. The README says there is no listing yet, and that stays true until then.
